[moved 2026-10-06: closed, Jev close-and-file-residuals 0.88 - fixed and pixel-proven 2026-09-06; every fix commit (311b016 2117027 eda7dd2 7c1439c 22e790f 58de415 0848030) is in 4.3.35's agent; residuals (a) and (b) live on as one P3 entry in issues.md]
- P2 TOAST OVERCROP — ROOT CAUSE = the slide-in ANIMATION RACE (owner-reported "back up your PC" wrong geometry; PINNED 2026-09-06 from the agent log at `Q:\Qubes Logs`). Toasts slide in from the right; when the agent's UIA card measurement lands MID-SLIDE it sees only the card's right strip → `TcApplyResult` computes `insets l=<big> t=30 r=1 b=13` (huge left, ~1px right) → cropped short + shifted right. PROVEN: the SAME complex 3-image toast measured `l=209 r=1` (→186×173) / `l=53 r=1` / `l=105 r=1` with animation ON, and `l=16 t=30 r=16 b=13` (correct card) THREE times in a row with `SPI_SETCLIENTAREAANIMATION=0`. Simple/hero toasts settle fast so they rarely race (measured correct); complex/taller toasts race often. Fable's race guard (toastcrop.c:623) only re-checks the WINDOW rect (stable during the slide), so it misses the card sliding INSIDE a static window (its own path-(f)). COMPLETE FIX SET (all committed together, agent 7c1439c, owner "I need ALL the fixes ... at once" 2026-09-06): (1) PROPER CROP — toasts use raw-largest (311b016) + asymmetry guard `|l-r|>32` rejects a mid-slide read (2117027); (2) ANIMATIONS OFF — SetShadowsMain disables SPI_SETCLIENTAREAANIMATION on the user token (2117027, log-confirmed applied); (3) FAST FOR SIMPLE TOASTS — the 50ms settle re-walk is CONDITIONAL on the asymmetry check (eda7dd2), so a clean symmetric read accepts immediately with zero added latency; (4) CROP-BEFORE-SHOW — geometry hold (MapDeferred/CROP_BEFORE_SHOW_TIMEOUT_MS=400) was always on; the CONTENT hold (SliceMapHold, main.c:199) was DEFAULT-OFF and is the piece I deferred and never turned on — a slice-fed window maps with a zeroed slab → "black borders flash before it settles" (owner-reported) → flipped DEFAULT ON (7c1439c, registry SliceMapHold=0 restores old behavior, chrome scope 1 = slice path; de-slice/broker path has its own first-frame fix). Nothing else was reverted/discarded — audited 2026-09-06. **CROP FIXES (1,2,3 + the geometry half of 4) are GOOD** (overcrop fixed, pixel-confirmed content toast on the eda7dd2 build). **BUT the SliceMapHold CONTENT-hold (7c1439c) is a BLACK REGRESSION, not shippable**: rig-confirmed BY PIXELS on win11 24H2 — a slice-fed toast (PwNoteSliceFedMap; de-slice guest but the toast is slice-fed) maps PERSISTENTLY BLACK with SliceMapHold=1, because SliceContentReady (main.c:2697) releases on `PwSliceContentTick!=0` and PwNoteSliceContent (main.c:5547) sets that on the FIRST composite copy — which is the zeroed/black slab; the real content lands seconds later. So the readiness predicate means "a copy happened", not "painted". SliceMapHold=0 avoids black but maps-then-paints (the appear-delay = map-before-render). FIX IN PROGRESS: make the readiness predicate mean ACTUALLY painted (non-black), bounded by CROP_BEFORE_SHOW_TIMEOUT_MS fail-open. Validate BY PIXELS (an earlier log-based PASS was false — the log said content-ready while the toast was black; judge-output-not-logs). FIXED + PIXEL-PROVEN (agent 58de415, 2026-09-06): the readiness predicate now means PAINTED (slicepaint.h SlicePainted: 8x8 grid, painted iff >=2% samples have B/G/R>16), set on the window's own buffer, re-sampled every copy until non-black; SliceContentReady = PwSliceContentTick only; SliceChromeContentReady level 1 now content-holds slice-fed TOASTS on the de-slice path too (de-slice MENUS unchanged). Offline suite slicepaint_test.c 15/15, DEFECT_ANYCOPY 5-fail. RIG PIXEL result (win11 24H2, judged by cropping the toast rect + PIL mean, NOT the log): the complex bitmap toast renders NON-BLACK (mean 163, 100% non-black, hero+text+buttons visible - image sent to owner), map after paint (QGASLICEMAP painted=1). SliceMapHold stays default ON. RESIDUALS (polish, NOT black): (a) the map releases at the ~400ms CROP_BEFORE_SHOW_TIMEOUT rather than instantly on the paint signal (held_ms=422; a bounded delay, far better than SliceMapHold=0's "few seconds", but wire release-on-paint for snappiness); (b) BROKERDIMS feed-loss fires once during the toast's size transition (broker keeps a window-sized WGC channel across the card-size PwResizeWindow re-registration; frame 396x415 != slab 396x579 rejected) - transient, the toast recovers non-black within the hold, but fix the broker to size for the full window / re-open the channel on re-registration; (c) rapid-fire History.Clear can clear a toast before it paints (painted=0) - test artifact, not user-facing. NOTE: the earlier "control-view union overcrops" theory is WRONG — the union INCLUDES the FlexibleToastView card, so it does not overcrop; with animation off BOTH the union and raw-largest rules measure the card correctly. The shipped `raw-largest` change (agent 311b016) is therefore a correctness tidy-up, not the fix; kept as belt-and-suspenders (tested to pick the card) but the animation-disable is the real fix. Old (superseded) framing follows for the record: [diagnosed 2026-09-06, code-confirmed, live-corroborated). The 2026-09-03 card-finder rewrite (agent 9399acd control-view-first + 78a602e union, BOTH ancestors of HEAD 96330e5 = shipped) makes toastcrop.c:583-599 prefer the UIA ControlViewWalker (raw view only as fallback), so a toast is cropped to the UNION of its content controls (text/buttons/logo) INSIDE the card's padding+rounded border instead of the drawn card — a SILENT OVERCROP, the exact failure the module's own header (toastcrop.c:35-38) says it must never have. Passes every guard (a ~85%x75% content box clears the 40% floor) and logs as success ("toast card in WxH window, insets ..."). BackupReminder is the toast the module was TUNED on (toastcrop.h: 396x332 window carried a 377x287 card, insets ~2/31/17/14, measured 2026-08-11 under the OLD raw-view finder); no toast re-measured after 09-03 (windowing.md:25 asserts union==card by reasoning, only measured a menu). Because entry->X/Y/W/H also drives the per-window CAPTURE crop (capture.md:36), the pixels are cut to match. LIVE corroboration (win11-a0tb 24H2, 2026-09-06): a realchoice reminder toast rendered at 214x157 vs the 377x287 baseline — consistent with overcrop (raw size not captured; the insets line needs the agent log path, elusive this session). FIX (ranked, NOT implemented): (1) per-surface view — menus keep control-view-first (the 09-03 fix that killed menu edge strips), TOASTS use raw view (the pre-09-03 rule that produced the 08-11 numbers); (2) never crop a toast tighter than its largest single qualifying element; (3) ship a log line dumping both views' unions so the field can classify without a rig. Picking (1) vs (2) needs one live measurement: dump BOTH RawViewWalker and ControlViewWalker subtrees for a real toast (is the card element in the control view on 24H2?). REPRO: bridge off / service.legacy-toasts, fire a reminder-scenario toast as the interactive user, fullshot geometry.txt + winenum + the insets log; PASS iff dom0 rect == raw-view card ±2px with the rounded border intact. NOTE: the misc.md:17 "no w/h sanity guard" theory is STALE — SanitizeWireGeometry (send.c:105) refuses non-positive w/h; a negative rect gives an invisible toast, not a wrong-geometry one. [also: user-facing] [verified 2026-09-06]

[moved 2026-10-02: closed by the owner - "p1-4 is what we STARTED fixing with current round, and you confirmed that idle load is gone"; evidence: rz27 acceptance rest-steady, 10 x 60 s at rest on the burn scene - dwm 0 to 0.03% of a core, our process family 0, agent 0, broker 0; rz29's acceptance re-measures it (M2)]
- P1 [capture, product-relevant] **DWM BURNS 118% OF A CORE ON AN ORDINARY EIGHT-WINDOW DESKTOP WHILE OUR AGENT IS CAPTURING.** Measured 2026-09-26 on win11de-ctl (German Win11 25H2, QWT 4.3.33+agent.4ba0e950ad00, 4 vCPUs, desktop 5120x1440), per-process CPU as a CPU-seconds delta over 6 s (locale-independent; the localised performance-counter route returned empty and empty was NOT read as idle). WITH eight app windows mapped - 2x Notepad 3816x1004, ApplicationFrameWindow 1216x941, Explorer 1139x641, 2x WindowsTerminal 1129x635, Paint, a 343x166 dialog: `dwm 118%, Notepad 41%, System 34%, gui-agent 34%, explorer 14%` of one core each, ~250% total on a 4-vCPU guest. AFTER closing them, same guest, same method, minutes later: `dwm 9.6%, gui-agent 5.5%, System 5.7%`, and two further samples showed the guest idle. The owner independently reported the guest's File Explorer window as "slow as fuck" during the loaded period and confirmed it fine afterwards. **A stock Windows desktop with eight static windows does not put DWM above a core**; DWM idles near zero when nothing animates. That implicates continuous capture-driven composition, i.e. ours. **THE CONTROL HAS NOW BEEN RUN (2026-09-30, w11-ds, Win11 24H2, 5120x1440, candidate agent 9B8C574E = de-slice candidate + notifhost fix; per-process attribution, rig silencer on):** a static desktop of EIGHT app windows during the samples - 2 Notepad 3816x1004, 3 Explorer (two of them File Explorer windows the shell RESTORED at logon), Settings, and 2 "Location is not available" error dialogs (**CORRECTED 2026-09-30: this line first said nine windows including "a Terminal". That Terminal was the harness's own run-in-session console window, present only while a listing ran and absent from every idle sample; the Paint and two Terminal windows `open-scene.ps1` was meant to start NEVER OPENED, silently - so this is NOT the 2026-09-26 window set, and the opener must assert what it opened**) - OURS attrib (dwm + our processes + the scene apps) **145.6% of one core [133-156, n=3]** (dwm 73, gui-agent 34, the apps 40, broker 0.2) vs OFF 0.00 [n=3] and STOCK 0.07 [n=2], every range disjoint; whole guest 205 vs 27-30. Jev (`scratchpad/jev-exp2q-*`): cost over one core **0.90**, caused by our capture **0.62**, look first at DWM composition driven by capture **0.62**. **BUT IT IS A STATE, NOT A CONSTANT:** the same binary on the same open scene read 15-23% in two later samples (26 and 35 min after the scene opened, a fresh agent before one of them), with the capture ALIVE (text typed into a Notepad reached dom0 in ~4 s) and the Notepads served without broker slots in both states; the one broker-polled window was Settings (ApplicationFrameWindow on the PrintWindow route, ~1.4 renders/s with nothing changing). What switches the burn on is being measured (`scratchpad/dwmburn/burn-state.sh`). PREVIOUSLY: **NOT YET ESTABLISHED, and the decisive control had NOT been run:** whether the DWM load is caused by our per-window capture (PrintWindow and/or WGC sessions forcing DWM to composite continuously) or merely correlates with having windows open. The control is the same window set with the agent (and gui-watchdog, which respawns it) stopped. **RETRACTED on the way here:** I first called this window set an abnormal "pile" and an artifact of my own corrupted launch commands. The commands were mine; the configuration is not abnormal - eight windows including an editor, two terminals and a file manager is an ordinary desktop, and the owner pointed that out. Scaling with window count is established; "eight windows is unreasonable" is not, and was self-serving. [verified 2026-09-26]

[moved 2026-10-02: closed, Jev 0.91]
- P3 [user-facing, geometry + capture; OURS - the agent's dom0-drive path (ADR-gui 2); FIXED agent 001b525 (build rz23), owner-verified 2026-10-02 01:03 "all perfect"] [verified 2026-10-02] **A DOM0 EDGE-RESIZE REPLAYED FOR SECONDS AFTER RELEASE, AND EVERY DOM0 RESIZE LEFT A BORDERED WINDOW 12-14 PX NARROWER AND 6-7 PX SHORTER THAN DRAGGED - FIXED.** The owner's Settings report ("the window contents stay the same size, but gets misplaced in window borders"; "very slowly, but the last resize did not settle"), traced 2026-10-02 00:00 (settings-drag-1, rz21): 650 dom0 configures -> 519 size-only async SetWindowPos, none gated (the 2026-08-12 latest-wins gate compared only the origin); the window lagged p50 1.9 s / max 9.9 s and replayed for 3.6 s after release; 392 of its lagging sizes were announced back during the drag and 45 after it, 436 of them dom0's size minus 14x7 (SetWindowPos got announce-space sizes). The same 12x6 loss on an ordinary Win32 window under dom0's local.WinResize: 6 of 6 resizes on rz21, 0 of 6 on rz23 (resize-ab-1). Fix: a size post is in flight until the window takes it; every geometry announce is held while dom0 drives or its geometry lands; the size is converted. On rz23, the owner's own drag: 310 configures -> 54 posts, 2 own announces during the drag, 3 in 0.41 s after it ending on dom0's final size, none short. RETRACTED on the way (said to the owner and to Jev): "Settings' own content stayed at 502x1141" - WGC's last arrival was 897x728; the stale picture was OUR capture publishing nothing after the replayed resize (frame 502x1141 vs request 897x728 for 3.5 min, slot kept, no re-open). That stall did not recur on rz23 (published frame = request at the end) nor under a guest-side resize stream on rz21-capture (settings-stall-1, frame followed within 1 s); its exact broker mechanism is NOT established. Residual: when the window takes more than 200 ms per resize, extra posts can queue and an older one landing reads as taken - 3 announces stepping the border by up to 10 px for 54 ms after release; remedy, if seen: a longer in-flight bound for size posts.

[moved 2026-10-02: closed, Jev 0.84]
- P1 [capture, product-relevant, BLOCKS the de-slice goal] **AFTER A COLD BOOT THE RELAY DOES NOT DELIVER A SOURCE CHANGE; WARM IT DELIVERS RELIABLY.** Jev: `established` **0.92**, `blocks_acceptance` **0.83**, `severity` = `p1-cold-boot-relay-not-live` at confidence **0.99**. Measured 2026-09-26/27 on win11de-ledb (German Win11 25H2 26200.8037, release build 36267989204, deploy 9 passed / 0 failed, installed gui-agent hash == package, installed wgcbroker hash re-verified against the verified disc before every census). **WARM, THE RELAY IS GOOD:** three graded census runs (three internal censuses each) all returned ROUTE PASS, DELIVERED PIXEL PASS, SPATIAL PASS and STALENESS PASS, with per-class spatial MAD over 576 central tiles of Terminal/NRB **0.0**, NOREDIRECTIONBITMAP **0.0**, per-pixel-alpha (`ovr=1`) **1.5**, LWA_COLORKEY **0.0** and the live toast **2.2**; and a SIX-CELL A/B reading `GenFrames` directly (ABI 15, zeroed per session), alternating the demotion rule OFF/ON three times each with every advance confirmed consumed, returned `genFrames 1 -> 2 delivered=YES` in all six, route unchanged. **COLD BOOT, SAME GUEST AND BUILD, agent restart deliberately skipped so the graded agent is the instance the boot produced, exactly ONE fixture instance asserted:** ROUTE still PASS and the four rogue classes still match spatially (0.0/1.5/0.0/0.0), but STALENESS **FAIL** - `delivered-vs-before 0.0`, `delivered-vs-source 21.8`, advance confirmed consumed after ~3 s - and the DIRECT measurement that cleared the warm case read **`genFrames 1 -> 1`**: the relay did not deliver. The toast also regressed from MAD 2.2 warm to **17.7** cold, signature 28 against 15. **WHY THIS IS NOT ANOTHER INSTRUMENT ARTEFACT** (six of mine were found and fixed in the same session): `GenFrames` is read straight from the shared section, is zeroed per session, and involves neither the signature throttle (fixed), the tile grid, the pixel survey, slot-to-class correlation, nor a fixed sleep - and the identical measurement on the identical build returned delivered=YES six consecutive times warm. **THIS IS EXACTLY THE SHAPE THE PROJECT'S OWN RULE WARNS OF:** *"a restart CLEARS the fault the user then hit on a cold boot; a reboot is part of acceptance"* - warm passes, cold boot fails, and the cold boot is the state a user meets. **NOT ESTABLISHED:** the mechanism; whether it is permanent after a cold boot or a warm-up that resolves; whether it affects all relay slots (routes passed and three classes matched, so those slots had delivered something earlier in the run); and whether the toast's regression shares the cause. **RE-INSTATED AND LOCALISED (Jev `reinstate` **0.94**, `evidence_quality` = driven-slot-identified **1.00**, `blocks_acceptance` 0.70). I WITHDREW THIS P1 PREMATURELY AND THAT WITHDRAWAL IS ITSELF RETRACTED.** The withdrawal rested on an ALL-SLOT count (4 of 5 relay slots advancing on a cold boot) in which I never checked WHICH slot was the driven window - and in that same output `slot0 route=1 1 -> 1` had not advanced. Since the sentinel advance re-supplies exactly ONE fixture window, only its slot can move for that reason, and the other four advancing is explained by the fixtures having just been created. Two measurements that DO identify the driven window both show the defect: the census staleness cell, rewritten to grade only the window whose GUEST render actually moved (`src moved 20.5, delivered moved 0.0, vs new source 21.8`), and the same selector used for the warm test - the slot whose published signature is the 1080-colour gradient - reading `genFrames 1 -> 1` across a confirmed advance. Warm, that identical selector gave `1 -> 2` in SIX consecutive cells. **THE DISCRIMINATOR, run with the driven slot identified (Jev 0.88), SETTLES WHERE THE FAULT IS:** on one cold boot, `COLD BOOT, boot-produced agent: driven slot genFrames 1 -> 1` (nothing), then `SAME BOOT, agent-only restart: driven slot genFrames 1 -> 2` (DELIVERED). **So the fault is in the agent/broker instance THE BOOT PRODUCES, not in the boot or the guest's state - restarting the agent on the same boot repairs it.** That is a narrow, actionable target: the broker/agent startup path on a first run after a guest boot. **REPRODUCED DETERMINISTICALLY, AND THE CONDITION IS PART OF THE DEFECT (2026-09-27, later):** on the defective build (rel-36267989204, installed broker hash fa7ae721 checked against that release's MANIFEST) the full-window-set census on a cold boot FAILS 2 of 2 with numbers identical to the original report - `QubesChromeReproUlwContent` src moved 20.5, delivered moved 0.0, vs new source 21.8 - and `Windows.UI.Core.CoreWindow` spatial MAD 13.7 (original toast 17.7), so the second class regresses too. **With a MINIMAL fixture set (4 relay channels, no Terminal, no Start menu, no toast) the same build delivers on 6 of 6 cold boots** (driven slot selected by HWND equality against the agent's AddWindow map, stimulus confirmed by the guest's own render hash, genFrames 1 -> 2). The defect therefore needs the loaded window set, not merely a cold boot, and any harness that drives one fixture will call it fixed. **MECHANISM, now evidenced rather than inferred:** in the reproducing capture the driven slot's `FRAMES arrived` and `ARRIVALS raw` are stuck at 2 BEFORE and AFTER the advance, across two samples 5 s apart, while `RELAYHOLD srcChanged` goes 0 -> 1 - so the broker's own detector measured the source change and NO WGC frame ever arrived; `tools/frame-age.py` on the same capture puts that slot's last publish 81.5 s before the reading (its opening frame) and 87 s at the next sample. It is a dead DWM thumbnail registration, not a publish guard, not the signature throttle, and not a late frame. Channels opened seconds earlier in the same capture have frames 0.8-1.3 s old, so new registrations work while existing ones are dead. **DEAF, NOT LATE:** changing ONLY the post-advance wait from 6 s to 45 s still FAILS, 2 of 2 cold boots - so the frame is not merely arriving after the census looked. **MY MECHANISM READING IS NOT SUPPORTED, per Jev asked with all of the above (wire log 2026-09-27):** `mechanism` = **insufficient-evidence 0.43** at confidence 0.29, with `dwm-throttles-the-alpha0-destination` 0.24, `session-churn-around-the-measurement` 0.16, `wgc-frame-pool-stopped` 0.10 and my own `dead-thumbnail-registration` only **0.07**; `reregistration_is_the_right_repair` **0.32**; and the named discriminator is `watch-the-destination-window-composition` **0.53** ahead of `recreate-the-pool-instead` 0.40, with "running the fix answers it" at **0.00**. **THE DISCRIMINATOR RAN, AND IT NAMES THE DEAD PART (2026-09-27, same build, same boot as a reproduced failure):** with deafness first ASSERTED on the driven slot (`FRAMES arrived` 8 -> 8 and `ARRIVALS raw` 8 -> 8 across a source change the guest's own render hash confirms, `srcChanged=1`), a FRESH WGC session opened from a separate process on the broker's OWN destination window (`0x20284`, class `QubesWgcRelayDest`, alpha 0, 360x240, visible) returned **frames=1 every second for 8 s with colours=1080 and nonBlack=1080/1080** - 1080 being the exact signature of the source's gradient - and a control session on the SOURCE window likewise. **So DWM is compositing the thumbnail, the destination is capturable, its content IS the source's content, and only the BROKER'S OWN long-lived session receives nothing.** Jev on that evidence: `mechanism` = **brokers-own-capture-session-died 0.82** (confidence 0.77), with `dead-thumbnail-registration` and `dwm-throttles-the-alpha0-destination` both at **0.00**. Corroborating: in an earlier failing capture every long-lived relay's last publish was its opening frame while channels opened seconds earlier were 0.8-1.3 s fresh - new sessions work, existing ones are dead. **TRIGGER, MEASURED AS A RATE - and NOT established as the trigger:** an interleaved A/B on ONE unchanged binary, a cold boot per cell, loaded window set every cell, deafness asserted from the broker's counters and the stimulus from the guest's own render hash: **WITH** an all-window `PrintWindow` sweep before the advance, **DEAF 3/3** (arrived 2->2, 9->9, 2->2); **WITHOUT** it, **DELIVERED 3/3** (9->10, 10->11, 7->8), no cell skipped or refused. But the SAME sweep passes **WARM** (three census runs earlier tonight), so the sweep alone is not sufficient and the boot-produced broker instance is the other half - Jev: `trigger_established` **0.21**, `warm_pass_undermined` 0.68, `product_relevant` **0.71** ("a capture session that dies silently and is never detected is a defect whatever provokes it"). The missing half of the conjunction is still unknown, and the sweep is part of MY apparatus - the agent does not sweep, though the broker PrintWindow-renders individual sources on its own detector and channels survive that. **NEXT STEP, per Jev at its highest confidence of the session:** `next_step` = **subscribe-to-item-closed-and-observe 0.96** (confidence 0.95), with `recreate-the-session-now` and `observe-and-repair-in-one-build` BOTH at **0.00**. A code read explains why the state was invisible: the broker **never subscribed to `GraphicsCaptureItem.Closed`**, so a closed item is indistinguishable from a window whose content stopped changing - no error, no counter, the slot still ACTIVE, and a fresh session on the same window working. An OBSERVATION-ONLY build (`a3e3228`, ABI 16 `ItemClosed` / `ItemClosedTick`, no behaviour change) now records it; the thumbnail repair was **reverted unrun** in the same commit. If the counter moves on a deaf channel the repair is a session recreation; if it stays 0 the item was NOT closed and the mechanism is still open. **FIRST OBSERVATION RUN (2026-09-27, build a3e3228, broker b1576c87, installed and hash-verified): the reproducer that was DEAF 3/3 on the previous binary DELIVERED on both gradeable cells** (`arrived 6->8` and `6->9`, `itemClosed=0->0`; a third cell REFUSED because the fixture did not launch - 0 instances - and was not graded). **THIS IS CONFOUNDED AND IS NOT YET A RESULT ABOUT THE BUILD:** installing recreates the subject from the golden, so the build AND the guest's freshness changed together, while every DEAF measurement was taken on a guest that had been through many boots and installs. A control - the PREVIOUS build installed the same way on an equally fresh guest, same reproducer, same cell count - is running; until it reports, "the symptom disappeared" is unattributed. **A second honest caveat:** subscribing to `GraphicsCaptureItem.Closed` is not perfectly inert. It installs a handler and takes a strong reference to the item, so the observation can itself perturb the lifetime it observes; if the control shows the previous build failing on a fresh guest, then the subscription suppressing the defect is a live reading and must be treated as evidence about the mechanism (an item whose lifetime was ending), not as a fix. **THEREFORE the thumbnail-re-registration repair (built, release 36274042739, ABI 16 RelayReregs) IS NOT INSTALLED and IS NOT CLAIMED AS A FIX** - and on this evidence it cannot be the fix, since it deliberately keeps the same session and pool that the measurement shows to be the dead part. It stays in the tree unvalidated while the discriminator runs: `tools/thumbprobe --capture <hwnd>` (added for this) WGC-captures the destination window the broker publishes as `relayDest`, so that frames-with-content there means DWM is compositing and the broker's own capture SESSION is the dead part - in which case renewing the thumbnail would fix nothing and the session must be recreated instead. Also still open: whether the CoreWindow spatial regression (13.7 cold) shares the cause. **THE LESSON, which cost several wrong conclusions tonight:** a measurement that does not identify WHICH slot serves the window under test can support either answer. Identify the slot from the guest or by a property of the window (its published signature), never by position in a list or by a class name from an accumulated log. An attempt at that tonight produced an INVALID reading (the slot selector returned empty and the greps mismatched, giving 63 -> 1) and is recorded as no result rather than as evidence; a later attempt refused outright because the live fixture could not be located in any slot. **FOUR MORE INSTRUMENT DEFECTS were found and fixed on 2026-09-27, and each had produced a verdict:** a slot selector reading 6 whitespace fields from a 9-field snapshot (a present slot read as "no capture channel", faking a control FAILURE); `log()` writing to stdout while the grading function returned its verdict there (three delivering cold boots printed "0 pass, 0 fail, 0 skip"); a `class=X$` anchor applied to raw log lines, which continue past the class (the harness refused to find a window its own log had mapped, and started a second fixture racing for the sentinel file); and - the worst - `guest/wgcbroker-peek.ps1`'s ABI assertion, which could never fire because `$ABI` and `$abi` are ONE variable in PowerShell, so an ABI-16 reader parsed an ABI-15 section in silence (slot0 correct, later slots drifting 8 bytes each: hwnd=0x1, hwnd=0xa, req=17410688x0) and TWO cold-boot censuses were graded on the misparse, reporting "1 slot" and STALENESS NO DATA. Those two runs are void. All four now have selftests that were driven to FAIL on the defect they guard. Runner-up measurements: check whether direct-WGC slots deliver on the same cold boot (0.21), and whether it resolves with time or activity (0.16). **ALSO NOTE, a rig failure of my own making:** ten test subjects accumulated over the session exhausted dom0's thin pool - `Cannot create new thin volume, free space in thin pool qubes_dom0/vm-pool reached threshold. Request refused` - which blocks any guest from starting and reads as a mysterious guest failure if the pool is not checked. Nine were removed. [verified 2026-09-27] **2026-09-27 (evening): THE CONTROL RAN, THE FIRST ATTRIBUTION IS RETRACTED, A FIX IS UNDER TEST.** Probe cells (cold boot, loaded window set, all-window PrintWindow sweep, driven change confirmed, verdict from the broker's own counters; the probe, reader and fixture binaries rebuilt exactly from the previous session's transcript): the OLD build on a FRESH clone **DEAF 2/2** (freshness excluded, Jev 0.63); current main, whose broker registers GraphicsCaptureItem.Closed, **DEAF 2/3** - so **the Closed handler is NOT the fix** (Jev had attributed 0.81 on the observation build's two deliveries, which were chance). Pooled without a fix: **DEAF 7 of 10** cold boots. Fix under test: `6e7ed6d` - the broker RE-OPENS a deaf relay (fresh destination, thumbnail, pool and session) once before ever demoting it (Jev: issue established 0.94, design sound 0.73; named risk 0.86 that a fresh session in the same process is deaf too). Verification bar per Jev: 3 cold-boot cells all DELIVERED with at least one showing the re-open fired (SESSION gen advancing), then 3 cold-boot census runs with all four verdicts PASS. [verified 2026-09-27] **2026-09-27 (late): THE RE-OPEN FIX HOLDS; THE TOAST IS A DIFFERENT DEFECT, AND MY FIRST TOAST FIX IS RETRACTED AND REVERTED.** On the re-open build every cold-boot census (5 runs across two builds) passed ROUTE and - on every boot where the ULW windows had a slot - STALENESS (the cold-boot deafness case now tracked); Terminal, NRB, ULW-content and COLORKEY MATCH. The only remaining failure is the shell toast (spatial MAD 17.7 / 17.7 / 17.7 / 17.7 / 13.7). **RETRACTED: "the toast is stale from its fade-in"** - my inference, never measured; a staleness check built on it (f35d80b) fixed nothing (toast unchanged on its own build; on 2 of 3 boots the test hosting it never even ran on the toast's slot) and was never seen to fire; reverted (f28d892, Jev revert-now 0.98). Jev on the toast: its RELAY renders it unfaithfully 0.83 vs stale 0.09 - warm it is served on plain WGC and matches (2.2); cold, its first plain-WGC session goes deaf and the ladder drops it onto the relay (15 colours vs 28). Fix under test: 36d6a59 - a quiet plain-WGC channel gets one fresh plain-WGC session before stepping down (Jev 0.85). **2026-09-30: THE COLD-BOOT CENSUS PASSES 3/3 ON THE v4 PACKAGE (release-package 36639767284, which carries every broker fix on main - 6e7ed6d, 36d6a59, 01bce20, 3be5415, 45ed344), pre-registered before the run:** `w11de-v4c` (German 25H2 golden, upgraded from the release disc, installed broker f6674f61 == the package manifest), coldboot-census-loop.sh RUNS=3, each a cold boot graded on the boot-produced broker with the full loaded window set - every run ROUTE, GHOST, SERVED, DELIVERED PIXEL, SPATIAL and STALENESS PASS; per-class MAD Terminal 0.0, NRB 0.0, ULW-content 1.5, COLORKEY 0.0 and the TOAST 1.4 on all three (it failed at 13.7-17.7 on every cold boot before 36d6a59); the driven source tracked every run (delivered moved 20.6 with the source's 20.5, 1.4 from the new render); broker deaths 0/0/0. PIXEL (own-surface proxy) is REVIEW as in every earlier run and is not part of the bar. THE ONE CRITERION STILL OPEN is the one 45ed344's acceptance also missed - 'feed LOST' must read 0: runs 1-2 read 0, run 3 read 1 - the NRB fixture on the relay (hwnd 0x301bc) had its frames rejected for 16,422 ms (frame 346x233 vs slab 348x234) until the agent's stuck detector forced a BROKERREREG, after which it served 348x234 and graded MATCH. CORRECTED THE SAME NIGHT - my first reading here ("the agent's slab moved without the slot's request following") was WRONG and is retracted, as is the agent change built on it (7fd533b, reverted c265f66, never shipped). The agent log of that boot for 0x301bc: registered at 346x233 (first frame served, window mapped) -> RestyleGuestCaption grew the window to 348x234 -> the agent RETARGETED the slot to 348x234 at once (BrokerRetarget QGABROKERKEEP, 'slot kept, session not recreated') and attached a 348x234 slab -> the broker never served that card: the relay destination had been sized 346x233 at open (DwmQueryThumbnailSourceSize), its capture cannot cover a larger card, the retained-capture republish refuses a card larger than the capture, and a static window brings no arrival -> 16.4 s later the stuck detector's full re-registration (logged reqStale=0: the request WAS current) re-opened the relay at 348x234. So the defect is in the BROKER: a relay whose requested card outgrows its destination is never re-sized. **2026-09-30 (later): THE OPEN CRITERION IS FIXED IN THE BROKER - wgcbroker b813346, "re-open a relay whose source outgrew (or shrank from) its destination"** (release-package 36652188404 = the v4 package + this change only). The relay destination is sized once, at open; a relay window that changes size afterwards gets a card the broker cannot cut, and a static window brings no arrival. A deterministic RESIZE TEST (scratchpad/relay/relay-resize-test.sh: the NRB fixture asserted served on ROUTE 1 (RELAY), grown 2x1 px from inside the session, the broker slot polled; each poll ~5 s, so served times are upper bounds) FAILS on the current broker - 1 of 3 resizes served, and only through the agent's stuck-detector re-registration (which the broker re-opens only if its reconcile pass lands between the agent's unregister and register), 2 STUCK for 30 s (feed LOST x2, forced re-registrations x2) - and PASSES on the fix: 3/3 served by the broker's own re-open (session opens 3->4->5->6), 0 feed LOST, 0 re-registrations (Jev: defect_demonstrated 0.95, fix_shown 0.92; residual confounds timing-luck 0.31, subject-state 0.19). TOTALS control 2/8 (never by the broker itself), fix 8/8; Jev on both rounds: defect_demonstrated 0.96, fix_shown 0.97, confound none 0.85 (timing-luck and subject-state 0.07 each). THE COLD-BOOT CENSUS ON THE FIX (w11de-rsz, 3 cold boots, pre-registered bar): every verdict PASS on every run, feed LOST 0/0/0, re-registrations 0, broker deaths 0, per-class MAD identical (Terminal 0.0, NRB 0.0, ULW-content 1.5, COLORKEY 0.0, toast 1.4) - the criterion 45ed344's acceptance and the v4 census both missed now reads 0; with a natural trigger rate near 1 in 3 boots, 0/3 alone would be weak (~30% by chance) - the resize test is what carries the mechanism. - P3 [CLOSED 2026-09-10 - THE DEFECT NEVER EXISTED; a fix was shipped for it anyway] "SHUTTING A GUEST DOWN WITH WRITES IN FLIGHT PRODUCES AN UNCLEAN SHUTDOWN" IS REFUTED. ORIGINAL CLAIM: idle shutdowns clean 3/3 both routes, under ~2 GB of in-flight writes both UNCLEAN 3/3; a flush-and-settle was shipped in 4.3.25/4.3.26 (306a94b) on that basis. WHY IT IS VOID: the probe read Kernel-Power 41/6008 with StartTime=LastBootUpTime.AddMinutes(-20), no end bound and no timestamps recorded, while rounds ran 4-5 min apart and the instrument-validation HARD KILL - which writes exactly one 41 and one 6008 - ran 2 min before round 1. The probe printed the ids of EVERY match and all six loaded rounds printed exactly 'events:41,6008' (two events); a round producing its own marker would have shown four ids, then six. THE COUNT NEVER GREW, so zero of six loaded shutdowns logged a new marker. The 'idle clean' rounds ran BEFORE the injection. RE-MEASURED with each round scoped to a stamp from the guest's own clock (mgmt/harness/flush-durability-ab.sh): load 0/6 unclean across two load shapes - including a writer VERIFIED still growing at 1.7-2.7 GB per 10 s at the instant of shutdown - idle 0/6, and no boot-disk storage complaint in any round. ARCHITECTURAL REASON IT COULD NOT HAVE BEEN WHAT WAS CLAIMED: a write xenvbd has ACKED cannot be lost while dom0 lives - xenvbd holds the SRB PENDING until a blkif response, blkback responds only after the bio completes, that data is then in dom0's block stack which outlives the guest, and xen_blkif_disconnect refuses teardown while inflight>0. Load can only enter through 'how far did Windows get', never through flush semantics. WHAT STILL STANDS: a guest HARD-KILLED or destroyed mid-write really does come back unclean (that is what the injection shows), which is why every wedge specimen that was killed reached Startup Repair. THE SHIPPED FLUSH IS NOW REVERTED (30f2393, owner instruction: revert anything beyond tidying that had no effect). It was a precaution against a defect that does not exist, and it is a SUSPECT in the one that does - it forces a synchronous flush at the inter-stage transition, which runs on the EMULATED disk (see the guest-stability entry, Class A). SHIPPED-BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: 4.3.25/4.3.26 carry it, the next package will not. -DeferVgaDisable and the two dead-theory harnesses (idd-vga-ab.sh, vram-mode-churn.sh) went with it; shutdown-cleanliness.sh's 20-minute look-back - the instrument that manufactured this whole entry - is fixed to 30 s and now prints event timestamps. COLLATERAL DEFECTS FIXED: health-check.ps1's prev_shutdown_orderly windowed with EndTime=LastBootUpTime - a window ENDING at boot, while 41/6008 are written early in the CURRENT boot - so it could never fire and had been passing for something it could not detect; the NTFS dirty bit is a check that cannot fail (read NOT Dirty on a provably unclean boot), so the per-mount NTFS volume-health verdict (event 98) was added; and docs/RELEASE-NOTES-4.3.26.md was PUBLISHING the void table to users as fact. MEASURED CLEAN-VOLUME BASELINE, worth keeping: 131/131 event-98 verdicts across 50 boots said healthy, zero autochk runs. [also: installer, test-framework] [verified 2026-09-10]

[moved 2026-10-02: closed, Jev 0.92]
- P2 [FIXED AND PROVEN 2026-09-09] THE STAGE-1 REBOOT WAS A COIN FLIP; IT IS NOW A DEFINED PATH. A pristine guest has no PV drivers bound, so stage 1's reboot went out via the emulated ACPI/CF9 reset. In QEMU that raises `qemu_system_reset_request`, and TWO consumers race for it: the generic main loop (`qemu_system_reset` -> `qemu_devices_reset`, an IN-PLACE reset that never tells Xen) and the Xen accelerator's ioreq handler (`destroy_hvm_domain` -> SHUTDOWN_reboot -> toolstack -> on_reboot=destroy). Domain-destroys is the INTENDED Qubes behaviour; the in-place reset is the leak. FIX (guest-side, stands alone): stage 1 requests an ACPI S5 POWEROFF, which sets `shutdown_requested` - a separate flag with no `qemu_system_reset` path, whose only consumer is the Xen ioreq handler - so the domain goes down every time and the next start is always a fresh domain with the PV bus bound. It costs no capability: under Qubes a guest cannot restart itself either way (on_reboot=destroy), so `/r` never bought a self-restart, only a chance of an invisible in-place reset. Fast Startup is SET AND VERIFIED in the same block, because `shutdown /s` with hiberboot enabled is a hybrid shutdown whose next start RESUMES instead of booting - that would defeat the fix silently, and client Windows ships with it ON. EVIDENCE: 7 clean installs on the fixed path, 7/7 DESTROY, 0 WARM, 0 UNKNOWN, confirmed by a second instrument. CAVEAT ON STRENGTH, because the raw numbers overstate it: the historical control ("8 destroy / 6 warm" over 14 runs) was measured with prime-run's `restarts` counter alone, a 20 s poll that demonstrably misses short halts - three of the seven fixed-path runs recorded restarts=0 and were genuine destroys - so an unknown number of those six historical "warm resets" were not, the control's true rate is unknown and likely below 43%, and the 0.57^7 power calculation resting on 43% claims more than it should. The direction is not in doubt; the multiplier is. The historical runs CANNOT be re-checked: no xid was ever recorded for them. NOT ESTABLISHED: how the guest's vCPUs are reset in the in-place case (under Xen, QEMU models devices, not vCPUs), so the second half of the warm-reset mechanism is UNKNOWN - owner ruling 2026-09-09, not worth chasing, no useful application. Plausible but unverified consequence: an in-place reset reinitialises the device model without cleanly re-establishing PV state (grants, event channels, xenstore), which would explain why warm resets usually worked and occasionally left the PV bus permanently unbound. [also: installer] [verified 2026-09-09]

- P3 [SHIPPED AND VERIFIED IN 4.3.26] THE ERROR-NOTIFY ROUTE HAD NEVER DELIVERED A NOTIFICATION IN ANY RELEASE, while its suite reported 10/10. `Send-QwtError` returns `send` as soon as notifhost is LAUNCHED - it deliberately does not wait - and notifhost, run as SYSTEM, passes `SYSTEM` as qrexec-client-vm's local-user field, so the relay never connects back (`relay never connected` in bridge.log). Every real caller is SYSTEM: gui-agent is a service, activate-idd/deactivate-idd run from the installer. Fixed by re-running in the console user's session via the Task Scheduler; verified by a photographed bubble. KEPT AS A STANDING LESSON rather than deleted: the defect was invisible for as long as the suite graded ACKS, and it was caught the same day a dom0 render witness was added - which then needed THREE corrections of its own (whole-screen density loses to a busy desktop; persistence fails because a bubble auto-dismisses; region-local ambient only works with an ASSERTED notification-free baseline). Any check that grades "we handed it off" instead of "a human could see it" is the same bug waiting. [also: test-framework] [verified 2026-09-10]

[moved 2026-10-02: closed, Jev 0.77]
- P2 [FIXED 2026-09-09, fail-proofed] THE RESCUE GATE WAS DEFEATED BY MISSING DATA - prime-run's cpu reader printed the sentinel `9999` ("very busy") whenever libxl stats were UNREADABLE, so an unreadable stat and a busy guest were the same value, and it was the one value that disables recovery. Measured: win11-clean, 4.3.22 campaign, `admin.vm.Stats` went unreadable at the instant the install stuck (`cpu=` empty from t+414s to the end), every poll reset the quiet streak, `quiet >= 8` was never reached, the one-shot rescue never fired, and the run polled to the 3600 s DEADLINE. The floor condition held throughout - ONLY the streak was defeated. Fix: `NA` instead of `9999`, an unreadable cpu counts toward quiescence, plus a second INDEPENDENT trigger (`RESCUE_NOSHOW`, four consecutive no-window screen probes) because that is the direct observable of this stall and needs no stats. Fail-proof `mgmt/harness/failproof-prime-rescue.sh` replays the real recorded telemetry: defect re-introduced -> NEVER rescues (reproduces the deadline), fixed -> rescues at t+647s, healthy warm-reboot trace -> does not fire; 12/12. THE UNDERLYING TRIGGER IS NOT OURS AND IS NOT FIXED: a guest-initiated stage-1 reboot on a Xen HVM sometimes HALTS the domain and sometimes warm-resets it in place; on a warm reset the PV bus usually rebinds but occasionally does not, and stage 2's IDD activation then disables the emulated VGA so dom0 sees no window at all. That nondeterminism predates 4.3.22 (4.3.21 passed with it) and is why the harness needs a rescue that works. [verified 2026-09-09]

[moved 2026-10-02: closed, Jev 0.98]
- P3 [VERIFIED ON A GUEST 2026-09-21, AFTER THE VERIFICATION FOUND A SECOND DEFECT IN IT - not a clean green, and recorded as such: Jev graded the blanket claim "these fixes are verified on a guest" at 0.30 precisely because this one did not come out right first time] `KB2267602`'s row used to read "this probe cannot establish the version the offer carries", which made the item impossible to judge. The fix extracts the dotted quad from the offer's own title and compares it with `Get-MpComputerStatus`'s AntivirusSignatureVersion (digits only: the catalog's response LANGUAGE is nondeterministic, a version quad is not, and this is an EFFECT comparison - accept/reject still runs on the .msu filename). **ON A GUEST (win11de-fb9, env-assert gweck OK, artefact asserted before grading):** the first targeted pass INSTALLED the signature (`verified_by_effect=True`), which skips the branch entirely - so a repeat pass was run with the signature already current (SIG 1.459.324.0, offer advertises 1.459.324.0) and the log read `signature 1.459.324.0 is already at or past the offered 1.459.324.0 - nothing to do`. **THE DEFECT THAT VERIFICATION FOUND:** the LOG was right and the ROW was not - it still said `full signature package installed directly (verified by effect)` on a pass that had explicitly declined to verify by effect, and dom0 reads the ROW. The row now distinguishes three outcomes (already current / installed and verified / installed with the effect unverified) and carries `verified_by_effect`. **AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT THAT FIX WAS ALSO WRONG:** it keyed on the words `ALREADY CURRENT`, which appear only in the log line and never in `info_reason`, so it could not have fired - and text-matching is forbidden by ADR section 6. It is a structured `already_current` field now. Re-verified: `ROW kb=KB2267602 ok=True sev=ok eff=False reason=the image is already at the version this offer carries ...`. [verified 2026-09-21]

[moved 2026-10-02: closed, Jev 0.99]
- P3 [FIXED AND VERIFIED ON A GUEST 2026-09-21 - re-graded from P2, and the product gained the control that made it testable] The KB-less offer "Microsoft Corporation AudioProcessingObject Driver Update (1.0.4.7057)" was excluded with the reason "the catalog is keyed on KB so it cannot be resolved there, and there is no direct installer to fetch". The first half is true of the offer; the second half was FALSE. **RESOLVED AND INSTALLED ON win11de-fb9** (env-assert gweck OK, artefact asserted 177122 bytes before grading): `-OnlyUid 740c4b6c-bcfa-4f97-ae49-7bcc6933e696:1: acting on 1 of 7`; `no KB and no direct URL - trying the catalog by title`; `title resolve: 2 candidate(s); choosing by the architecture each PACKAGE declares (want amd64)` -> `2cd3775f... arch=arm64`, `aea787ca... arch=amd64`; then `driver added to the store (pnputil rc=0, verified by /enum-drivers)`. **MEASURED INDEPENDENTLY OF THE UPDATER'S OWN REPORT:** `pnputil /enum-drivers` matches for this driver went 0 BEFORE the pass to 2 AFTER it. **WHAT MADE IT TESTABLE AT ALL, and is now a product control:** `-OnlyUid`, which selects an offer by the UpdateID the offer itself carries (with or without `:rev`). `-OnlyKb` cannot name an offer that has no KB, which is exactly why this path had stayed verified off-guest only. Identity, never title text (ADR section 6). Its negative case came for free: a non-matching identity selected 0 of 7 and did not crash. **AND THE ROW NOW CARRIES THE MEASUREMENT:** it first read `ok=True` with an empty `verified_by_effect` and no reason - the same gap the Defender row had - so it now records `verified_by_effect` and names what was checked. [verified 2026-09-21]

[moved 2026-10-02: closed, Jev 0.98]
- P3 [FIXED AND DEMONSTRATED ON A GUEST 2026-09-20/21; WHAT REMAINS IS A CURIOSITY, NOT A DEFECT OF OURS - RE-GRADED FROM P1 2026-09-21 after the owner asked why a fixed item was still listed as open] `KB5007651` (Windows Security platform) was being excluded from dom0's count as "nothing to do on this image" while the offered installer carried a NEWER, APPLICABLE SecHealthUI than the guest had. ROOT CAUSE: `securityhealthsetup.exe` is a CONTAINER, not an installer, on a routeless path - it exits 0 in under a second and does nothing, and no extract switch works. FIX (5a930bb): `Get-EmbeddedAppx` carves the three .appx out of the PE (ZIP members, signatures untouched), tells the main package from its frameworks structurally, and `Add-AppxProvisionedPackage` installs it. MEASURED ON THE GERMAN 25H2 TEMPLATE: the provisioned SecHealthUI moved 1000.26100.8036.0 -> 1000.29628.1000.0 in eleven seconds, and the run that followed on win11de-fresh came back with ONE excluded item instead of three. The effect still decides: a call that moves no version is logged as exactly that, not as success. WHAT IS LEFT IS NOT OURS: why Microsoft's wrapper exe no-ops when invoked standalone. It writes no log of its own and it is built from the Defender/MpClient codebase. Nothing in our path depends on the answer. [verified 2026-09-21]

    (a) CONFOUNDED BY TIME. A with-feature arm followed by a without-feature arm, against a failure that CLUSTERS (2 events in 4 attempts inside one 5.5-hour window, 0 in the 27 installs outside it), differs by WHEN each arm ran as much as by the variable. If the condition is absent both arms read 0 and the feature looks exonerated; if it drifts in, whichever arm is running then looks guilty. CLAUDE.md already forbids this - 'Every build comparison runs at least 3 times per side, INTERLEAVED with the control' - and I proposed the sequential form anyway.
    (b) THE POWER NUMBER I COMPUTED FOR IT IS ALSO INVALID. '45 installs for 95% power' is binomial, i.e. it assumes independent trials at a constant p=6.5%. The cluster is direct evidence that p is NOT constant. Retracted. Under a two-state model (p~0 normally, high while some condition holds) the meaningful question is WHICH CONDITION, and no amount of uniform sampling answers it.
    WHAT A VALID PROCEDURE LOOKS LIKE, if this is ever run: arms INTERLEAVED install-by-install rather than in blocks, and the analysis conditioned on the cluster rather than pooled - or, better, skip sampling entirely and read the artifacts a natural occurrence now produces (DISKPROBE enumeration + MSI verbose log), which is one event rather than dozens.

The MoveUsers component (CoreComponents.wxs:337-350) does exactly two things: installs relocate-dir.exe and writes the registry value BootExecute='relocate-dir.exe C:\Users Q:\Users'. NO disk, volume or partition operation. PreparePrivateImg runs After=InstallFiles (Package.wxs:92), so at Q-creation time relocate-dir.exe is on disk and its BootExecute string is written but NEITHER HAS RUN (BootExecute executes only at boot). bind-dirs is not in the MSI at all. Clean installs clone from a pristine base that never ran junction code. There is therefore no mechanism by which either junction (relocate-dir OR bind-dirs) alters disk #1's number, name or RAW state before/during PreparePrivateImg - junction control is a downstream CONSUMER of Q:, not a producer of its absence. This does NOT contradict the owner's 09-14 point that bind-dirs is not excluded by the DATA (no pre-feature runs survive); it is excluded by the MECHANISM.

- P2 [PROFILED 2026-09-16 - VERDICT: NO SLEEP WORTH CHASING; THE LEVER IS RUNTIME C# COMPILES] INSTALL SPEED. Owner: "after we finish, chase sleeps". Profiled from the two fresh stage-2 logs (gaps between consecutive timestamped lines, largest first), no synthetic anything: CLEAN (WIN10, 3362004) stage 2 = 91 s: msiexec 33 s (not ours), vc_redist 9 s (not ours), payload SHA-256 over 84 files 9 s (a security property, keep), ETW-proxy account provisioning 7 s, PV-NIC seeding 5 s, updater-agent deploy 4 s, a 4 s hole after 'gui-agent.exe exited gracefully', PnP settle 3 s (a real event wait), devcon 2 s, hw-accel tweak 2 s. UPGRADE (win11 4.3.17->4.3.29) stage 2 = 24 s: ETW-proxy provisioning 12 s (HALF of it), PV-NIC seeding 3 s, driver-version check 2 s. The stage-transition Start-Sleep 5 s sites the audit listed did NOT appear on either measured path - they sit on the resume/retry branches. WHERE THE TIME ACTUALLY GOES: provision-etwproxy-account.ps1 has NO Start-Sleep - its cost is a runtime Add-Type of a ~90-line P/Invoke class plus New-LocalUser + LogonUser; pvnic-selfprime.ps1 invokes csc.exe on the guest to compile its ~370-line C# Windows SERVICE from source on every install (:588); set-autologon.ps1 and ensure-autologon.ps1 Add-Type too (one unguarded). Roughly 6-9 compiler invocations per stage 2, each seconds on PS 5.1 - an estimated 15-30 s of the 91, and 12 of the 24 on upgrade. Attributed from the gaps and the scripts' contents, not timed inside the scripts. THE ONE FIXED SLEEP ON THE HAPPY PATH: Install-QwtImproved.ps1:2553, 3 s after the display quiesce. Its comment says it lets the framebuffer grant 'go away' before device surgery; capture.c says grants are revoked only on the agent's own exit path, so on a GRACEFUL exit they are gone before WaitForExit returns and on a KILL they are never released - the 3 s is slack for dom0's unmap to land, which the guest CANNOT observe (audit #14: unobservable). Removing it would trade 3 s for an unverifiable cross-domain ordering risk. LEFT IN PLACE, deliberately. OWNER DECISION 2026-09-16: "keep compile-on-guest part" - the on-guest Add-Type / csc compiles STAY as they are. Prebuilding them in CI is NOT to be proposed again; the ~15-30 s they cost is accepted. With that, install speed has no remaining mandate: the sleeps are not there, and the compiles are kept. CLOSED. [verified 2026-09-16]

The English Enterprise-Evaluation image used for most testing has Defender effectively inert and completes the identical step in seconds (whole install ~4 min), which is why this has never been seen here before. **PRODUCT CONSEQUENCE, not just a rig artefact:** a user installing QWT on a normal Windows with Defender on can expect the PV-driver phase to take tens of minutes or to exceed any timeout the installer sets. **HARNESS CONSEQUENCE:** `cpu_time ADVANCED` must not be read as 'our install is progressing' - it needs to be attributed to a process before it means anything. [moved 2026-10-02: superseded, Jev 0.79]
- P1 [superseded detail] **ROOT CAUSE, from a CLEAN FRESH-BOOT measurement (this RETIRES the handshake verdict below; Jev `prior_verdict_retired` **0.87**):** on a fresh boot with NO resize, the Settings slot is perfectly healthy - `ctlseq=1` (one registration), `ack=2 ACTIVE`, `failhr=0`, session opened cleanly through firstPublish - and the broker published **exactly TWO frames** (`seq=4 frameid=2`, captick identical across samples 6 s apart) and then nothing, while the control slot ran `frameid 178 -> 189` in the same window and kept going. Those two frames ARE the splash. The splash is drawn by ApplicationFrameHost itself, i.e. it is the FRAME window's own content; the app's content lives in a `Windows.UI.Core.CoreWindow` child owned by a DIFFERENT process. So: **the WGC session captures the ApplicationFrameWindow's own surface, which stops changing once the hosted cross-process CoreWindow takes over rendering** - Jev `root_cause=frame-window-surface-stops-changing` **0.99**, fix `capture-the-corewindow-for-uwp-hosts` **0.73** (PrintWindow on the frame hwnd is proven to return the full page, so the polled-PW path at 0.19 is a viable second). **`chain_established` is 0.19 and stays there**: the counterfactual - that a session targeted at the CoreWindow DOES deliver changing frames - has NOT been run, and needs a build. RETIRED AS THE CAUSE, but real and separately measured: a post-resize state where `BrokerRegister` zeroes the handshake while leaving `Hwnd` unchanged, `Reconcile()` only reopens on an HWND change, and the slot sits agent-REQUESTED / broker-FREE for ever (Jev had rated that 0.93 when judged on the post-resize state alone). It is a genuine second defect on its own terms (`two_bugs` 0.72) and is what makes the freeze unrecoverable once a resize happens. **RETRACTED, was my third "unexplained" observation:** I reported that a relaunched Settings gets no slot, no dom0 mapping and no log line. On a fresh boot with tracing on it maps normally (886x693, tracked, `ApplicationFrameWindow`, broker slot 1 ACTIVE) - I had sampled too narrow a window and read absence as a defect. The owner corrected a related error the same way: what was on screen was lusrmgr, not Settings. Evidence: `scratchpad/` (captures, gitignored), Jev wire log. [verified 2026-09-25]

### RETRACTED (3)
[moved 2026-10-02: retracted, Jev 0.51]
- P1 [CORRECTED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-21 - I DIAGNOSED A GUEST TWICE AND WAS WRONG BOTH TIMES; THE PATTERN IS THE FINDING] `win11-acc`, left over from a failed prime-run, was started by a diagnostic probe, showed a booted Win11 desktop, and did not go down for twelve minutes after `qvm-shutdown`. I called that benign first ("no QWT, so it ignores ACPI" - fabricated, nothing measured says that) and then, when the owner challenged it, called it a WEDGED SPECIMEN of the deaf-guest class. BOTH READINGS ARE WITHDRAWN. The owner's: *"you again mistaken 'restarted without qwt and waiting for fuck knows what' for 'ignoring shutdown'"*. THAT FITS THE EVIDENCE AND MINE DID NOT: the guest carried a HALF-FINISHED INSTALL from the 11:27 campaign, and the probe leg that started it had NOTHING ASSIGNED - no answer stick. So it booted without QWT, resumed into the install's autologon desktop, and waited for a medium that was not attached, coming back up each time; every poll read `Running` because it kept returning, not because it was refusing. No wedge, no deafness, no specimen. THE RECURRING ERROR, which the owner named as happening EVERY TIME: a guest that has restarted and is WAITING gets diagnosed as something else - ignoring shutdown, wedged, stalled - because the state poll says `Running` and a screenshot shows a desktop. NEITHER IS A VERDICT. A desktop is what an autologon resume looks like; `Running` after a restart is indistinguishable from `Running` continuously unless the boot is timestamped. WHAT TO MEASURE INSTEAD, before naming any guest state: (a) has it BOOTED since the request - compare the guest's own boot time / uptime across two reads, not the toolstack's power state; (b) two screenshots a minute apart - does anything CHANGE; (c) what is it waiting FOR - which medium, service or stage the current boot expects and whether it is actually present. All three were available and none was taken. [also: test-framework] [verified 2026-09-21]

[moved 2026-10-02: retracted, Jev 0.74]
- P2 BOOT-TO-QREXEC VARIES 15-38 s AND THE CAUSE IS UNKNOWN. **RETRACTED 2026-09-09: the "~10 s slower on NON-CLEAN guests, sets are DISJOINT" claim below is WRONG and must not be repeated.** It was n=2 vs n=4 from a single campaign. The 4.3.21 final campaign added samples and the split does not survive: win11-acc CLEAN measured 33 s (the slowest standalone number on record) while win10-acc clean measured 15 s (the fastest), and across all 22 samples the ranges overlap completely - win11-acc (standalone, clean AND upgrade) 20/30/20/29/21/33/24, win10-acc 20/23/15, win11-app 38/28/30/22/23/34, win10-app 20/24/29/18/20/21. **The spread WITHIN one unchanged configuration (win11-acc: 20-33 s) is as large as the between-group difference the finding was built on**, so the grouping explained nothing. This is precisely the CLAUDE.md instrument rule I skipped: a metric must be shown stable on ONE unchanged binary across >=3 runs BEFORE any verdict, and I drew a disjointness verdict without ever establishing stability - the same way a whole bisect was voided once before. What the data now supports, weakly: a possible OS effect (win11 median ~28 s vs win10 ~20 s) that also overlaps and is NOT established. WHAT TO DO NEXT, in order: (1) establish the noise floor - boot ONE unchanged guest >=5 times and get the within-configuration distribution, because until that exists no comparison between configurations means anything; (2) only then compare configurations; (3) attribute with `mgmt/harness/boot-attribution.sh` (added 2026-09-09), which reads what Windows already records - `Diagnostics-Performance/Operational` event 100 carries BootTime/MainPathBootTime/BootPostBootTime per boot and 101/102/103/106/109 NAME the slow application, driver, service, background process or device. Check event 100 FIRST: if two guests report the same MainPathBootTime, the difference is AFTER the boot Windows measures (service start ordering, qubesdb readiness, QrexecAgent's 1 Hz readiness poll) and no 103 line will show it. NOT a release blocker (owner 2026-09-09) and not a regression against any baseline - no prior boot-to-qrexec numbers exist, because the harness previously recorded only "desktop shell up", a DIFFERENT quantity (autologon + profile + explorer) that says nothing about when dom0 can talk to the qube. Needs a guest that SURVIVES a run - cells reap at their start, so measure on a preserved subject or a dedicated boot. [also: graphics-performance] [verified 2026-09-09] --- SUPERSEDED ORIGINAL CLAIM, kept only so it is not re-derived: "~10 s slower on non-clean guests", acceptance 2026-09-08 (matrix.sh `start_vm` now records every cold boot to `$M/boot-to-qrexec.tsv`, and writes `no-qrexec-within-600s` rather than a number when a boot misses the ceiling, so absent guests cannot flatter it): clean installs 20, 20 s; upgrade-over-golden 30 s; AppVM cold boots 28, 30, 38 s. `{20,20}` vs `{28,30,30,38}` are DISJOINT. Not an AppVM-vs-standalone effect - the upgrade guest is a standalone too - so the discriminator is something the clean guests do not carry (installed state? service count? profile creation?). NOT a release blocker (owner 2026-09-09) and not a regression against any measured baseline: no prior boot-to-qrexec numbers exist, because until now the harness only recorded "desktop shell up at t+16-17 s", which is a DIFFERENT quantity (autologon + profile + explorer) and says nothing about when dom0 can talk to the qube. INSTRUMENT TO USE, do not theorise: Windows records the answer itself in `Diagnostics-Performance/Operational` - event 100 carries `BootTime`/`MainPathBootTime`/`BootPostBootTime` per boot, and events 101/102/103/106/109 NAME the slow application, driver, service, background process or device. That turns "~10 s slower" into "this component costs N ms". Two candidates worth testing first: our own service start ordering (`QrexecAgent`'s readiness wait on qubesdb is a 1 Hz poll, so up to ~1 s of slack per boot, and the SCM dependency orders process start rather than readiness), and simply more installed state on non-clean guests. Needs a guest that SURVIVES a run - the cells reap at their start, so this must be measured on a preserved subject or a dedicated boot. [also: graphics-performance] [verified 2026-09-08]

[moved 2026-10-02: merged into THE STALL]
- P1 [CORRECTED 2026-09-22 BY A 1:1 READ OF THE TWO RUNS] **THE STALL DOES NOT HAPPEN DURING THE INSTALL - IT HAPPENS BEFORE THE INSTALLER PRODUCES ANYTHING.** The same test ran twice on 2026-09-22 against the same golden (win11-qwt, QWT 4.3.17.0/77c4d69), the same package (4.3.29+agent.0ca8a70b) and the same harness: it PASSED at 09:30 and STALLED at 11:15. Their logs are IDENTICAL for the first 15 lines - disc verify, run marker, launch - and diverge at the first post-launch sample. THE DECISIVE FACTS, read from the artefacts: (a) the stalled run's `install.tail` contains ONE line, the harness's own run marker; the passing run's contains 31 lines ending in the RESULT trailer - **the guest produced ZERO installer output**; (b) the last qrexec call the guest ANSWERED was the marker write at 11:16:36Z, and the very next call, `cmd /c start "" /min D:\install.cmd /auto`, hit its 60 s `grun` timeout at 11:17:36Z - the log line "install.cmd /auto launched" is printed unconditionally and is NOT evidence that it ran; (c) by contrast the passing run was at `signalled Global\QGA_SHUTDOWN` with 172 installer log lines read by t+20s. **So the failure window is 60 seconds wide, between two qrexec calls, and nothing in the record shows the installer executing a single step. Every earlier framing of this issue - including mine, and the experiments built on it - assumed the stall happened during PV-driver replacement. That assumption has no support.** On the wedged domain (9626): two vCPUs at 100% for 17+ minutes, vCPU0 looping at ntoskrnl+0x25120a polling [KPRCB+0x2d80] with a 32-bit counter advancing, vCPU2 with vector 0xe1 requesting-undelivered across three captures; the other two vCPUs halted in HalProcessorIdle with IF=1 and nothing pending; NO disk I/O across the stall (xentop VBD counters identical 17 minutes apart). The 09-20 core has `KPRCB+0x2d80 == 0` on all four vCPUs, so the two specimens are NOT demonstrably one class. **Jev, after adversarial refutation of six candidate mechanisms (workflow wf_c0785dd5-6aa): NONE survives as a cause** - the best-ranked stands at `supported` 0.10 with `biggest_hole=direction-of-causation-unmeasured` 0.52; bugcheck 0.06, same-version DIFx reinstall 0.06, thermal 0.09, agent-exit 0.07, CD-frontend 0.04. **CAUSE UNKNOWN.** FOUR CORRECTIONS TO WHAT I PREVIOUSLY WROTE HERE: "0 active grant entries" was MISSING DATA (the capture carries dom0's own `grant-CAPTURE-INCOMPLETE` marker), not a measurement; the install did not start at 11:17:36Z, that is when the call TIMED OUT; the specimen was not released at ~11:30Z - it was captured alive at 11:42:13Z and released after; and the 16 A/B runs plus 33 aging cycles tested a premise the evidence never supported. **THE FAILED LAUNCH CALL IS NOT THE DISCRIMINATOR - measured 2026-09-22 17:36 on the first gated reproduction pass.** That pass matched the reference on all 8 recorded dimensions (gate: 0 DIFFERS, matches_recorded 0.70) and its launch call ALSO did not return (rc=255 after 31 s) - and then the guest came back, the installer produced 46 lines and a RESULT trailer at t+78s, exactly like the passing run. So a launch call that does not return is ORDINARY: the MSI replaces the agent, qrexec drops for ~60 s and returns. What is unique to the reference is that it produced ZERO installer output and NEVER recovered - i.e. the open question is whether install.cmd ever started there at all. I called that pass a reproduction when the event fired and was wrong: the signature it matched is shared by healthy runs. Evidence: `scratchpad/stall-win11nfy-*`, `/home/user/qwt-quick-upgrade/win11-nfy-20260922-173351/`, `/home/user/qwt-quick-upgrade/win11-nfy-20260922-{093018,111533}/`. [verified 2026-09-22] **NAMED 2026-09-25 (the observations above were already right; what was missing was the name).** `ntoskrnl+0x25120a polling [KPRCB+0x2d80]` is **`nt!KiIpiSendRequestEx`** spinning on **`_KPRCB.PacketBarrier`**, with `+0x2d84 = TargetCount` (how many targets still owe an acknowledgement) and vector `0xe1` = the Windows IPI vector; resolved against the guest's own ntoskrnl PDB, fetched from msdl using the CodeView GUID read out of the core (`tools/pdb-symbolize.py`). The requesting path, read off the stacks: the memory manager's working-set ager taking a TLB shootdown (`MiAgePteWorker` -> `MiAgeWorkingSetTail` -> `KeFlushTb` -> `KxFlushEntireTb` -> `KiFlushCurrentTbWorker`). The note that "the 09-20 core has `KPRCB+0x2d80 == 0` on all four vCPUs, so the two specimens are NOT demonstrably one class" is CONFIRMED and now has a mechanism: PacketBarrier==0 everywhere means no outstanding shootdown, which is the IDLE/lost-wakeup shape, while the 09-22 core has PRCB[1] PacketBarrier=1 TargetCount=1 - the SPIN shape. Two shapes (Jev `two_classes` 0.79); whether the SPIN captures share one mechanism is `insufficient-evidence` 0.65. **Blocked on the owner:** dom0 runs the old `11-wedge-forensics.sh`; the in-repo copy now extracts `vmcs-d<domid>.txt` and auto-takes the core on the SPIN shape, which is what finally gets both halves from ONE instance (Jev `next_action` 1.00). Read any bundle with `tools/wedge-vmcs.py`. [verified 2026-09-25] **THIRD SPIN SPECIMEN, AND THE FIRST WHOSE RIPs WERE RESOLVED - 2026-09-26, win11de-led.** A quick-upgrade deploy of release 36239589843 over the German golden wedged at the `install.cmd /auto` launch (call rc=124 at 60 s, qrexec then silent 359 s while cpu_time advanced 34718, screen=NOWINDOW). **OUR PACKAGE NEVER INSTALLED** - the launch call is what hung - so what wedged is the GOLDEN's stock QWT 4.3.29 and stock xenbus 9.1.0.0, and the build under test (the broker relay) is EXCULPATED (Jev `relay_build_exculpated` 0.89). An 8.6 GB ELF core was taken from the LIVE wedge in 46 s and the guest was not rebooted or recloned before it. `tools/xen-core-rip.py`: **vcpu0/2/3 all at RIP `0xfffff8029f6a5aaf` with the SAME CR3 `0x1aa000`; vcpu1 alone at `0xfffff80231dbca34`**, IF=1 everywhere. Resolved against `module-bases.txt` recorded BY THAT SAME BOOT (237 modules, recorder armed pre-install): `ntoskrnl.exe + 0x6a5aaf` for the three, **`xenbus.sys + 0x1ca34` for vcpu1**. NO PDB was matched, so neither RVA has a function name and the shared-RIP address is NOT yet shown to be `KiIpiSendRequestEx`. **SYMBOLISED LATER THE SAME DAY, AND IT INVERTS THE READING - RETRACTED: THIS IS NOT THE SPIN SHAPE.** The guest's own `ntkrnlmp.pdb` (GUID 24DE61DB3A27C5B383DFF181CB51F7E1, fetched from msdl with `tools/pdb-symbolize.py`) names RVA `0x6a5aaf` as **`HalProcessorIdle + 0xf`**. The three vCPUs sharing that RIP are therefore **IDLE**, and three idle cores sitting at one address is the most ordinary observation there is - it is not a fingerprint of anything. I had read "3 of 4 share a RIP and a CR3" as the multi-vCPU IPI-wait shape; that was wrong, and the Jev call above was asked on that mistaken premise (its answer, `shape-confirmed-culprit-unnamed`, happens to survive the correction - no culprit is named either way). **WHAT THE IMAGE ACTUALLY SHOWS: three vCPUs idle and exactly ONE vCPU busy - vcpu1, in `xenbus.sys + 0x1ca34`** - which puts today's specimen on the IDLE/lost-wakeup side of the two-shapes split, alongside the 09-20 core, NOT with the 09-22 SPIN core. That is consistent with the 09-22 re-read below (no shared RIPs there). `xenbus.sys` CANNOT be symbolised this way: its PDB is a private build path (`C:\builder\build\vmm-xen-windows-pvdrivers\...\xenbus.pdb`, GUID 890D5CF09D974F99A5D7D8D0AE1AA010), not published on msdl, so that RVA has no name and **the owner-retired xenbus line stays closed** (Jev 0.19) - a single busy vCPU inside a driver is where an instruction pointer was, not a cause. **AND NO culprit is named** - one vCPU's instruction pointer is not causation, and the retired xenbus line is NOT reopened by it (Jev `reopens_retired_xenbus_line` **0.19**; the line stays closed, stock xenbus remains the intended state). **THE TWO-SHAPES FINDING IS CORROBORATED** by re-reading the 2026-09-22 win10-acc core today: it has **no shared RIPs at all** (three vCPUs share only CR3 `0x1aa000`, all four RIPs distinct), where today's has three vCPUs on one RIP - so the specimens remain demonstrably not one class. Jev's next measurement: `unwind-the-stacks` 0.42, ahead of `second-specimen` 0.29 and `symbolise-the-two-rvas` 0.25 - a low-confidence spread, i.e. no single decisive next step. **TWO INSTRUMENTS FAILED DURING THIS CAPTURE AND ARE FIXED IN `8a961ba`:** (a) the dom0 forensics service returned a tar containing ONLY `capture.log: set DEV to the dev qube` - `dom0/11-wedge-forensics.sh` required `DEV` on line 30 while the qrexec wrapper sets only `VM` and returns the tar on stdout, so the automatic capture aborted before collecting a byte; DEV is optional now, and **re-installing the dom0 copy is still an owner action**. This was a RECENT regression, not a long blackout - the 2026-09-25 win11-nfy capture collected normally. (b) `tools/xen-core-rip.py` prints `<objdump produced nothing>` at every RIP in every core read today, so no instruction text has ever come out of it. Also unfixed: the memory image nearly did not happen at all, refused for being **104 MiB short of disk**. Evidence: `/home/user/wedge-cores/win11de-led-20260926T125020Z.core`, `/home/user/qwt-quick-upgrade/win11de-led-20260926-122139/`. [verified 2026-09-26] **THE WEDGED THREAD IS OURS, THE CALL IS NAMED, AND IT HOLDS TWO IRPs - 2026-09-26, same core.** Method: ABI-correct unwind using the guest's OWN `.pdata`/`UNWIND_INFO` read from the core's mapped PE images (`tools/core-unwind.py` - a heuristic scan is not enough, Jev rated one `cannot-tell-stale-vs-live` 0.66), then the process identified by walking `PsActiveProcessHead` and matching `_KPROCESS.DirectoryTableBase` to the busy vCPU's CR3, then the thread by bracketing RSP with `_KTHREAD.StackLimit/StackBase`, then its `_ETHREAD.IrpList`. All offsets from the guest's own PDB. **THE STACK** (terminates cleanly at the user boundary): `xenbus+0x1ca34` <- `xenbus+0x5c46` <- `xeniface+0xa7c1/+0xad5a/+0x16e3` <- `IopfCallDriver` <- `IofCallDriver` <- `IopSynchronousServiceTail` <- `IopXxxControlFile` <- `NtDeviceIoControlFile` <- `KiSystemServiceCopyEnd`. The scan's extra frames (`xen.sys`, `IopCloseFile`, `ObCloseHandleTableEntry`, `KiCommitThreadWait`) are STALE and absent from the real walk. **THE PROCESS: `gui-agent.exe`, pid 4612** (EPROCESS 0xffff9c87045ad0c0, DirTableBase 0x163243000, one match in 148). **THE CALLS, read from the IRPs themselves, not guessed:** that thread (ETHREAD 0xffff9c87045b4080) holds **TWO** outstanding `IRP_MJ_DEVICE_CONTROL` IRPs on the SAME xeniface device object 0xffff9c87039f4040 - `0x00222048` = **`IOCTL_XENIFACE_EVTCHN_CLOSE`** (the one the stack is stuck inside) and `0x00222093` = **`IOCTL_XENIFACE_GNTTAB_PERMIT_FOREIGN_ACCESS_V2`**. The vendored xeniface header says of the latter, verbatim: *"This IOCTL must be asynchronous. The driver doesn't complete the request until the grant is explicitly revoked or the calling thread terminates."* **SO THE THREAD THAT OWNS THE PERPETUALLY-PENDING FRAMEBUFFER-GRANT IRP IS ALSO THE THREAD ISSUING SYNCHRONOUS xeniface IOCTLs** - and in our source both sit on the main event loop: `CaptureStagingGrantNow()` (capture.c:683) is called from `SetSeamlessMode` (main.c:5845) and `libvchan_close(g_Vchan)` from `WatchForEvents` (main.c:11456); libvchan's teardown is what issues the close (`upstream/.../libxenvchan/io.c:578 XcEvtchnClose`). **JUDGED, NOT ASSERTED:** Jev `thread_sharing_is_a_defect` **0.80** - a hazard worth fixing on its own merits, because the grant's lifetime is tied to that thread; `preventable_on_our_side` = **`separate-the-grant-thread-from-teardown` 0.76** (give the grant a dedicated thread that does nothing else). But `now_a_diagnosis` is **0.49** - this is NOT established as the CAUSE of the wedge, and it must not be written up as one. **WHAT IS STILL MISSING:** `xenbus+0x1ca34` is unsymbolised (no published PDB), so it is NOT shown the CPU is inside `EvtchnClose` or a spinlock acquire; no disassembly was obtained, so "spinning" is inferred from one busy vCPU plus the `KeAcquireSpinLockAtDpcLevel` at DISPATCH_LEVEL on that source path (`xenbus/src/xenbus/evtchn.c:929`); WHO holds any lock is unmeasured and the other three vCPUs are IDLE, so "another CPU holds it" is NOT supported; whether the two IRPs interact is a hypothesis, not a finding; and it is ONE specimen of an intermittent fault - the next deploy on the same golden installed cleanly. **The owner-retired xenbus line stays closed:** none of this asserts a xenbus defect. `worth_reporting_upstream` 0.58, and any such report needs the owner's approval of exact text. [verified 2026-09-26] **2026-09-27: THE OWNER STOPPED THE IMAGE FORENSICS AND JEV JUDGED THE WHOLE LINE** (CORRECTED 2026-09-30 by the owner: what stopped was the effort on IMPROVING the forensics harness - "examining the immediate failure or fixing a thing quickly is a different story"; live stalls are examined with the existing capture) - root cause `insufficient-evidence` 0.45, `chain_established` 0.11, forensics moved the fix closer 0.24; the 09-26 grant-thread lead above scored **0.01** as THE cause. Under the owner's guest-side-only rule the best lever is a Windows processor limit (0.49, low confidence), which would most likely LOWER the rate, not stop it (0.91). Details and the rejected dom0 option: findings/wedge.md. [verified 2026-09-27]
