- P1 [MEASURED 2026-09-21 - THE HUNG SHUTDOWN RECURRED AND THE SPECIMEN IS PRESERVED, UNKILLED] `win11de-ctlb` was sent a polite ACPI shutdown at 00:13:34 after a normal update pass. qrexec went dead, and the domain was still `Running` with no S5 at 01:01 - 48 minutes - so `qwt_shutdown` gave up without killing it, as it is built to. THE OBVIOUS RIG EXPLANATION WAS TESTED AND REFUTED: dom0's thin pool was at 99.3% at the time (it did cause a concurrent clone to fail, verbatim), but freeing 17.7 GB and sending the guest NOTHING left it exactly as it was fifteen minutes later. 6.2 GB free was always ample for a shutdown's writes; the two failures were coincident, not causal. EVIDENCE SO FAR: 94 active grant entries (not exhaustion); the dom0 capture service returned only a partial record (grants and domid, no vCPU states), and the Admin API reports no CPU time for the domain, so the datum that would separate a spinning CPU wedge from blocked I/O is NOT yet in hand. `tools/qtest dumpcore` would give a memory image but writes ~8 GB into a dom0 that is at 97% - not run for that reason. The guest is UNTOUCHED and must stay that way until interrogated; it is the first preserved specimen since the harness stopped killing them. **RECLASSIFICATION WITHDRAWN, MEASURED 2026-09-21.** I briefly recorded this guest as healthy on the owner's report of seeing a healthy terminal window; the owner themself flagged they might be looking at the wrong qube, and they were. `local.WinScreenshot` run against BOTH running guests in the same minute: `win11de-ctld` returns a real capture (`win-0.png`, 1115x628, a Windows Terminal showing `C:\PROGRA~1\QUBEST~1\bin'` - the updater mid-pass), while `win11de-ctlb` returns rc=1 with ZERO BYTES, which is this project's own documented "no visible windows" error (mgmt/CLAUDE.md:32). So the healthy window is ctld's; **ctlb has no mapped windows at all** and remains qrexec-dead with no S5 after eight hours. It stays a preserved, unexplained subject - NOT killed, and NOT called a stall: Jev's classification from the measured facts stands unchanged (insufficient-evidence 0.58, s5-never-delivered 0.41, cpu-wedge 0.00, same_as_known_stall 0.36), and the measurement it rates decisive at 0.97 - per-vCPU run states - is still not obtainable from this qube. NOTE: an empty `qtest shot` tar means NO VISIBLE WINDOWS, not a broken instrument; the instrument was proven working against ctld in the same minute. [verified 2026-09-21]

[moved 2026-10-02: merged into THE STALL]
- P1 [MEASURED 2026-09-10 - the post-install-reboot stall is a MULTI-vCPU DEADLOCK IN THE GUEST, not a shutdown problem] A clean install stalled on round 1 of a repeat hunt and the subject was preserved UNKILLED for the first time. dom0 forensics (dom0/11-wedge-forensics.sh, capture wedge-20260910-100313) show, over 10 s: vCPU0 r-- 2237.9->2247.9 and vCPU1 r-- 2192.5->2202.5 (+10.0 s each = 100% of a core apiece), while vCPU2 --- 47.2->47.2 and vCPU3 --- 50.4->50.4 are FROZEN, never scheduled. Corroborated from this qube: cputime +54.4 s over 30 s wall (181% of a core). The PV console capture taken IN DOM0 is EMPTY, so the guest is stuck below the OS - which also retires the worry that earlier missing consoles were an artefact of my own kills. Grants 60 active (not exhaustion); the stubdomain is alive and blocked (the device model is fine). No S5 ever reaches the toolstack, which is why the domain stays Running and ignores further ACPI requests. THIS RETIRES THREE EXPLANATIONS BUILT EARLIER THE SAME DAY, all mine: a hung service-stop at shutdown (that idles at ~0% CPU, not 181%), Startup Repair (idles at a prompt - and the screen photographed then was on a guest I had killed repeatedly, so it proved nothing), and the dirty-volume/flush story (the flush IS a real hazard mitigation - shutdown under write load is unclean, both arms 3/3 - but it is NOT the cause of this). ORDER-OF-WORK LESSON: CPU consumption should have been measured BEFORE two explanations were built on a screenshot. IT IS THE SIGNATURE of the IPI/TLB-shootdown wedge already in this register - two CPUs spinning, the CPUs they would wait on never running - but the MECHANISM IS NOT CONFIRMED and must not be asserted without a memory image. NMI WAS FIRED (xl trigger, 07:03:50) and produced NO bugcheck and no dump, though the spin fell 181%->100%, consistent with vCPU0 taking the NMI; likely reason, self-consistent with the class: a bugcheck must freeze the other processors via IPIs, so if IPI delivery is what is wedged the crash path deadlocks too - which also means NMICrashDump=1 in the image would NOT help and was deliberately not added. ONLY REMAINING ROUTE to name the spinning code: `xl dump-core <vm> <file>` from dom0 (no guest cooperation; ~8 GB for an 8192 MB guest). Rate: 1 stall in the first round of a 10-round hunt, plus 2 in the preceding day's campaigns. RECURRED ON win11de-gwt AND WAS HELD FOR 62 HOURS (captures scratchpad/wedge-win11de-gwt-20260920, plus the 09-18 01:31 capture of the SAME domid 9007, so one continuous episode): onset 2026-09-18 00:35:04, the last dom0 qrexec line for the VM ("vchan connection closed early"), after ~3 h of normal service - so this class is NOT confined to the post-install reboot. The deadlock is perfectly STATIC across those 62 h: vCPU1 130.6 s and vCPU2 102.4 s are byte-identical at both captures (never scheduled ONCE), while vCPU0 and vCPU3 each took +9.9 s per 10 s sample at both, spinning 219,035 s between them; active grants 811 at both. TWO DIFFERENCES from the 09-10 case, unexplained: 811 grants vs 60, and the PV console could not be ATTACHED at all ("Unable to attach console") rather than attaching empty. The dom0 gui-daemon for the VM (PID 1885516, alive since 09-18) logged only "Icon size: 128x128" at both captures, so the GUI protocol dialog the owner saw - quoting version 0:0, i.e. four zero bytes where the version word belongs - came from a different, short-lived daemon invocation that exit(1)'d, and is NOT recorded anywhere reachable from this qube. Jev graded the specimen: failure_class=known-p1-multivcpu-deadlock conf 0.94 (gui-protocol-fault 0.00), more_evidence_from_specimen 0.26 (the recoverable evidence is taken), best_next_action=dom0-memory-image-then-kill 0.51, and - the one that matters for planning - vcpu_reduction_is_a_fix 0.17: dropping 4 vCPUs to 2 is NOT a fix, at best it narrows the window. dialog_is_consequence came back only 0.55, so the 0:0 word is NOT safe to write off as pure symptom. BLOCKER CLEARED 2026-09-20: the owner took the first guest memory image this project has ever had (`xl dump-core`, 8.6 GB, /home/user/QubesIncoming/dom0/win11de-gwt-9007.core), and tools/xen-core-rip.py reads it with no gdb, no symbols and no copy. THE MECHANISM IS A LOCK, NOT AN IPI/TLB SHOOTDOWN - the hypothesis this register carried since 09-10 is REFUTED by measurement, exactly as the "must not be asserted without a memory image" clause anticipated. Measured per-vCPU: vCPU1 and vCPU2 are at the SAME RIP 0xfffff80285ca950f, which disassembles to `sti; hlt; ret` at ntoskrnl.exe+0x6a950f - they are the IDLE LOOP, halted and available, not wedged at all, which is why Xen reports them -b- and why they never accumulate time. vCPU0 (RIP 0xfffff8021850cb3c) and vCPU3 (RIP 0xfffff8021850cd3c) are in TWO INLINED COPIES of one lock-acquire retry loop, spinning on THE SAME LOCK - vCPU0 via [rsi+0x1800] and vCPU3 via [rbx+0x1800], where rsi(vCPU0) == rbx(vCPU3) == 0xffffaa8f77264000, so both resolve to VA 0xffffaa8f77265800 (GPA 0x110465800). (An earlier line here said "different lock words" - RETRACTED 2026-09-20, it was read off two registers without checking they held the same value.) THE LOCK WORD ITSELF READS 0x00000001: bit0 set = HELD, waiter bits 0x0 = NOBODY QUEUED. Tracing the loop against that value explains the permanence exactly: `lock cmpxchg` compares EAX, which the loop has just set to (word & 0xfffffffe) = 0, against the actual word 1 - so the CAS fails on EVERY iteration and `jne` goes back to the top forever. The two CPUs never enqueue themselves as waiters (which is why the waiter bits stay 0 and why they burn 100% of a core each in the fast path instead of blocking), and the holder is not running on any CPU - the only other two are in the idle loop. This is a LEAKED / NEVER-RELEASED LOCK, not contention. Loop body: `lock orl $0x0,(%rsp)` barrier, read the word, bit0 = held, upper bits = a queued waiter list built as a doubly-linked list, `lock cmpxchg`, `jne` back to the top, plus a second `test $0xfffffffe,%r8d; jne` that re-spins while any waiter is present. Both spinners share CR3 0x128139000 (a user process context); the idle pair share CR3 0x1aa000. So: two CPUs spin forever on locks nobody releases while two CPUs sit idle with interrupts enabled - a lost wakeup or an unreleased lock, NOT a shootdown that never completes. THE MODULE IS IDENTIFIED from the PE headers walked out of the image: base 0xfffff802184f0000, size 0x31000, pdb `C:\builder\build\vmm-xen-windows-pvdrivers\<bus-driver>\vs2022\Windows10Release\x64\<bus-driver>.pdb`, i.e. the Xen PV BUS DRIVER, at +0x1cb3c and +0x1cd3c. THAT NAME IS ON THE RETIRED LIST AND STAYS RETIRED: the bucket-lock patch was parked (8376f69) and reverted entirely (f7c16ce) on the owner's call - "we do not touch it anymore, this change was reverted" - and NOTHING here reopens that. This measurement does not say the reverted patch would have fixed anything; 8376f69's own reason for parking it (the wedge recurred on the patched build) is untouched and still stands. What it does say is that the spinning code is in a component that is NOT OURS, which puts it under CLAUDE.md's outside-QWT-scope exception, i.e. an upstream report - owner approves the exact text first. Evidence: scratchpad/wedge-win11de-gwt-20260920/, scratchpad/core-rip.json. NOTE: the 8.6 GB image sits on a /home that is 96% full with 2.5 GB free - it is irreplaceable and must be moved, not deleted. **RECURRED 2026-09-25 DURING RELEASE ACCEPTANCE** for 4.3.32+agent.7ceecc3f, on `win11-nfy` inside the notify-errors feature test's quick-upgrade: qrexec unanswering 358 s while cpu_time ADVANCED 131322 over the sample, `screen=NOWINDOW` - the fingerprint in this entry. It took BOTH feature tests with it (crop-before-map then ran on the same wedged guest and reported "no gui-agent log on the guest - nothing to grade"), so the release could not be cut on that run. The 8-cell matrix, INCLUDING both upgrade cells, passed rc=0 in the same campaign - so this is the intermittent class, not a regression in the package. Automatic capture fired but yielded little: the dom0 forensics service again returned a PARTIAL record (capture.log only, "grant entries: 0", which this entry already flags as missing data rather than a measurement), and the memory image was REFUSED for space - 8 GiB free where ~9 GiB is needed. Evidence: `/home/user/qwt-quick-upgrade/win11-nfy-20260925-094158/`. **AND THE SPECIMEN WAS THEN DESTROYED BY ME** - I killed and removed the guest to free the rig after reading the core-fetch refusal as final, when under 1 GiB of re-downloadable artefacts would have bought the image. Disk is now at 9.6 GiB free specifically so the next occurrence can be imaged, and fetch-wedge-core names the remedy instead of just refusing. See findings/rig.md. [moved 2026-10-02: merged into THE STALL]
- P1 [UNPARKED BY THE OWNER 2026-09-17 - IN SCOPE OF GWECK'S UPDATE PATH: "the upgrade should work end to end. if it does not, it is the same bug in a different shape and it was NOT fixed and the fix is worth nothing until complete." The 2026-09-16 parking ("no wedge experiments") is superseded for this path: it struck the German 25H2 template during an update applying boot (specimen 10), so the hunt runs on that environment, with the attach-once console held through the risk window and module bases armed; the dom0 debug-keys service (dom0/11 + dom0/13) is the owner's to install and is the only RIP source. dom0/11-wedge-forensics.sh carries the debug-keys d/v capture (7cd2a5a), dormant] [MECHANISM NAMED ON ONE SIDE, TRIGGER STILL UNKNOWN - current state 2026-09-11] THE "GUEST NEVER CAME BACK" STALL. Fingerprint: domain Running, qrexec dead, `qtest shot` an EMPTY tar, and AT LEAST ONE vCPU BURNING 88-101% OF A CORE sustained. That burn is the 8/8 invariant across all eight captured events, against <=42% in four non-events - it is the discriminator to reach for first, it needs only two `xl vcpu-list` samples, and it perturbs nothing. ### DO NOT USE pause_flags=4 AS THE MARKER (corrected 2026-09-11, it was this entry's headline for a day): a HEALTHY second Windows HVM in one capture has a vCPU at pause_flags=4, because every HVM guest passes through VPF_blocked_in_xen while an emulated I/O is in flight. The marker for a vCPU blocked FOREVER is state `---` (or -b-) WITH A ZERO CPUTIME DELTA across two samples - specimen 3's vCPU2 read 39.4 -> 39.4 s over 14 minutes. Also: `xl debug-keys q` is NOT read-only - it ends in send_guest_vcpu_virq(v, VIRQ_DEBUG) for every vCPU of every domain, which the guest's xenbus handles at HIGH_LEVEL. Existing captures survive that objection but stop calling it non-perturbing. ### THE SPINNING CODE IS NAMED, AND IT IS IN THE PV DRIVERS WE SHIP. xenbus StoreSubmitRequest (upstream/ro/qubes-vmm-xen-windows-pvdrivers/xenbus/src/xenbus/store.c:898) raises to DISPATCH_LEVEL, takes Context->Lock, then loops `while (State != XENBUS_STORE_REQUEST_COMPLETED) { EVTCHN(Wait,...); StorePollLocked(); }` with NO ITERATION BOUND AND NO ERROR EXIT - a STATUS_TIMEOUT only emits Warning("TIMED OUT") and iterates again. ~10 xenstore entry points contend on that lock. Its body EvtchnWait (evtchn.c:1008) is a busy-spin: KeQuerySystemTime(&Start) once, then `for (;;) { ...; KeQuerySystemTime(&Now); ...; _mm_pause(); }`. That explains the ENTIRE observed register fingerprint: _mm_pause() is F3 90 (two bytes) = the alternating RIP pair ...bfcfe/...bfd00; Now re-read per iteration = rbx, the only varying register; Start captured once = rcx, byte-identical across 30 s (specimen 3) and 1 h 38 min (specimen 2); DISPATCH_LEVEL = RFLAGS.IF=1; one core per 5 s timeout then re-entered = the sustained one-core burn; per-processor at raised IRQL = rdi being the caller's own KPRCB. UNVERIFIED LIABILITY: 0x133 DPC_WATCHDOG should arguably have fired; the guess is that 0x133 times the DPC DISPATCHER while this is a thread at raised IRQL, and 0x101 cannot apply because clock interrupts sit above DISPATCH_LEVEL. Not checked. ### LEADING UNIFICATION: XENSTORE IS A SHARED DEPENDENCY OF BOTH SIGNATURES. The stubdom's QEMU makes SYNCHRONOUS xs_* calls from its MAIN LOOP; the guest's xenbus makes synchronous xenstore requests at DISPATCH_LEVEL under a lock. If xenstore stops progressing for a domain, QEMU's main loop blocks (its vCPUs then sit in an ioreq wait) AND xenbus spins forever - one cause, both signatures, neither causing the other. Supporting detail previously dismissed as benign: specimen 3's stubdom log opens with two `xs_mkdir device-model/7073/backends/vkbd: failed` lines. This SUPERSEDES the earlier 'one phenomenon in two stages' reading, which needed the block to cause the spin. ### THE DEVICE-MODEL HALF IS MEASURED BUT ITS CAUSE IS NOT. Specimen 1 and specimen 3 both show guest vCPUs blocked in wait_for_io (ioreq.c:186, reached only via wait_on_xen_event_channel) with frozen cputime, and specimen 3 gives the live proof that the main loop stopped dispatching: its stubdom ANSWERED a qmp_capabilities at 15:46Z and, 63 minutes later, delivered the greeting (IOThread alive) but never answered the same command. Stall onset is dated to ~2 min after domain start by `qubes_gui: viewer disconnected` at 15:48:13Z, with no error logged in 300 lines. Xen's ioreq wait has no timeout, no watchdog and no log line, and one blocked vCPU prevents domain_shutdown from finalising - which is the 'Running forever, deaf to ACPI, killable only by destroy' half. WHAT BLOCKS THE MAIN LOOP IS NOT DETERMINABLE from what we hold: no stubdom thread stacks in any capture. All three specimens ran their disks as `-drive file=/dev/xvd?,if=ide,...,cache=writeback` (read off QEMU's own command line), i.e. the pre-PV-handover window. ### WHAT REPRODUCES IT: ONLY A REAL CLEAN INSTALL, and everything cheaper is excluded by measurement - PV-path idle proven reboots 0/50; EMULATED-path idle proven reboots 0/30 (every cycle a fresh domain, so device-model freshness alone is NOT sufficient); emulated-path 1 GB write rounds with and without an explicit flush, interleaved, 0/20; ACPI shutdown under VERIFIED in-flight load 0/6 load + 0/14 idle (2026-09-11 - this REFUTES the n=1 'specimen 2 hit on round 1' premise, and 8 of 15 load rounds voided because the liveness check measured total bytes, which plateaus once the writer wraps its filenames; fixed to a monotonic counter). Eight events total exist across ten captures in /home/user/rel/rev2/ and the dom0 tarballs; four of today's install rounds were clean. NO RATE SHOULD BE QUOTED: six of the eight events are three same-guest pairs, while one guest absorbed 8 clean installs, 50 reboots and 12 flush rounds before its single event - so this may be a state a guest image enters rather than a per-boot probability. ### FIX SPACE. CONCRETE, on the named half: bound StoreSubmitRequest's loop and return an error, so a xenstore request that never completes degrades to a recoverable failure instead of a permanent wedge. It is upstream code we ship UNMODIFIED, so patch-in-fork versus report-upstream is an OWNER decision. dom0-side knobs are RULED OUT by the owner (so cache=none/directsync and iothread changes are off the table). WITHDRAWN: 'install PV storage in stage 1' - the two stages exist only because our binaries are test-signed and Windows needs a reboot after `bcdedit /set testsigning on`, so stage 1 cannot install it, and the proposal would itself cost the extra reboot the owner ruled out. Note -RebootAtEnd is OFF by default and the PV drivers 'hand over at the qube's NEXT START', so after a default install the guest stays on emulated IDE for the rest of that session. ### NEXT ACTIONS, in order: (1) harvest a spinner's module bases - the arming instrument (mgmt/harness/arm-module-bases.sh, wired into clean-install-repeat.sh and flush-durability-ab.sh) proves itself but has never been harvested in eight events, which is the ONLY reason every spinner here is still a raw address; with it, tools/resolve-guest-rip.py turns the RIP into xenbus+offset and CONFIRMS or KILLS the StoreSubmitRequest identification outright. (2) Decide the xenbus loop bound (owner). (3) The device-model side needs stubdom thread stacks, which no capture has. ### RETIRED, do not resurrect without new evidence: the flush_area_mask <- hap_track_dirty_vram <- dm_op 'dirty-VRAM teardown deadlock' (those frames belong to OTHER DOMAINS - a different qube's stubdomain and two dom0 vCPUs - and the vfree is an ordinary per-call bitmap free); the emulated-VGA disable as trigger (it happens ~65 s into stage 2; the wedge hit at 2 s); msiexec as SOLE trigger (specimen 2 had no install); a dirty volume as CAUSE (it is a consequence of a hard stop); 'load across the transition' (route pooled p=0.81; load pooled 1/7 vs 0/64 after today's run, p~0.10); and 'no S5 reaches the toolstack' / 'the NMI produced no bugcheck', which are properties of a 06:23 event for which NO capture exists. [also: upstream, installer, test-framework] [verified 2026-09-11] ### SPECIMEN 4 (win10-cyc, d7245, captured live 2026-09-11 07:04Z) - PURE SPIN, NO BLOCKED vCPU, AND TWO RECURRING SITES OCCUPIED AT ONCE. Evidence: /home/user/rel/dom0-evidence-cyc/. Over a 5-min vcpu-list interval: vCPU0 +293.4 s (97.8% of a core, r--), vCPU3 +296.9 s (99.0%, r--), vCPU1 and vCPU2 +1.2 s each (0.4%, -b-, i.e. ORDINARY IDLE - not frozen). So there is NO frozen counter and NO ioreq-blocked vCPU anywhere: THE DEVICE-MODEL STALL IS NOT NECESSARY FOR THE SPIN, which finally decouples the two signatures and retires the 'Class A causes Class B' stage model outright. FINGERPRINT CONFIRMED A FOURTH TIME on vCPU0 across the recorded 30 s d1/d2 interval: RIP fffff80068cbfd00 both times, rcx 9fad9eff4e6b0000 BYTE-IDENTICAL (= EvtchnWait's Start, captured once before the loop), rbx 73b26eef -> 8e07545f (= Now, re-read every iteration), rdi fffff80066116180 ending 180 (= KPCR+0x180, the caller's own KPRCB), RFLAGS IF=1. TWO DISTINCT SITES, both now seen across specimens by their KASLR-invariant low 20 bits: SITE P = ...BFCFE/...BFD00, the 2-byte _mm_pause loop (specimens 2, 3, 4); SITE Q = ...CCD35/...CCD5C, a loop of at most 0x27 = 39 bytes (specimen 2's odd-one-out vCPU0, and specimen 4's vCPU3). Specimen 4 has ONE vCPU AT EACH, simultaneously, and site Q's registers differ (rcx=2, rbx a pointer, rdi still a KPRCB) so it is a different function. THAT IS PRECISELY WHAT StoreSubmitRequest PREDICTS: one CPU inside EvtchnWait's pause loop WHILE HOLDING Context->Lock, and another spinning to ACQUIRE that same lock - 39 bytes being about right for a spinlock-acquire loop. TIMING: the spinners hold ~41,000 s each against a ~16 h domain lifetime, which places spin onset around THREE HOURS AFTER the cycler finished, on a guest left sitting IDLE with nothing driving it. So this did not need an install, a shutdown, a load or a transition - it needed only time, which fits a periodic xenstore operation (a watch or poll) whose request never completes. MODULE BASES WERE ARMED on this guest (the cycler arms up front), and they persist on the Q: private volume, so once it is stopped they can be recovered OFFLINE and tools/resolve-guest-rip.py will confirm or kill the xenbus identification. That is the first time in nine events this has been possible. HARNESS DEFECT FOUND BY IT: the cycler reported rc=0 'did not reproduce' and left this wedged guest standing - its detection only fires when a round's probe fails, so a wedge arriving after the final probe passes through as a clean run. ### ARMING GAP IN THE ACCEPTANCE CELLS - FOUND 2026-09-14, NOT YET CLOSED (matrix.sh is held open by a running campaign, so the edit waits). The arming call sits at matrix.sh:773, in the POST-install path, immediately after 'guest came back with a session'. But WIN11-reinstall - the cell that has now stalled twice (2026-09-12 and again 2026-09-14 08:44) - dies DURING run_install, which is called at cell_reinstall line 1120, BEFORE that line is ever reached. So the cell most likely to wedge is the one cell guaranteed to be unarmed when it does. The guest IS reachable earlier: cell_reinstall establishes a session at line 1117 and cell_upgrade at 1138, both before their run_install. FIX: arm at the top of run_install, the single chokepoint for all four install-running cells (reinstall/upgrade/seeded/stock), where qrexec is already proven to answer. SEPARATELY: prime-run.sh arms NOTHING - it only NAMES arm-module-bases.sh in its DEADLINE message (0 invocations), so the clean-install cells record no bases at all. The recorder writes to Q:\Qubes Logs with a C:\ fallback, which is worth noting while the missing-Q: defect is open: no Q: means the table lands on the volume that gets wiped. [verified 2026-09-11] ### THE TWO SPIN SITES ARE NOW RESOLVED TO MODULES - first time in nine events (2026-09-11). Module bases were recorded on every boot of specimen 4 by mgmt/harness/arm-module-bases.sh (22 boots, 170 modules each, on the persistent Q: volume), the guest was killed and rebooted cleanly, the file was dumped over qrexec and tools/resolve-guest-rip.py scoped to the LAST PRE-REBOOT boot (2026-09-10T22:24:34Z guest clock): SITE P  0xfffff80068cbfd00 -> **ntoskrnl.exe + 0x2bfd00**; SITE Q  0xfffff8006c1ccd5c -> **xenbus.sys + 0x1cd5c**. THIS INVERTS THE ASSIGNMENT I GUESSED AN HOUR EARLIER and the corrected version fits the evidence BETTER. Site Q, the <=39-byte loop, is in XENBUS.SYS - the driver WE SHIP - and is EvtchnWait's _mm_pause() busy-spin, i.e. the CPU HOLDING Context->Lock. Site P, the 2-byte pause loop, is in NTOSKRNL - the WAITERS, spinning in Windows' own spinlock-acquire primitive for that lock. That re-reads the register fingerprint more naturally than my EvtchnWait attribution did: rcx byte-identical across 30 s and 1 h 38 min is the LOCK ADDRESS (invariant by construction), rbx the only varying register is a backoff/spin counter, rdi is the KPRCB a spinlock acquire naturally carries. Specimen 4 caught ONE vCPU AT EACH SITE SIMULTANEOUSLY - holder and waiter in one snapshot. So StoreSubmitRequest (store.c:898) holds Context->Lock across an unbounded EvtchnWait spin awaiting a xenstore response that never arrives, and every other CPU entering any of its ~10 xenstore entry points piles up in ntoskrnl's spinlock acquire. STILL OWED: module+RVA is not a function name. xenbus.sys+0x1cd5c needs that build's PDB (or a matched disassembly) to be pinned to EvtchnWait specifically - the MODULE attribution is solid, the FUNCTION is inference from the loop size, the register pattern and the source. ### AND THE CYCLER DID REPRODUCE IT - MY '0/6, PREMISE REFUTED' IS RETRACTED. From vCPU3's 41472.6 s at the 07:04:12Z sample, the spin began 2026-09-10T19:32:59Z; the guest had booted 19:24:34Z (the cycler's FINAL round) and the cycler finished 19:33:28Z - twenty-nine seconds later. It ran ~8 min normally, then wedged at the end of the last round. So ACPI SHUTDOWN UNDER VERIFIED IN-FLIGHT LOAD IS A WORKING REPRODUCER at roughly 1 in 15 rounds / 78 minutes, which is ~4x cheaper per event than clean installs - and the earlier conclusion that it 'did not reproduce' was an artefact of the harness scoring rounds only up to its last probe. THE DETECTION GAP IS THE LESSON: a harness that grades per round and stops must ALSO probe after the final round, or it reports clean while leaving a wedged guest standing. [verified 2026-09-11] ### SITE Q IS PINNED TO A FUNCTION, AND IT IS NOT EvtchnWait - my identification is REFUTED (2026-09-11). Method, so it can be repeated: xenbus.sys was extracted from the LOCAL installer.msi with 7z (the MSI streams out under opaque fil* names; it is the 185352-byte one) and its SHA256 68907F1F3D5F279E... MATCHES the live guest's copy byte for byte, so the disassembly is of exactly the code that wedged. objdump -d at ImageBase 0x140000000 + RVA 0x1cd5c gives a loop from 0x1cd30 to 0x1cd5c (0x2c bytes) that matches __HashTableBucketLock (xenbus/src/xenbus/hash_table.c:120) INSTRUCTION FOR INSTRUCTION: KeMemoryBarrier() -> `lock orl $0x0,(%rsp)`; `Lock = Bucket->Lock` -> `mov 0x1800(%rbx),%r8d`; `Writers = Lock & 1` -> `and $0x1,%ecx`; `Old = Readers << 1` -> `and $0xfffffffe,%eax`; the Readers/Writers increment -> `inc %ecx`; `InterlockedCompareExchange(...) != Old -> continue` -> `lock cmpxchg` + `jne 0x1cd30`; and `if (!Writer || Readers == 0) break` -> `test $0xfffffffe,%r8d; jne 0x1cd30`. So the spinner is a WRITER acquiring a hash-table bucket lock and spinning UNTIL READERS DRAIN. THERE IS NO `pause` INSTRUCTION AND NO KeQuerySystemTime CALL IN THAT LOOP, which kills the EvtchnWait/StoreSubmitRequest identification outright - the MODULE attribution (xenbus.sys) stands, the FUNCTION was wrong, and the register story I built on it (rcx = EvtchnWait's Start, rbx = Now) is withdrawn. THE FIRST LINE OF THAT FUNCTION IS THE IMPORTANT ONE: `KeRaiseIrql(HIGH_LEVEL, &Irql)`. It spins at HIGH_LEVEL - the highest IRQL, above the clock - which is a far better explanation of why NO WATCHDOG EVER FIRED (0x101 CLOCK_WATCHDOG_TIMEOUT, 0x133 DPC_WATCHDOG) and why the 2026-09-10 NMI produced no bugcheck, than the DISPATCH_LEVEL story it replaces. NOTE A PREDICTION OF MINE THAT FAILED AND WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSERTED: I expected RFLAGS.IF=0 at HIGH_LEVEL; the capture reads 0x297, i.e. IF=1. That is CONSISTENT, because Windows x64 implements IRQL through CR8/TPR and does not mask IF - so IF cannot be used to infer IRQL on this platform. ### RE-EXAMINED 2026-09-11 (owner did not buy 'an obscure upstream bug nobody noticed'): THE 'LEAKED READER' STEP IS WITHDRAWN - THE LOCK ITSELF IS THE DEFECT, AND THE CAPTURED REGISTERS PROVE IT. (1) WHICH LOCK: the function at xenbus+0x1cd00 raises to IRQL 15 (`mov $0xf,%cl`), spins on `0x1800(%rbx)`, then drains the list at `0x1808(%rbx)` into a stack-local list and frees each node with tag 'HASH' (0x48534148) - that is HashTableDpc, and 0x1800 = 256 buckets x 24 bytes = Table->Hidden. The spinner is a WRITER on the HIDDEN bucket, which is NEVER taken as a reader, so 'readers not draining' cannot be the state and no reader-path audit can find anything. (2) THE LOCK WORD IS IN THE CAPTURE: RIP 0x..cd5c is the instruction after `lock cmpxchg`; on failure cmpxchg loads the live word into eax. Specimen 4 (d7245v3, dom0-evidence-cyc/d2.txt) reads rax=1 r8=1 rcx=2 rdx=0 r9=2 r11=0xf; specimen d6955v0 (dom0-evidence/d1.txt, 2026-09-10, different domain, different KASLR base) reads the IDENTICAL rax=1 r8=1 rcx=2 rdx=0 r9=2 r11=0xf. So Hidden.Lock == 1: writer bit set, ZERO readers, and no CPU inside a Hidden critical section (the other spinner is in ntoskrnl; the Hidden sections are ~10 list instructions at IRQL 15 with no calls, so nothing can be parked inside one). A writer bit that nobody holds. (3) HOW THE CODE MANUFACTURES IT, verified in the shipped binary (SHA 68907f1f..., the guest's copy) not just the source: __HashTableBucketLock computes `Old = Readers<<1` (writer bit STRIPPED) and `New = (Readers<<1) | ((Writers+1)&1)` from ONE unlocked read of the word. Writer B spinning behind writer A reads Lock=1 -> Old=0, New=0 (=(1+1)&1). If A releases between B's read (0x1cd35) and B's cmpxchg (0x1cd54), the CAS 0->0 SUCCEEDS, B leaves the loop (r8d&~1==0) believing it owns the lock while the word is 0. B's unlock then computes New = (Lock&~1) | ((Writers-1)&1) = 0 | ((0-1)&1) = 1 (binary: `and $1,%ecx; dec %ecx; ... cmpxchg` at 0x1cdd0) and CAS 0->1 succeeds: a PHANTOM writer bit, held by nobody, permanently. The NEXT toucher of Hidden - any HashTableRemove (every EvtchnClose, every foreign-page unmap) or the DPC - spins at IRQL 15 forever, because with the bit set `Old` can never equal the word. That is exactly the observed word, rcx=(1&1)+1=2, rdx=0. (4) A SECOND, DETERMINISTIC DEFECT in the same function, not yet observed: a writer whose first CAS lands while Readers>0 sets the bit and then can never exit - every later iteration strips its own bit from Old and fails the CAS, including after the readers reach zero. Same fix. (5) WHY 'NOBODY NOTICED' IS EXPECTED, NOT SUSPICIOUS: the poison needs two writers on the same table's Hidden bucket on two CPUs within ~50 ns, i.e. two channel closes (or a close and the free-DPC) colliding. Writers are HashTableAdd/Remove = EvtchnOpen/Close and grant map/unmap. On XenServer these happen at device start/stop/migration only. On Qubes EVERY qrexec call and vchan teardown goes xeniface IOCTL -> XENBUS_EVTCHN(Close) -> HashTableRemove (xeniface/ioctl_evtchn.c:106), and an ACPI shutdown under load closes everything on all vCPUs at once - the reproducer that works. Once poisoned the wedge fires at the next close, minutes to hours later, on an otherwise idle guest (specimen 4). Upstream master (5c07da3, 2026-08-13, fetched today) still carries the identical function; the commits since our e76d03e touch neither hash_table.c nor its callers, and a single keyword search turned up no prior report - which is absence of a find, not evidence of absence. Not obscure: a textbook lost-update in a hand-rolled lock, reachable only under a churn pattern XenServer does not generate. (6) SITE P (ntoskrnl+0x2bfd00) REMAINS INFERENCE: a CPU parked at IRQL 15 never acknowledges an IPI, so any CPU issuing a TLB shootdown or generic IPI call spins in ntoskrnl waiting for it - consistent with rdi=KPRCB and 2 spinners + 2 idle in specimen 4, but the function is unresolved (needs the guest ntoskrnl.exe + its PDB; no win10 guest was running and pdbparse does not build here). It is a consequence, not a cause. (7) FIX: rewrite __HashTableBucketLock/Unlock so the writer bit is set by ONE CAS from a word with the bit clear and cleared by ONE CAS from a word with exactly the bit set, and readers spin while the bit is set - or replace the bucket lock with a plain KSPIN_LOCK at HIGH_LEVEL / upstream's AcquireHighLock (src/common/high.h). Deploy = patch in the fork AND report upstream with the register evidence (pvdrivers are outside QWT, so the report qualifies; owner approves the text). Reproducer for the defect-reintroduced proof: a user-mode evtchn open/close storm on 4 threads via xeniface should wedge a stock guest and not a patched one. ### GUEST-SIDE MITIGATION AND WHY IT GOT WORSE (2026-09-11). The xenbus.sys we ship is the PREBUILT QubesOS binary (qubes-repo/vmm-xen-windows-pvdrivers/bin, byte-identical to stock 4.2.2 - stage-qwt-repo.ps1 'we do not BUILD those'); only xenbus.inf is patched and the .cat regenerated. So stock QWT carries the defect too, and the first captured event (wedge-20260806-150900, win-idd-test) predates every churn source below. What changed is the RATE at which the race is offered: (a) vcpus=4 on every testbed guest including win10-base (Qubes default 2; on 1 vCPU the race is impossible, on 2 one pair can collide, on 4 six pairs); (b) qrexec churn - every qrexec connection in either direction makes the guest the vchan SERVER, i.e. one EvtchnOpen + one EvtchnClose + grants in the guest, and the harnesses probe over qrexec continuously while loading and shutting down (flush-durability-ab.sh, catch-specimen.sh: probes every 2-10 s, writer + liveness over qrexec, then ACPI shutdown closing every channel on all vCPUs at once); (c) the updates relay (guest/qubes-updates-relay.cs, since 2026-08-10) spawns one qrexec channel PER HTTP CONNECTION with an up-to-8-wide warm pool and paced closes - its own 2026-08-20 comment already names 'per-channel churn' as the suspected wedge trigger; (d) the notification bridge adds one call per toast (minor). Mitigation that actually removes the defect: build xenbus from our pinned e76d03e plus a one-file fix to hash_table.c in CI, exactly as pv-xenvif.yml already builds xenvif from source (build.ps1 + Inf2Cat + signtool, green on every release since 2026-09-09), and ship it in place of the prebuilt one; interfaces/revisions are unchanged because the source pin is unchanged. Mitigations that only lower the rate and are worth doing anyway: vcpus back to the Qubes default of 2 for anything not measuring SMP; a multiplexing relay (one long-lived channel, many HTTP streams) instead of a channel per connection; fewer qrexec probes per round in the harnesses. Nothing user-mode can close the DPC-versus-Remove window, so pacing closes cannot make it zero. ### FIXED IN THE FORK AND PROVEN BY A DEFECT-REINTRODUCED A/B (2026-09-11). The fix: patches/xenbus-hash-table-lock.patch rewrites __HashTableBucketLock/Unlock so the writer bit is taken by ONE compare-exchange from a word read with the bit clear, cleared by ONE compare-exchange 1->0, readers wait while the bit is set and a writer waits for readers after taking it. Built by .github/workflows/pv-xenbus.yml at the exact commit Qubes v4.2.0-1 pins (e76d03e, interfaces unchanged), monitor service neutralised as for the prebuilt INF, test-signed, staged as pv-drivers/xenbus by make-setup.ps1 and installed by Install-QwtImproved.ps1 with pnputil after the MSI (DriverVer 9.1.0.<run> outranks 9.1.0.0; pnputil reported 'reboot needed', stage 2 reboots). Verified at the binary level: the rebuilt xenbus.sys has no instance of the old derivation and the DPC's hidden-bucket loop is test-bit / pause / CAS / wait-readers. THE PROOF (mgmt/harness/evtchn-storm-ab.sh with tools/evtchnstorm, 4 threads opening+closing unbound event channels via xencontrol.dll, ~6-8k closes/s; run /home/user/rel/evtchn-storm-ab-20260911T083815Z): STOCK arm = win11-qwt clone, xenbus 9.1.0.0 on the guest: qrexec dead before the tool's first 5-second progress line, 300% of a core sustained, classified WEDGED at t+81 s. FIXED arm = quick-upgrade with release-package run 34579096533 (4.3.26, xenbus 9.1.0.439 read from the running guest): 2,972,459 open/close pairs in 300 s, 0 errors, qrexec answering afterwards, CLEAN. Same golden, same tool, same thread count, one variable. The first stock run was VOID for an instrument reason worth keeping: the tool's stdout is a qrexec pipe and was block-buffered, so its probe line arrived only at exit - now unbuffered. Also learned: a killed subject was restarted by a still-pending qrexec call (the queued-call trap), so the harness now stops the storm's process group and holds Halted before removing. STILL OPEN: (a) the fixed driver has passed quick-upgrade + the storm, NOT full acceptance - a release is a gate (matrix.sh) and none was run; (b) the upstream report (win-pv-devel / xenbits xenbus) with the register evidence and the patch awaits owner-approved text; (c) the rate reducers (vcpus 2 for non-SMP work, a multiplexing updates relay, fewer harness probes) are separate work and still worth doing; (d) site P in ntoskrnl remains unresolved and is now moot for the fix. FULL ACCEPTANCE, FIRST CAMPAIGN (2026-09-11, run 34579096533, on Opus): the package gate first refused the rebuilt xenbus's resource-only xenbus_monitor.dll (no code, no PDB - verify-release-package.sh now attributes it through its sibling .sys and gates xenbus like xenvif, negatives proven); then the matrix reported 66 passed / 6 failed, ALL SIX on one health check, prev_shutdown_orderly, 'could not determine shutdown cleanliness at step dirty-bit: The parameter is incorrect', identical on win10 and win11, clean/reinstall/upgrade. THAT IS AN INSTRUMENT DEFECT, NOT THE DRIVER: the 2026-09-10 20:59 rewrite of that check (abd9a7e) placed `$step = ...` INSIDE the Get-WinEvent @{ } filter literals, where PowerShell parses it as a hashtable entry keyed by the current $step - proven at the parser (@{ $step = 'x' } yields key 'dirty-bit' and leaves $step unchanged) - so Get-WinEvent rejected the polluted filter and the error was blamed on the dirty-bit step. The check had never run in a live campaign between that rewrite and this one. Fixed in guest/health-check.ps1 - and the SECOND campaign (run 34588339799) then failed the same six cells at the NEXT step, 'autochk-1001', same error text, which was a second, independent defect in the same rewrite: the Application-log query named ProviderName 'Microsoft-Windows-Wininit', and Get-WinEvent rejects that outright because that ETW provider is linked to the System log and its Diagnostic channel, not Application (measured with a probe on win11-nfy: the exact filter throws EventLogException, Id-only on Application returns count=0). Fixed by matching the source on the returned records instead. THE CHECK IS NOW PROVEN BOTH WAYS on the same guest: clean boot -> pass:true with all four signals read; hard qvm-kill mid-write then boot -> pass:false, unclean_events 41,6008, no error. That campaign also cleared BOTH feature tests, including notify-errors with WITNESS=RENDERED (the pre-existing session-0 failure is gone) and a CD-boot upgrade with two proven reboots; the only failure class left was this instrument. THIRD RE-RUN (run 34594988407, package built from 6123de3): matrix 71 passed / 1 failed, prev_shutdown_orderly PASS in all six graded cells, both feature tests PASS for the second consecutive campaign, no CD-boot deafness. The one failure, 'WIN11-appvm boot 2: notepad opened but dom0 got NO window', is a dom0-side capture result (six empty shot tars over 100 s while qrexec answered and the guest then took an orderly ACPI shutdown 58 s later; the owner was switching dom0 desktops, and the screenshot service returns only windows on the current one); first such event in 27 recorded AppVM boots. It withheld the acceptance record. 4.3.26 was ALREADY PUBLISHED 2026-09-10 from pre-fix bytes, so the fix (driver-repo-only, agent sha unchanged) needed its own version: bumped to 4.3.27 (agent e89b5be) and PUBLISHED 2026-09-11 as a TESTING release at the owner's explicit request (override and publish, for GWeck to field-test) - https://github.com/arkenoi/qubes-win-idd-driver/releases/tag/v4.3.27-agente89b5be. Full acceptance did NOT run to a recorded CLEAN verdict on the 4.3.27 bytes; cut-release has no acceptance-skip flag by design, so it was published manually via gh release create after replicating cut-release's other gates (green build 34605517156, verify-release-package 14/14, provenance/version/tag on the build commit) - NO acceptance record was fabricated. The 4.3.27 bytes differ from the campaign-validated 4.3.26 bytes only by the version number. A clean full campaign then RAN: 2026-09-11 on run 34610274411 (4.3.27+agent.e89b5be, the build carrying the hardcoded-'user' fix), matrix 72 passed / 0 failed across all eight cells with all six AppVM boots mapping windows, both feature tests PASS, verdict COMPLETE and RECORDED against the ISO - the first fully clean acceptance of the day, and the one that retires both instrument defects and the AppVM capture mis-grading. ### THE HARDCODED-'user' ACCOUNT DEPENDENCY (field bug, GWeck 2026-09-10) IS FIXED. A guest whose local account is not named 'user' could not update: Windows Update reported no network/no proxy and qrexec-wrapper logged authentication failures. Root cause was two coupled assumptions - dom0 sends the DEFAULT target user 'user', and exec.c string-matched that against the logged-on console user, discarding the valid token it already held on a mismatch and calling LogonUser('user','.','userpass') for a non-existent account, so every interactive service (the updates relay above all) failed to launch. The password is wrong on our own guests too (the installer sets 'qubes'); it only ever worked because the name matched and the token was reused. Fixes: patches/windows-utils-interactive-token.patch (an INTERACTIVE service runs as the actual logged-on console user whenever we hold its token; non-interactive and the not-logged-on LogonUser fallback untouched) applied in BOTH workflows that clone windows-utils - as a PATCH because CI clones that repo fresh at WINDOWS_UTILS_REF, so an edit to our upstream/ro mirror would have shipped nowhere; the relay and NotifyClient now pass SYSTEM for the stdio-only connect-back handler (qrexec-agent maps it to a NULL userName, removing the account dependency entirely); run-as-user.ps1 derives the principal from the live console session; qrexec-wrapper logs a run-as degradation loudly instead of silently. WHY ACCEPTANCE COULD NEVER HAVE CAUGHT IT, and the standing blind spot: every guest our installer builds is named 'user' (mgmt/autounattend*.xml), AND the testbed's dom0 policy runs our qrexec as NT AUTHORITY\SYSTEM, which takes the run-as-current-user branch and never reaches the logon path at all. mgmt/harness/nonuser-account-probe.sh exists to change the one variable the campaign cannot (it renames the interactive account and cold-boots onto it); it has NOT yet been run. ### BUILD-SYSTEM DEBT SURFACED BY THIS FIX: both workflows clone six or seven QubesOS repos at build time pinned to TAGS, not SHAs - a movable ref, so builds are not reproducible and upstream can change what we ship; we also keep read-only mirrors of the same repos that can silently drift from what CI builds. pv-xenvif already pins by SHA and documents why. Converting these to SHA-pinned submodules is owed work, deliberately deferred so it would not change the build inputs under this acceptance run. The AppVM check now asks the GUEST for the notepad window before grading and scores guest-present/dom0-empty as INVALID-INSTRUMENT. LEFT: one clean full campaign for the record (owner decides when - the campaigns photograph the dom0 desktop and locked the owner out for a day); cut-release; the upstream report (owner-approved text); the rate reducers (vcpus 2, multiplexing relay, fewer probes); and the documented upgrade-path exposure (an existing guest can wedge on the boot that installs the fix, before it binds). Everything else in all eight cells passed, including three cold boots of each AppVM cell and the xenbus_monitor-disabled assertions, on xenbus 9.1.0.439. AND THE SAME CAMPAIGN CAUGHT THE STOCK WEDGE LIVE: the notify-errors feature test's quick-upgrade booted win11-nfy (a win11-qwt clone, i.e. STOCK xenbus 9.1.0.0 until the upgrade lands) with the release CD; qrexec answered at 10:14:29 and 10:14:40, was dead by 10:15:40, and the domain then burned 198% of a core over a 30 s sample with qrexec dead - the 8/8 fingerprint, on the pre-fix driver, one minute into a boot, under nothing but the boot-time channel churn. Ninth event, first one caught by the field path during acceptance. The subject is discarded before the re-run (no dom0 register capture is possible from this qube, and two identical register specimens already exist). [verified 2026-09-11] ### THE BOUND IS IN AND THE PATH THAT BROKE WAS RE-RUN CLEAN [2026-09-12]. patches/xenbus-store-submit-bound.patch gives StoreSubmitRequest a 12-poll-period (60 s) limit, de-links the request BEFORE dropping Context->Lock (so a late response takes the existing SPURIOUS RESPONSE path), frees the response buffer and returns NULL - every caller already NULL-checks. Validated on release-package run 34688879873 (head 1396408, 4.3.29+agent.ff54fd9ca039), cells win11-clean + win11-reinstall: MATRIX 22 passed, 0 failed, both ACCEPT=PASS, three boots answered qrexec in 23/22/29 s. The decisive comparison is the SAME cell on the pre-fix package (run 34681406901): there the guest came back from the post-reinstall reboot and then went deaf across the next four minutes - DEV_CONS unreadable, the ITL key read taking 60 s, xenbus_monitor querying as '<no service> | <no state>' - and the accept reboot never returned (22 min, ACCEPT=FAIL 'guest did not answer qrexec after reboot'). This time every one of those reads answered in seconds and both boots returned. NOT YET EVIDENCE THAT THE BOUND FIRED: the Error("GIVING UP after 60 s") it emits goes to the hypervisor console, which needs a dom0 shell to read, so what is shown is the absence of the wedge on the path that produced it, n=1 per side against an intermittent defect - not a caught-and-survived stall. NEW SPECIMEN (recorded only - track stays PARKED): 2026-09-16 campaign rel-35104113559 (package 4.3.29+agent.0ca8a70b4eef on ec82353), cell WIN11-upgrade (win11-iqi 4.3.17 -> release ISO, subject win11-acc): qrexec unreachable within ~2 min of install.cmd starting, cpu_time ADVANCING (~198% of a core, every sample of the 900 s quiet-wait), screen=NOWINDOW, ACPI shutdown ignored 420 s; C:\qwt-improved-install.log holds ONLY the run marker (no PRECONDITION line - the installer died before its first write), MSI verbose log empty, stall.tar 0 bytes; module-base recorder WAS armed before the install (239 records) but no RIP was captured because dom0/11-wedge-forensics.sh is not installed in dom0 (owner's action, parked). The other five cells (win10 clean/reinstall/upgrade, win11 clean/reinstall) passed 70/70; acceptance record FAILED, cut-release refuses the ISO. Evidence /home/user/qwt-accept/rel-35104113559/campaign/matrix/WIN11-upgrade-*. Same fingerprint as specimens 1-8 (dead qrexec + spinning vCPU); NOT chased. [verified 2026-09-16] SPECIMEN 10 (recorded only, track PARKED): 2026-09-17 on the GERMAN Win11 25H2 TemplateVM win11de-gwt (26200.8037, package from CI run with a49e558, no netvm), verify2 run of the Qube-Manager-path fixes: the reboot-pending shim pass s1 completed, the guest took its own `shutdown /r /t 60`, the applying boot came up with a session at 08:28:15Z and dom0 read updates-available=1 at 08:28:23Z; then a PowerShell probe came back TRUNCATED at 08:30:23Z, qrexec died at 08:32:45Z (vchan connection timeout), the PV console printed a login prompt at 08:56:12Z and then answered NOTHING to the username or to any later attach (owner 2026-09-17: read that as the console PROCEDURE failing, not as the guest dying - see the P2 instrument entry right below); admin.vm.Stats cpu_usage 0 / cpu_usage_raw 0 while cpu_time advanced 503848 over 135 s (~373% of a core across 4 vCPUs) - the dead-qrexec + spinning signature, here with a console that died LATER than qrexec; ACPI shutdown refused (400 s + 150 s), qvm-kill x2 + qtest kill -> Halted at ~09:40Z. UBR still 26200.8037 (the applying boot had only the .NET package staged). Module bases NOT armed (not an acceptance cell), no RIP. Subject discarded and the arc re-run from a fresh unpark of the pretuesday park (never reused). Not chased. Evidence: the agent's incident note in scratchpad/gweck25h2/verify2/ (arc2.out, qcon-raw.out/err). [verified 2026-09-17]

[moved 2026-10-02: merged into THE STALL]
- P1 [MEASURED 2026-09-17, release 4.3.29.538 run 35171496552 - THE STALL RECURRED, AND THE HARNESS ATE THE SPECIMEN] WIN11-clean on win11-acc: install COMPLETE (installer rc=10, qrexec answering at t+498 s), then the guest went deaf DURING STAGE 2'S OWN POWER-OFF and never delivered S5 - state=Running for the next 32 min, qrexec dead 1083 s, per-window tar 0 bytes, cpu_time +35035 ms per 20 s (175% of a core). prime-run prints a line on any observed Halted at 5 s cadence and on_reboot=destroy makes a halt permanent, so the domain demonstrably never powered off: this is the SHUTDOWN path, not the boot path, and it matches the 2026-09-10 dom0 finding "no S5 ever reaches the toolstack". prime-run's own line "the job rebooted it after install" is an interpretation and was WRONG here - retract it wherever quoted. THE SECOND FAIL IS THE SAME EVENT: WIN11-reinstall took the same guest 1 s later (its entry state is a park the failed clean cell never made, so it could not have passed whatever the subject's health), could not ACPI-halt it in 660 s, killed it; WIN11-upgrade recloned win11-iqi over its volumes 4 s after that. No RIP, no module-base dump (none exists for this cell despite prime-run saying the recorder was armed), no console - THE SPECIMEN IS GONE. win11-acc is NOT contaminated (recloned, upgraded, graded PASS - by accident, not design). Harness half FIXED in 8553e1f: unpark checks the park exists BEFORE touching the subject; the dead-qrexec probe is reachable with zero pending calls (it was nested inside the pending-calls branch, unreachable in exactly its own case); cpu recorded before any last-resort kill; a latent `local vm=$1 ... ck="ckpt-$vm-installed"` set -u expansion bug split in park and unpark; self-test tools/tests/unpark-precheck-selftest.sh with both defect knobs seen to fail. STILL OPEN: (a) reclone destroys an H3.5 evidence guest - mgmt/harness/evidence-lock.sh is ORPHANED (no caller in matrix.sh / prime-run.sh / release-acceptance.sh) and the shape must be archive-then-release, not a bare refusal; (b) `_pr_cpu_state`'s MOVING branch prints "the P1 SPIN fingerprint" on the cpu delta ALONE - calibrated 2026-09-17 against a healthy qrexec-answering win11-acc at 262-327% of a core, i.e. HIGHER than the stalled guest's 175%, so the delta is the weakest leg and the wording must say the conjunction (Running + qrexec dead + empty tar + sustained >100%); (c) mgmt/fixtures/<vm>.json is not written by reclone and not refreshed on a failed prime, so a subject's provenance receipt can describe a guest that no longer exists; (d) the direct measurement of the stall is still missing - no per-vCPU sample (dom0 `xl vcpu-list`, owner-only) and no whole-domain cpu_time delta was taken during the 660 s window (the latter is now taken by 8553e1f). Forensics and Jev rubric grades: scratchpad/release/verify-20260917T195851Z-1365809/forensics/ (r1-r5). RECURRED AGAIN 2026-09-17 21:31Z, ON A THIRD GUEST AND A THIRD OS IMAGE - win11de-gwt, the German 25H2 TemplateVM, no install running, netvm='' - AND THIS SPECIMEN IS ALIVE AND UNKILLED, which is the first time item (d) is actually reachable. Full conjunction measured 22:04-22:10Z: state Running since 21:31:10Z (FLAGS TrU-----), qrexec DEAD (two bounded probes, 30 s qvm-run and 25 s qrexec-client-vm, both zero bytes and killed by their own timeout), w_screen NOWINDOW with a 0-byte per-window tar, and ACPI shutdown REFUSED TWICE (qvm-shutdown --wait rc=1 "Failed to shut down", the second attempt burning 60.4 s) - i.e. no S5, exactly as in dom0's 2026-09-10 finding. Jev graded it state_class=p1-stall confidence 1.00 and will_self_resolve noul=0.09 (scratchpad/jev-win11de-gwt-state.json). NOT killed - the owner's standing order and the fact that it is the GWeck template; its pre-update park ckpt-win11de-gwt-pretuesday is Halted and safe. SO THE STALL IS NOT INSTALL-SPECIFIC AND NOT WIN11-ACC-SPECIFIC: it has now been seen on win11-acc (post-install power-off), on the 2026-09-10 clean-install subject, and on an idle German template that was merely shut down. CONSEQUENCE FOR THE RIG: a stalled guest whose name matches ^(win(10|11)|prime-) BLOCKS EVERY CAMPAIGN CELL - prime-run.sh:106 refuses to start, and matrix.sh's _halt_other_windows first burns ~10 min per cell on an ACPI shutdown the guest cannot service - so this one wedge cost the 2026-09-17 evening's six-cell non-visual acceptance entirely (package built and verified, campaign never launched). [verified 2026-09-18]

## Closed entries moved out of the CURRENT STATE head 2026-10-21

Verbatim, in the order they stood in findings/issues.md. They were retired from the live
register because they are fixed; the text is kept here in full.

- OSCILLATIONJUDGEDBYCOUNTNOTIDENTITY [P2 gate, mgmt/harness/wu-e2e.sh oscillation check; **TOOL BUILT AND TESTED, NOT YET WIRED**] [verified 2026-10-20] **A COUNT CANNOT TELL AN OSCILLATION FROM A PUBLICATION.** The check asserts dom0's updates-available did not change across the scan, which conflates the SAME offer coming back after a pass resolved it - our defect - with Microsoft publishing a NEW one while the scan ran, which is not. Both move the number from N to N+1. Owner 2026-10-20: *"can we sanely handle the situation when update was published AFTER the campaign start and not fail there?"* KB2267602 is the case that matters: Defender Security Intelligence is republished several times a day under a NEW UpdateID each time, so by count it reads as one thing oscillating for ever - which is also the driver behind [[WUQUIETDEFINITIONS]]. **THE DATA WAS ALREADY THERE:** qubes-windows-update.ps1:919-922 captures each offer's UpdateID and RevisionNumber off the live COM object, kept precisely so a scan can tell "the same offer the last pass resolved" from "a genuinely new one" (ADR section 6, no title parsing, no locale dependence). `tools/wu-oscillation-verdict.py` now decides by identity: an UpdateID absent before the scan is NEW-PUBLICATION (rc=0, the run does not fail); an UpdateID the pass reported RESOLVED and still present is OSCILLATION (rc=1); a higher RevisionNumber on the same id is a republication, not a return; an unreadable or identity-less side is rc=2 input error, never a quiet pass. 8 assertions, seen to fail - identical sets give rc=0 unresolved and rc=1 resolved, so the verdict tracks the fact rather than the number. **SCOPE, measured, so it is not overstated:** the existing check brackets only the SCAN (`dom0_before_scan` is taken after the pass), so a publication during the long install pass never tripped it - run 3 round 3 went to '1' during its pass and still reported `oscillation OK`. The exposure is a publication inside the ~1 minute scan window. NOT WIRED INTO wu-e2e.sh YET, deliberately: it makes the gate more permissive, and an untested permissive change in the last gate before the record risks a false PASS, which is worse than the false FAIL it prevents. It lands after the campaign now running on build 39476792836.
- CROPTESTHARDCODESTHELOGPATH [P2 instrument, mgmt/harness/crop-before-map.sh:167; **CLOSED 2026-10-20**] [verified 2026-10-19] **A TEST THAT HARD-CODES Q: CANNOT SEE LOGS THE PRODUCT WAS TOLD TO WRITE ELSEWHERE.** crop-before-map returned INSTRUMENT rc=2 on run 39225775322 with `no gui-agent log on the guest carries a RUNNING agent's pid - either no agent is running or it has written nothing, so there is nothing to grade`, and line 167 is `Get-ChildItem "Q:\Qubes Logs\gui-agent-*.log"` - a literal path. LogDir is configurable, this campaign ran with LOGVOL_BIND=1, and 792a2b2e deliberately stopped the installer forcing LogDir back to Q: when a value is already set, so a guest whose LogDir names anywhere else is invisible to this test by construction. The failure text blames the guest ("either no agent is running or it has written nothing") for what is the test looking in one place. It is the same SHAPE as the four blind cells - a consumer naming a path instead of asking where the logs are - though it is not established that win11-nfy's LogDir had in fact moved; that needs a read of the golden's registry. The fix is the same either way: read LogDir (per-module key first, then the root, as log.c:358 and CfgOpenKey do) instead of naming a drive. Related: [[PINNEDLETTERISNOTEXCLUSIVE]].
  **RECONCILED 2026-10-20 against acceptance run 3 on build 39225775322 (the third campaign of this release, clean work dir).** It resolves LogDir the product's way (per-module key, then root, then Q: as the historical default) and names what it searched when it finds nothing. PASS in this campaign and the previous one, after INSTRUMENT rc=2 before the fix. 82cb8fac.
- PINNEDLETTERISNOTEXCLUSIVE [P1 instrument, the ROOT CAUSE of SWEEPBLINDWHENLOGDIRISNOTLIVE; mgmt/harness/logvol-bind.ps1 Pin-LogVol + every LogDir consumer; **HARNESS SIDE CLOSED; cause REPRODUCED ON DEMAND**] [verified 2026-10-20] **ANOTHER REMOVABLE VOLUME TAKES THE PINNED LETTER AND LogDir KEEPS NAMING IT.** Reproduced on win10-acc by A/B/A, 2026-10-20, with no clean install needed. (A) one removable device, the baseline: `Z: root=Z:\`, `root LogDir = Z:\qwt-logs\win10-acc`, that directory holds 123 files whose newest were written in the probe's own minute. (B) a second 64 MB FAT stick added to qemu-extra-args on the same nec-usb-xhci, nothing else changed: `Z:` still exists and LogDir still reads `Z:\qwt-logs\win10-acc`, but **`Z:\qwt-logs\win10-acc` DOES NOT EXIST** and every module has fallen back to `Q:\Qubes Logs` (fresh writes at 14:57:10-14:57:23). `Get-Volume` names the swap outright: `D: label='DIAG' 252MB top-level: qwt-logs, LOGVOL.TAG` and `Z: label='DUMMYSTK' 64MB top-level: <empty>` - the dummy holds the pinned letter and the log volume was pushed to D:. (A again) dummy removed, args restored: `Z:` is the log volume once more, 123 files, newest written that minute. **THIS IS THE CLEAN-CELL SYMPTOM EXACTLY** - LogDir correct, the volume present, the named directory absent, the guest logging to Q: - and it is why four cells swept 0 QWT lines on run 39225775322 while their logs sat on the image. **IT ALSO CORRECTS A RECORDED REFUTATION:** h3, "the pinned letter dangling when the USB device set changes", was recorded REFUTED 0.87 on the run-8 pack, and the pin succeeding (`letter=pinned`, `found=D:` then `found=E:`) was read as the letter therefore holding. The pin fixes where OUR volume goes; it does not make the letter EXCLUSIVE, and a second removable volume present at boot can own it first. The clean cell is exactly where a second removable volume exists - prime-run attaches the answer stick beside the DIAG volume. **THE FIX MUST NOT BE ANOTHER LETTER.** A path that names a drive letter is a path another volume can claim; the binding has to be to the volume itself (label/serial/GUID at module start, or a mount point under a fixed path), or the harness must verify after every boot that LogDir resolves to the DIAG volume - by label, not by existence - and re-bind when it does not. Related: [[SWEEPBLINDWHENLOGDIRISNOTLIVE]].
  **RECONCILED 2026-10-20 against acceptance run 3 on build 39225775322 (the third campaign of this release, clean work dir).** The harness no longer depends on the letter holding: it re-asserts the bind on the boot grading reads and proves the path end to end at the bind and again before the pull, so a stolen letter is corrected rather than survived. NO PRODUCT CHANGE WAS MADE, deliberately - owner 2026-10-20: "the PRODUCT never needs the volume, it is purely test environment feature", so LogDir stays a plain path the product already understands and keeping it true is the harness's job.
- QREXECOUTPUTLOSTWHENTHECALLERHANGSUP [P2, and the gate condition that failed two cells; guest/qrexec-wrapper + mgmt/harness callers; **HALF CLOSED**] [verified 2026-10-19] **THE ERROR LINES THAT BREACHED error_lines_undeclared ARE OUR OWN CODE SAYING OUTPUT WAS LOST, AND THEY LAND ON A HARNESS CALL TO THE SECOND.** win10-upgrade breached with 2 and win10-reinstall with 1. The raw lines, guest-local (= UTC+03:00): `[20261019.125419.110-6680:5388-E] VchanSendMessage: vchan already closed with 2 byte(s) of output data (msg 0x191) still to send - that output does not reach the peer` and `[20261019.125419.183-6680:3148-E] wmain: an output pump is still running at exit - a data error: output this call produced may not have reached the peer`. 12:54:19 local is **09:54:19 UTC**, which is the campaign log's own `[09:54:19] schtasks: <no output>` - `mgmt/harness/arm-module-bases.sh:160` runs `schtasks /create /tn QwtModuleBases ...` through `qtest run`, captures the output into `$armed`, receives NOTHING, and prints `<no output>`. `schtasks /create /f` prints a SUCCESS line, so what the harness recorded as unremarkable is the guest telling us the output never arrived. The third line, a W at `12:51:09` = 09:51:09 UTC, says `the peer hung up first, a data error or a stop` and falls inside the harness's own polling window (`[09:51:04] WIN10-upgrade: t+21s ... (continuing in 12 seconds)`). So the family is the SAME as the already-declared QGAVCHANFAIL: the caller hangs up and the guest-side wrapper is still pumping. **NOT DECLARED HERE, DELIBERATELY.** The declaration of QGAVCHANFAIL was earned - four measured attempts to remove the cause first, then Jev at 0.94 - and the owner's rule on this metric is that an error line our code wrote is a defect to fix at its cause, never to hide. The cause here is not yet attempted: whether `qtest run`'s client closes the vchan before the guest's pump drains, or the wrapper exits without draining it. Fixing it also matters beyond the gate - a harness probe that silently loses output can make any check that reads output lie, which is what `<no output>` did here. Related: [[a-verifier-that-reads-its-own-bookkeeping-is-inert]]. **CAUSE ATTEMPTED 2026-10-20, NOT REPRODUCED - so it stays OPEN and stays UNDECLARED.** On win10-acc: 20 consecutive `qtest run "cmd /c echo MARK<i>"` calls returned their output 20/20, and the campaign's own schtasks line - run verbatim, including the `/ru SYSTEM /sc onstart /rl HIGHEST /f` form - returned `SUCCESS: The scheduled task "QwtModuleBasesPROBE" has successfully been created.` on stdout with stderr EMPTY (the probe task was deleted afterwards and a query confirms it is gone). So the loss is timing-dependent, not a property of the call: the campaign hit it 18 s after `session up`, on a guest still settling from boot. **A SEPARATE DEFECT FOUND WHILE LOOKING:** `arm-module-bases.sh:160` ends the capture with `2>/dev/null`, so a schtasks that FAILS prints its reason to a discarded stream and the harness reports the same `<no output>` it reports for a lost one. Those are different events and the log cannot tell them apart. The code-level mechanism is now named: `VchanSendMessage` finding the vchan already closed makes `HandleDataMessage` fail, which sets `EXIT_REASON_DATA_ERROR` (qrexec-wrapper.c:1737), and the exit path at :2133 logs at ERROR for every reason that is not PEER_CLOSED, CHILD_DRAINED or DRAIN_EXPIRED. A peer that hung up is the ORDINARY end when the read loop notices it and a DATA ERROR when the send path notices it first - the same event, two severities, decided by a race. That asymmetry is the thing to fix, and :746-:759 already demote two other cases of it.
  **RECONCILED 2026-10-20 against acceptance run 3 on build 39225775322 (the third campaign of this release, clean work dir).** The DOUBLE REPORT is fixed (core-agent 179bc52, in build 39476792836): a send that finds the vchan closed latched EXIT_REASON_DATA_ERROR, so the exit path logged a second ERROR about a teardown the closed-vchan line had already named with its byte count. The demotion is conditional on the loss having been reported, so a loss nothing else named is still loud. THE LOSS ITSELF REMAINS OPEN and was NOT reproduced: 20/20 short qtest calls returned their output and the campaign's own schtasks line returned its SUCCESS message with stderr empty. It stays undeclared.
- SWEEPBLINDWHENLOGDIRISNOTLIVE [P1 instrument, mgmt/harness/matrix.sh + the guest-side log collector; **CLOSED 2026-10-20**] [verified 2026-10-19] **FOUR OF EIGHT CELLS WERE GRADED BLIND WHILE THEIR LOGS SAT ON THE VOLUME.** win10-clean, win11-clean, win10-appvm and win11-appvm all failed INVALID-SWEEP-BLIND with "0 QWT line(s)". The collector says why, in its own meta.json: `logdir.pathb64 = Z:\qwt-logs\<vm>` with **`exists: "0"`** - at sweep time the guest could not see that directory, so the collector fell back to `Q:\Qubes Logs`, which holds only install-era files (12 of them, no QWT-format line), and the sweep reported CLEAN-but-blind. The two cells that passed carry the same path with `exists: "1"`. **THE LOGS ARE NOT LOST.** The cell-end pull of the very same volume returns them: `LOGVOL-PULLED files=25`, and the image holds a full set - gui-agent-20261019.log (14773 b), qrexec-wrapper (64977 b), get-image-rgba, qrexec-agent, qubesdb-daemon, bridge.log, etw-proxy.log, module-bases.txt. So the grading is blind only because the SWEEP runs before the PULL and reads the guest live; the evidence it needs is already on an image this qube can read with mtools and no guest at all. The bind is not at fault and must not be blamed again: all four cells' receipts read `LOGVOL-BIND ok ... drive=Z: found=D: letter=pinned old=Q:\Qubes Logs new=Z:\qwt-logs\<vm> copied=21 safeflush=1 modules=15`. The four blind cells are exactly the ones that PARK (checkpoint) or are AppVMs; the two live ones neither park nor re-create. **THE PARK HYPOTHESIS THAT FOLLOWED FROM THAT IS REFUTED, by direct measurement on win10-acc 2026-10-20.** A plain start of the campaign's leftover subject shows the whole chain intact: `Z: root=Z:\`, root `LogDir = Z:\qwt-logs\win10-acc`, a per-module key naming the same path for all 13 modules INCLUDING gui-agent, the directory holding 123 files whose newest three were written in the same minute as the probe, and gui-agent alive. Then halt -> `checkpoint.sh park` -> start, which is the clean cell's own sequence: the chain is STILL intact and the guest writes to Z: on that boot (newest files at 14:46:08-14:46:10, the boot that followed the park). So the park does not break it, and neither do the bind, the pin, the root key, the per-module keys or the restore. Those are eliminated and must not be re-chased - together with h1 enumeration (refuted 0.96), h2 cross-VM restore (0.79), h3 dangling pin (0.87) and h4 the installer re-seeding LogDir (fixed by d1e2e348 + 792a2b2e, and this campaign proves the fix did not resolve the blindness). **WHAT IS LEFT** is what the clean cell has and this probe did not: a FRESH prime-run install, and a DEVICE-SET CHANGE across the bind - `clear_prime_leftovers` rewrites qemu-extra-args at 09:31:10 while the running guest keeps the old set, the bind at 09:31+ therefore sees the volume at `found=D:` with both priming sticks present, and the next boot has only our device. Settling it needs the clean cell reproduced, i.e. prime-run, which is a heavy path and needs its own Jev call; the probe above is as far as a leftover subject can go.
  **RECONCILED 2026-10-20 against acceptance run 3 on build 39225775322 (the third campaign of this release, clean work dir).** Every cell that was blind now produces guest evidence: win10-clean 2589 QWT lines, win11-clean 2374, win10-appvm CLEAN 253, win10-reinstall CLEAN 1610 - against 0 in both earlier campaigns. 18 `log path PROVEN end to end` receipts, no cell graded on an unproven path. Closed by the post-boot re-assert (30f3347b), the per-boot appvm bind (bde8ddfd) and the round-trip proof at bind and again before the pull (bde8ddfd, c996ee9b).
- APPVMLOGDIRNAMESTHETEMPLATE [P2 instrument, mgmt/harness/matrix.sh appvm cell; **CLOSED 2026-10-20**] [verified 2026-10-19] **THE APPVM SWEPT A DIRECTORY THAT BELONGS TO ITS TEMPLATE.** win10-appvm and win11-appvm had their OWN volume created and attached (`LOGVOL-ATTACHED vm=win10-app id=win10-app image=.../logs-win10-app.img loop19 frontend=xvdj`), but the LogDir their collector read is `Z:\qwt-logs\win10-acc` - the TEMPLATE's directory, not `win10-app`. The AppVM inherits the template's registry and the cell never re-binds it to its own volume, so the path it looks in cannot exist on the volume it was given. Related: [[appvm-private-seeds-at-creation]].
  **RECONCILED 2026-10-20 against acceptance run 3 on build 39225775322 (the third campaign of this release, clean work dir).** The cause was not the missing bind but a bind that could not survive: an AppVM's ROOT volume is volatile, so the boot-1 bind was discarded by boot 2 and the guest reverted to the template's LogDir (receipts: bound `id=win10-app` 13:27:25, collector read `win10-acc exists=0` 13:30:32). Binding on EVERY boot fixed it: three binds, three proofs, `win10-appvm: guest logs swept CLEAN (21 file(s), 253 QWT line(s))`.
- SWEEPSAYSNOTATTACHEDWHENITWAS [P2 instrument, mgmt/harness/matrix.sh:325; **CLOSED 2026-10-20**] [verified 2026-10-19] **A FALSE REASON ON A REAL FAILURE.** grade_cell_logsweep prints "no log volume was attached for this cell" when `LOGVOL_IMG` is empty, and the appvm cells printed it although their volume WAS attached minutes earlier. The message describes the variable, not the rig: the pull at the end of the previous cell clears `LOGVOL_IMG`/`LOGVOL_VM`. A wrong reason on a real failure sent this investigation at the bind, which was working. The message must state what was actually measured - volume attached or not, LogDir path, and whether it existed.
  **RECONCILED 2026-10-20 against acceptance run 3 on build 39225775322 (the third campaign of this release, clean work dir).** The clause now reads the collector's own measurement (meta.json logdir path + exists) instead of a shell variable the previous cell's pull cleared, and quotes the path. 783ca418, seen to fail both ways.
