- GRADEROWSDROPPEDBEFORETHEREPORT [P2 instrument, mgmt/harness/toast-hold-test.sh; FIXED 2026-10-19 7d41676f] [UNVERIFIED on the rig - selftest only, seen to fail three ways; toast-hold-test.sh is not in the release campaign] **THE REPORT DELETED THE ROWS IT TOLD YOU TO READ.** The grade-row echo at toast-hold-test.sh:587 is an anchored ERE that listed `CLOCK` but not `CLOCK-REF`, and the anchor wants the `|` straight after the label, so all four CLOCK-REF rows were dropped - three of them the INSTRUMENT rows meaning "this run has no usable clock reference", while the GRADED row the filter KEPT says, in the grader's own words at toast-hold-grade.py:994, "see CLOCK-REF". NS-SIZE and REMAP-DUMP went the same way; NS-SIZE is the owner's rule of 2026-08-27 (a non-seamless desktop resized to cover the host) with a regression measured 2026-10-06. Both emit FAIL rows and FAIL rows drive the verdict (toast-hold-grade.py:1033), so no run was graded WRONG by this - only the reason was invisible, which is the part an operator reads. The check added to tools/tests/toasthold-grade-clock-selftest.sh enumerates every label the grader can emit (37 row() calls with a literal, 5 with the fire's own label from EXPECT's keys, 27 distinct) and tests each against the harness's OWN filters, IN THE GREP MODE THE HARNESS USES THEM IN - the first version read them all as EREs, and `^GRADED|` is a literal pipe to plain grep but "GRADED or EMPTY" to grep -E, so it matched every label and the check PASSED over the defect it names. A ZZZ-NOSUCH probe now makes an inert check report itself inert.
- AUTOPLAYTOASTTIMESTHESHELL [measurement + THREE corrections of mine; the LOGVOL_BIND mechanism is REFUTED - by the run-8 pack (LOGVOLBINDNOTMOUNTED, 2026-10-19), NOT by the stick argument below, which is wrong] [verified 2026-10-19] (from the campaign captures - the stick's own contents and the agent's own timestamps; no rig action needed) **I TWICE EXPLAINED THIS WRONG BEFORE CHECKING.** The owner saw "Select to choose what happens to removable drives" during the 4.3.37 acceptance and asked what it had to do with "log volume not bound". I answered that it timed our log stick enumerating 35 s after the agent's LogInit, and that this corroborated LOGVOL_BIND=0. Both halves were wrong.
  **CORRECTION 3 (2026-10-19; Jev my_claim_wrong 0.85): THE "REFUTED BY OUR OWN STICK" PARAGRAPH BELOW SUBTRACTS TWO CLOCKS.** `02:00:56 quick-upgrade[win11de-tup]: [INFO] QWT logs bound to the log volume` is the harness's UTC stamp; `gui-agent-20261019-050014-4652.log` is the guest's LOCAL name, and that run's sweep declares `tz=+03:00` (`~/qwt-accept/rel-39175573529/template-update.d/qu/win11de-tup-20261019-015900`). So the file was created at 02:00:14Z, 42 s BEFORE the bind, and the bind's own receipt reads `LOGVOL-BIND ok mode=registry id=win11de-tup drive=Z: found=D: letter=pinned old=Q:\Qubes Logs new=Z:\qwt-logs\win11de-tup copied=83 safeflush=1`: the file was one of the 83 COPIED onto the stick, not written there by an agent that started with LogDir bound. "The volume was attached at 01:59:15, three hours before the boot" is the same subtraction: `01:59:15 ... log volume attached` (UTC) preceded the agent's LogInit (05:00:14 local = 02:00:14Z) by 59 s, which still says the device was present at power-on and proves nothing about a bound LogDir surviving a reboot. The h2 paragraph below ("a cross-VM volume restore ... rolls the binder's registry value back") was judged **REFUTED 0.79** on the run-8 pack, and the next step it motivates is superseded by the one measurement that settles h4 - `reg query "HKLM\Software\Invisible Things Lab\Qubes Tools" /v LogDir` on a bound guest after an install - which d1e2e348 took the same day and read `Q:\Qubes Logs` (see LOGVOLBINDNOTMOUNTED, 2026-10-19). The clock trap itself is closed at the source - every host-side stamp is UTC and every campaign log declares its zone first (CAMPAIGNLOGTZSPLIT, widened 2026-10-19).
  (1) WHICH DEVICE IS NOT ESTABLISHED (owner: "Are you SURE at this point it stays mounted?"). The ETW signal names no drive; no harness action is logged at 05:00:49; no device-arrival record was collected for that cell; and the agent log ON the volume carries lines from 05:00:14, BEFORE the toast, so that content was copied there rather than written live. The release ISO is attached as removable on some cells too.
  (2) THE TOAST TIMES THE SHELL, NOT THE DEVICE (owner: "if it was attached before the boot, it should had enumerated before gui, no?"). AutoPlay is a shell feature - the prompt cannot be shown until a session with Explorer exists - and the same boot gives: secure desktop ENTERED 05:00:14.602, LEFT (logon) **05:00:31.546**, first WGC frame with windows appearing **05:00:40.649**, AutoPlay **05:00:49**. Eighteen seconds after logon, nine after the shell went live. The volume was attached at 01:59:15, three hours before the boot, so a device present at power-on enumerates during boot and the toast cannot be its arrival.
  **CONSEQUENCE, and it is the useful part:** the reason matrix.sh gives for declining the bind - "a USB volume behind an emulated xHCI controller enumerates on its own schedule, so a module that starts first opens no log at all - and never retries" - has no measurement behind it either. What IS measured there is the OUTCOME (nothing written to the volume after a park/restore; WIN10-clean's battery passed before the bind was wired in and failed in every campaign after), not the mechanism. If the volume actually enumerates during boot, the bind's failure has a different cause - and the bind is what would have given host-readable QWT logs for win11-reinstall, which went silent with nothing to read.
  **[WRONG - see CORRECTION 3] AND THE MECHANISM IS NOT MERELY UNPROVEN, IT IS REFUTED - BY OUR OWN STICK** (owner: "then why it generally worked? if it was like this none of our boots would contain proper logs on stick"). `quick-upgrade.sh:433` calls `logvol_bind_guest` UNCONDITIONALLY, and that run logged `02:00:56 QWT logs bound to the log volume`. The guest rebooted at ~04:59:34, and the stick carries `gui-agent-20261019-050014-4652.log` whose FIRST line is `LogInit: Log started` at 05:00:14.041, alongside logs from 2026-10-15. So a bound LogDir survived a reboot and the volume WAS present and writable at the agent's LogInit. A removable volume attached before power-on enumerates during boot, exactly as the owner said.
  **[the restore cause below is h2, REFUTED 0.79 - see CORRECTION 3] WHAT IS LEFT OF THE ORIGINAL MEASUREMENT** is narrow and does not mention enumeration: after a cell's **park/restore** nothing new reached the volume, and WIN10-clean's accept battery passed before the instrument was wired in and failed in every campaign after. A cross-VM volume restore puts back a system volume from a checkpoint taken BEFORE the binder ran, which rolls the binder's registry value back - and the recorded symptoms are what a LogDir pointing somewhere unreadable looks like (`any_agent_log_held_open: false` with the agent alive, the IDD check finding no line to read, the console-window check INVALID-INSTRUMENT). That is a plausible, mundane cause with nothing to do with USB timing.
  **NOT CHANGED NOW, DELIBERATELY.** LOGVOL_BIND stays 0 for this release run: flipping the acceptance instrument on the strength of a new hypothesis, immediately before a release campaign, is the thing that produced the recorded history in the first place. The next step is specific - bind AFTER any park/restore (or decline to bind only on the cells that get restored) and re-measure WIN10-clean's battery (superseded 2026-10-19: h2 is refuted; the h4 `reg query` was taken and read `Q:\Qubes Logs` - LOGVOLBINDNOTMOUNTED) - and it is worth doing, because a bound volume is what would have given win11-reinstall host-readable logs when it went silent.
- LOSTLOGSTARTEDSLIDTHEUPTIME [P2 instrument, tools/log-sweep.py; FIXED 2026-10-19] [UNVERIFIED on the rig - pending the re-run] **A LOST LOG LINE BREACHED A GATE ON A GUEST WHOSE OWN LOG SAYS EVERY EXIT WAS ORDERLY.** win10-clean failed the 4.3.37 acceptance on `agent_instances_per_boot = 2 (max 1)`. An unclean guest shutdown left **7976 NUL bytes** inside `gui-agent-20261019.log` (15191 across five files on that cell; ZERO on the other seven), swallowing pid 3748's `Log started`. The normal order is `Log started` -> `System uptime` -> `process ID`, so that uptime landed on the PREVIOUS record and only the pid line opened a new one: record 0 = {3652, uptime 12.421 - 3748's}, record 1 = {3748, uptime None}. Boot attribution reads the init's uptime, so 3652 and 3748 were both billed to boot 4. FIX: a SECOND uptime in one record opens a new record there - the uptime is the only init line that cannot be confused between instances, which this file's own comment already argued. Verified on the failing capture: instances now (3652,b2) (3748,b3) (3808,b4), per_boot 1, **breaches []**. T58 + the `nulhole` fixture built from that capture's bytes, seen to fail against HEAD (per_boot=2, both in one boot).
- NULHOLEWASABSORBEDSILENTLY [P2 instrument + an open question about the guest; instrument FIXED 2026-10-19, CAUSE OPEN] [UNVERIFIED on the rig] **LOST DATA READ AS A COMPLETE RECORD.** The sweep parsed around the NUL hole above without a word; the collector's `partial` flag does not cover it (the file is whole and under the cap, with a hole in the middle). A NUL run of 512 bytes or more is now a `nul-hole` data problem, which raises the INVALID-data class - "this cell was not graded" - rather than a product verdict. **OPEN: why win10-clean lost 15 KB across five files and no other cell lost any.** The agent exited cleanly at 02:39:04 (`QGAEXIT ... an expected exit, not a failure`) and the watchdog reached preshutdown, then the guest was back at 02:39:19 - fifteen seconds, which is a reset rather than a shutdown-and-boot, and a reset is what loses the Q: volume's unflushed tail. **PARTLY ADDRESSED 2026-10-19:** the battery's boot-path reboot waited up to 300 s for `Halted` with TWO exits and no marker - a guest that did not halt in the bound fell out of the loop and was STARTED ANYWAY, which is exactly how a domain still writing loses its unflushed tail. `tools/accept-clean.sh` now latches the marker, names the bound, logs how long the halt took, and FAILS rather than starting on top of a running domain. Whether that loop is what lost win10-clean's 15 KB is still not established - precisely because it never said which exit it took - so the next occurrence will say.
- RELAYBOUNDTHEPIDNOTTHEPORT [P1 product, guest/qubes-updates-relay.cs + guest/install-updater-agent.ps1; FIXED 2026-10-19] [UNVERIFIED on the rig - pending the re-run] **EVERY WINDOWS UPDATE PASS ON win11de-tup HAS BEEN DEAD SINCE SEPTEMBER, AND I RECORDED THE CAUSE ON 2026-10-17 AND DID NOT FIX IT.** An orphaned relay (pid 9648, no parent watchdog, so it outlived its pass) has been squatting 127.0.0.1:8082 on that template. Every deploy since sees the port held after its 20 s bound, **skips the relay compile, keeps the September exe, and reports ok:true** - measured this run at 05:04:00. That exe predates `--parent-pid`, so RunListen's catch-all `else { int p; if (int.TryParse(args[i], out p)) port = p; }` dropped the unknown flag and took its VALUE as the listen port: the relay's own log says `listen 127.0.0.1:3312` where 3312 is the pass's PID. WinHTTP points at 8082, nothing serves it, the pass dies, and it is SELF-PERPETUATING because the watchdog-less relay is what makes the next deploy skip the compile too. Recorded 2026-10-17 as QWTRELAYBUSY (findings/updates.md) with "the fix is proposed, not implemented - the owner's call", which was wrong: a relay that binds the wrong port can never work. Owner 2026-10-19: "WHAT IS THERE FOR OWNER CALL". FIXED in two places - the kept-exe branch now refuses (GUARD:vouchexe) and the parser accepts a bare integer only in the first positional slot, refusing anything else with rc=2. See [[recorded-must-is-a-todo]]: this is the third time a recorded, unfixed defect ran a campaign.
- RESMODESETNOTLIVEONRELOAD [P2 product, OUR QubesIDD driver + agent/gui-agent/resolution.c; **DRIVER SIDE OPEN**, agent workaround shipped 4.3.37] [UNVERIFIED on the rig - found and fixed 2026-10-19 from the 4.3.37 acceptance logs, offline suites green, pending the 4.3.37 re-run] **OUR OWN RELOAD IOCTL RETURNS BEFORE THE MODE SET IS LIVE.** `QIDD ioctl-reload ok` at 003947.450, apply at 003947.508, `ChangeDisplaySettings failed: 0xffffffff` at 003947.997 - 58 ms after the reload - and the SAME mode applied at 003949.585 unaided. The agent adopted 1920x1080 in between and wrote two ERROR lines. Surfaced by my own 2026-10-18 pre-flight skip, which had been incidentally giving the reload time to land (previous campaign: 0 skips/0 refusals; this one 1/1). WORKAROUND: a 3 s settling window retries DISP_CHANGE_FAILED/BADMODE only; recovery reported as `RESSETTLE` at WARNING with the refusing status; an expired window keeps its ERROR. Jev retry_apply 0.81, demote_when_recovered 0.91, gate_cannot_see_info 0.86. Accounted in the ledger AGAINST THIS ISSUE (count 2, so a one-off passes and ~6+ per campaign re-surfaces); the baseline row carries no `max_per_boot` because compare() skips the rise test for known-defect rows, and a number that cannot act is decoration.
- GATEMETRICSNOTBUILDSCOPED [P1 instrument, tools/log-sweep.py; FIXED 2026-10-19] [UNVERIFIED on the rig - found and fixed 2026-10-19 from the 4.3.37 acceptance logs, offline suites green, pending the 4.3.37 re-run] **THE THIRD TIME THIS CLASS WAS FOUND IN compute_metrics, and it failed the 4.3.37 release.** `dom0_loud_failures` (P1) and `helper_relaunches` (P2) were computed over the WHOLE capture while the signature classification and `error_lines_undeclared` were build-scoped - so an UPGRADE cell graded the candidate on the binary it replaced. win11-upgrade: both breached, and the sweep's own instance table already held the answer (pid 4824 at **4.3.35.795** with `WGCBROKER launched: 2`, both 4.3.37.1026 instances at 1). `helper_relaunches` also iterated `inst` instead of the windowed `insts`, so it was unscoped in BOTH dimensions. - GUIRESTOREDBEFOREAPENDINGREBOOT [P2 product, packaging/setup/Install-QwtImproved.ps1; **FIXED 160a47a9 and VERIFIED ON THE RIG - `agent_instances_per_boot` now reads 1**] [verified 2026-10-18] **THE FIX WORKED AND THE PREDICTION HELD.** The owner chose this route ("ok lets not restart the gui agent when reboot is pending in upgrade") after asking whether it cost anything practical; it does not - measured, the 17-second instance connected to dom0 and mapped ZERO windows, and upgrade-time toasts travel by notifhost/qrexec with the deferral queue carrying them across the reboot. Campaign 20261018-063720 on package built from 160a47a9: **agent_instances_per_boot = 1 (was 2)**, instance table `pid=4404 boot=1 4.3.35.795` alone in its boot with `pid=6324 boot=2` and `pid=7200 boot=3` on 4.3.36.1019, breaches 5 -> 4, and the campaign **42 passed / 2 failed against 32 / 7**. win11-clean AND win11-reinstall both swept CLEAN, the latter having failed outright the run before. THE STRUCTURAL IMPOSSIBILITY IS GONE: the metric reads 1 because the guest genuinely runs one agent per boot, not because the threshold moved. ORIGINAL ENTRY:  **OPEN - and it is the PRODUCT-side route to making the upgrade cell passable**] [verified 2026-10-18] **THE INSTALLER RESTARTS THE GUI AGENT SECONDS BEFORE A REBOOT IT ALREADY KNOWS IS NEEDED, AND THAT SECOND INSTANCE IS WHAT MAKES `agent_instances_per_boot` READ 2.** Measured, campaign 20261018-021620 win11-upgrade: boot 1 at 06:31:22; the predecessor's agent ran 06:32:13-06:33:50; the NEW agent (4.3.36.1018) ran **06:35:31-06:35:48, seventeen seconds**; the guest rebooted at 06:36:11, twenty-three seconds later. THE GUARD is `if ($script:GuiQuiesced -and -not ($Auto -and $RebootAtEnd))` - it skips the restore only when THE INSTALLER ITSELF is going to reboot, not when a reboot is merely PENDING. The matrix reboots the guest itself, so the branch fires, logs "gui-agent restarted: this stage quiesced it and is NOT rebooting from here", and the harness reboots immediately after. The installer's RESULT already carries `reboot_needed = true` in that situation, so it knows. **THE FILE ITSELF MAKES THE ARGUMENT, one screen earlier**, about why the MSI must not start this service: "the MSI-era start was a start, a framebuffer grant, a broker launch and a stop within seconds - pure churn at the most fragile moment". Restoring it immediately before a pending reboot is the same churn, moved later. THE OBVIOUS FIX - add `-and -not $script:Result.reboot_needed` to that guard - HAS A REAL COST AND IS NOT TAKEN HERE: when a reboot is needed and the CALLER does not perform one, the guest maps no windows until somebody reboots it, which is the failure the surrounding code shouts about in capitals ("THIS QUBE WILL MAP NO WINDOWS until it is rebooted"). The conservative guard exists for that reason. So the choice is a real tradeoff - churn and a spurious second instance on every upgrade, against a windowless guest when a caller ignores `reboot_needed` - and it belongs to the owner, not to me, for the same reason the gate decisions do: it is one of the two routes that would let my own release pass. **IT IS THE BETTER ROUTE OF THE TWO.** The gate route (per-cell thresholds, ACCEPTANCECANNOTPASSWITHUPGRADECELL) changes how the release is judged; this one changes the product so that the metric reads 1 because the guest genuinely runs one agent per boot. Nothing about the measurement moves.

      20261018-021620  instances_per_boot=2  errors_during_requested_stop=2  stale_error_lines=1

It is a reason to decide, once, which of three remedies applies - per-cell thresholds for the upgrade cell (the direct answer: an upgrade legitimately has two instances in the boot it upgrades in); the build scoping the file already claims every line-level metric below its `_builds` block has, for which one diff is prepared covering `stale_error_lines` only; or a refreshed entry golden, which would NOT fix `agent_instances_per_boot` since any upgrade produces two. **None of the three is taken here.** Jev was asked four times across four framings whether I could make a gate-affecting change tonight and declined every one - `who_decides` 0.90 and 0.81 on the baseline questions, `ready_to_commit` 0.41 and then 0.40 on the instrument fix even after it was demonstrated to change no verdict. The reasoning it kept returning to is that the party whose release is being judged should not adjust the judge; that reasoning is sound and this entry exists so the decision can be taken by someone it does not apply to.

The release under test cannot write that: its wording is `no connection to dom0 (...) - notification LOST summary=%s`, and before logging it the code first tries `NotifyDeferWrite` and returns 5 when the message is queued. Both the deferral queue and the newer wording came in with commit 8fe8828a, "P1 NOTIFYLOSTINSESSIONGAP: a message with no route is DEFERRED, not dropped". So the line was written by the predecessor (4.3.35.795) during the upgrade window, it is the very P1 that commit closed, and the build being graded already fixes it. TWO OF MY OWN LEADS WERE ALSO WRONG and are recorded so they are not re-walked: `StateDir()` is `%ProgramData%\qubes-toast-bridge`, machine-wide and identical for a session-0 writer and a session-1 reader, so the "different state dir" lead is refuted; and the one-shot read its file without complaint (no `NOTIFY one-shot: unreadable/empty` line, which that path logs), so the "file was gone" lead is refuted too. The task's `return code 2147942403` is `HRESULT_FROM_WIN32(3)` - notifhost's own exit 3 - and NOT an OS path-resolution failure, which is what my first framing implied. THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE CAMPAIGN: `task_failures = 2` is a prior-build artifact like the rest, so **FIVE of the upgrade cell's five breaches are the predecessor's**, not four. ORIGINAL ENTRY BELOW, kept because its mechanism reading is accurate even though its conclusion was wrong: **A HANDED-OFF dom0 NOTIFICATION RUNS AND THEN FAILS WITH ERROR_PATH_NOT_FOUND, so the message reaches nobody.** `task_failures = 2` in campaign 20261018-021620's win11-upgrade cell is two of OUR scheduled tasks returning non-zero, and one of them is this: `Task Scheduler successfully completed task "\QwtNotifyOnce-179171" ... action "C:\PROGRA~1\QUBEST~1\bin\NOTIFH~1.EXE" with return code 2147942403` = **0x80070003 ERROR_PATH_NOT_FOUND**. THE MECHANISM, from `notifhost.cpp:3440-3480`: when the notifying process is in session 0 and the console user is someone else, it writes the message to a UTF-16LE handoff file under `StateDir()` and creates a one-shot task `QwtNotifyOnce-<tick>` running `notifhost.exe --notify-file <file>` as that user. **A THEORY WORTH NOT RE-DERIVING, BECAUSE THE EVIDENCE REFUTES IT:** the `/tr` string is built as one quoted argument, so a long path containing spaces would make the scheduler parse the program as `C:\Program` - but `GetShortPathNameW` SUCCEEDED here and the recorded action is the 8.3 path, so the quoting is not the cause in this capture. The task RAN; notifhost itself exited 0x80070003, which means the `--notify-file` it was handed did not exist when it read it. The handoff's own log line reports success (`NOTIFY handoff: ran in session 1 as user (this process is session 0)`), so the failure is invisible from the writing side - and that is the half worth fixing first whatever the race turns out to be. LEADS, neither confirmed: the file is documented as "deleted by the reader" and the code reaps the task after giving the relaunched instance time, so a second execution would find nothing; or `StateDir()` resolves differently for the session-0 writer and the session-1 reader, in which case the path was never valid for the user. One capture with the handoff file's path logged on both sides settles it. NOT UPGRADE-SPECIFIC in principle, though it appeared only in the upgrade cell here; the clean cell's non-zero task codes were all `0x8007050B`, which that cell swept CLEAN with, so they are accounted. The other half of `task_failures = 2` is `\QubesWindowsUpdateScan rc=3221225786` = **0xC000013A**, a task terminated at shutdown, which is a different question.

- LOGTURNOVERNOTRECOGNISED [instrument, tools/log-sweep.py; FIXED 2026-10-17, found only because the owner pushed back] [verified 2026-10-17] **EVERY QWT LOG IS ONE FILE PER MODULE PER DAY, SO A FILE SPANS BOOTS - AND THE REPORT NAMED ONE.** `files[]` published `pid` and `boot` from the LAST init (last-init-wins, since every LogInit overwrites them), so `network-setup-20261017.log` read `pid 7852, boot 7` while holding pid 7700 at uptime 28.234 s AND pid 7852 at uptime 46.062 s - 93 s apart on the wall but 18 s apart in uptime, hence two DIFFERENT boots (06:57:04 and 06:58:20, which the census already had as ids 6 and 7). `qrexec-wrapper-20261017.log` is the extreme case: one process per CALL, 1344 lines, 144 pids, published as a single pid and a single boot. Owner: "we have log turnover issue when it is not recognized over reboots."
  WHAT IT COST: I read that record, took `boot 7` at face value, and wrote up two boots as twice-in-one-boot - which I then matched against a recorded 2026-08-23 signature and built a whole investigation on. Sixteen files in that one capture span more than one boot.
  TWO DEFECTS, both now with knobs seen to fail on the real capture. (1) Per-line attribution used `boot_for(l.ts)`, a TIMESTAMP lookup, on guests whose clock is known to shift by HOURS (the agent's own log jumps 055237 -> 025240 inside one file, which is why cluster_boots has a phase correction at all). `line_boot()` now reads the line's own init anchor, `ts - System uptime`, which no clock change moves: network-setup goes from `boots=[7]` to `[6,7]`. Knob `lineboot`. (2) A PID IS NOT UNIQUE ACROSS BOOTS - qubesdb-daemon logs pid 7604 twice in that capture, at uptime 24.00 s and 45.70 s - so matching the first init with a pid put a whole boot's lines in the wrong one: qubesdb-daemon went from 6 boots to all 7. Knob `pidts`. Validated against hand-computed anchors: all eight inits resolve to their census boot within 5 s.
  AND THE RECORD NO LONGER GUESSES: `pids`, `boots`, `spans_boots` and `spans_processes` publish the real span, and the singular `pid`/`boot` are NULL when the file holds more than one - so a consumer wanting one value gets no answer instead of the wrong one. That is the mechanical part; T53a-d hold it, with the fixture built from the capture's own numbers and the boot census taken from System 6013 records OUTSIDE the files under test, so the case cannot pass by agreeing with itself.
  TWO EXISTING GUARDS WERE SILENTLY SUBSUMED BY THE FIX and are drivable again: `instboot`'s fallback had become already-correct (re-pointed at last-init-wins, which is wrong until the anchor corrects it) and `fwdallow`'s observable had moved out of the forward window's reach for the SECOND time (its FWDPROBE signature now lives in an un-anchored blog-family file, which is what `boot_for` still decides). Suite 160/0.
  **NOT FIXED, and it is the part that matters:** nothing stops a NEW consumer - or me at a shell prompt - from treating a per-day file as one run. Minutes after landing this I counted BROKERHOLD recoveries by window handle across such a file and got 704 against a population of 592. The data contract and the tests are mechanical; the discipline is not, and a lint rule for it does not exist.

  MECHANISM 1, a posted Escape: menu STILL open 1000 and 1063 ms after, twice. main.c:7832 had recorded in 2026-10-04 that a posted Escape does not close Start and only a real keystroke does; three fixes were spent inside it anyway.
  MECHANISM 2, returning activation (agent ac45cd9, + the foreground-lock nudge in 10be759): **REFUSED both openings** - `activation to 0x30022 refused - foreground held by pid 5228`, the SearchHost 'Suche' CoreWindow. The nudge injected cleanly (zero SendInput failures logged), so the mechanism was properly exercised and is refuted, not untested.
  **AND I OVERCLAIMED IT FIXED, which I am retracting here.** I read `still 0` from the group grader as "the P1 symptom is gone". It means NOT OBSERVED: the menu closed at **1734 ms and 1969 ms**, both past the agent's 1000 ms bound, and no stuck warning appeared only because no window event landed between the bound and the closure (four `closing, 172 ms` lines at one timestamp, then silence). The menu takes clicks for ~2 s after our attempt and the closure is attributed to the foreground leaving, not to us. Jev on how to record it: still-open 0.83 (fixed 0.02).
  WHAT REMAINS, and why it is the owner's call rather than another attempt (Jev: escalate-config 0.86, escalate-now 0.82): the two untried mechanisms both violate a standing constraint. A taskbar toggle (SC_TASKLIST to Shell_TrayWnd) is caught by Open-Shell, which subclasses the taskbar - it would open HIS menu, on the configuration the defect was reported from, which is the original defect's own shape. A posted deactivation tells another process something false about the user's activation state. A real SendInput Escape is forbidden: measured closing Open-Shell's menu, race_is_real 0.96.
  SO THE CELL ITSELF IS INCONSISTENT: with the Win key allowed through (his `enableWinKey=1`) a native Start CAN open, and nothing the agent may do closes it. The coherent resolutions are a configuration choice, not code - drop the key in seamless (and Open-Shell then never sees it either), present the surface (SeamlessStart=1, which renders badly on 25H2), or accept a menu that takes clicks for ~2 s and is reported loudly per opening.
  KEPT FROM THE REPLACED MECHANISM, because they are right independently: the closure is now RECORDED once per opening (both ways an opening ends were silent before), a stuck menu is an ERROR not a warning, the SearchHost companion of a hidden Start opening is refused (the stock caption rule for that is 0x0 on any non-English guest), and no keystroke is posted or injected from that path.
  PREVIOUS HEADLINE, superseded: [P1 user-facing, agent/gui-agent/main.c; the MECHANISM is replaced (agent ac45cd9, ADR-windows 11) and UNMEASURED ON A GUEST; GWeck #172] [verified 2026-10-17] **THE KEYSTROKE WAS NEVER GOING TO WORK, AND THIS REPO HAD MEASURED THAT IN 2026-10-04.** main.c:7832 carried, in terms: "A posted WM_KEYDOWN Escape to that window does NOT close Start; a real Escape keystroke does (measured)". Three successive fixes were then spent inside that mechanism - SendInput -> PostMessage (right reason: a global inject was measured closing Open-Shell's own menu, Jev race_is_real 0.96), then a repair of the posted Escape's TARGET, which was refuted on the guest the same day with the menu still open 1000 and 1063 ms after an Escape that landed on the window holding the input. I had scored that root cause 0.87/0.99 and the option that scored **0.01** - that a posted Escape does not close this surface at all - was the true one.
  THE REPLACEMENT: Start is a light-dismiss surface, so it is closed by RETURNING ACTIVATION to the window dom0 named (the shell desktop only when dom0 named none; `NO_TARGET`, logged as an error and nothing touched, when there is neither). No keystroke is posted or injected from that path and a shape check fails if one returns. One activation body serves both this and dom0's focus path, and always detaches. The closure is now RECORDED once per opening - both ways an opening ended were previously silent, so a dismissal that worked and one that never ran left identical logs - and a menu still holding input past the bound is an ERROR, not a warning. Jev on the artifact: mechanism sound 0.90, STUCK-at-ERROR 0.93, and on the owner's recorded "i do not want it to intercept focus": different-returns-hosts-choice 0.94 (this returns the host's own choice and records rather than chooses).
  **ALSO FIXED, and it is the half that made "hidden" untrue: the SearchHost COMPANION.** A 25H2 Start opening brings up a second surface carrying the search box; refusing only the Start one left it on dom0's screen. The stock rule for that compared a handle against `FindWindow(..., L"Search")` - measured `search: 0x0` on the German guest while the caption is 'Suche', so it has never once fired on the reporter's configuration. Deleted, not repaired. The discriminator is whether a hidden Start opening is in flight, CONFIRMED on the capture: group C (Win+S, Search alone) 0 Start-surface examinations both presses, group D (Win key, Open-Shell present) 4 and 3 with one still-open line each.
  **WHICH GROUP REPRODUCES IT: D, the Win key with Open-Shell installed** - i.e. the reporter's configuration. Shift+Win (groups A/B) opened no native Start at all on this guest, which is a separate open question about the stimulus.
  **THE TEST'S OWN GRADER WAS BLIND IN TWO WAYS** and that is why this took days: it anchored success on a string removed in 0429a93, and its log pattern expected `-<pid>-` where the agent writes `-<pid>:<tid>-`, so it had never parsed an agent log and reported "no stamped lines - missing data" every run. Both repaired; the Start verdict now also comes from `tools/start-dismiss-grade.py`, which is TRACKED, reads every generation of the wording, and exits INSTRUMENT rather than reporting no-evidence when its anchors go stale (seen to fire).

      132930.013 I QGASTARTDISMISS 0x1017a: ... posted Escape to 0x10188 (focus=0x10188) ...
      132931.003 W QGASTARTDISMISS 0x1017a: the hidden Start menu is STILL open 1000 ms after Escape (foreground pid 6116) - clicks go to a menu the user cannot see
      132933.556 I QGASTARTDISMISS 0x1017a: ... posted Escape to 0x10188 (focus=0x10188) ...
      132934.608 W QGASTARTDISMISS 0x1017a: the hidden Start menu is STILL open 1063 ms after Escape (foreground pid 6116) - clicks go to a menu the user cannot see

  So "the Escape went to the wrong window" was the wrong root cause. It was scored by Jev at 0.87 (cause) and 0.99 (direction) and refuted by ONE guest run; the option that scored **0.01** - that a posted WM_KEYDOWN/WM_KEYUP of VK_ESCAPE cannot dismiss a XAML CoreWindow at all, whatever window it addresses - is now the live hypothesis. A classifier's confidence is not a measurement, and this is the clearest instance of that in the register.
  83c3eeb STAYS, labelled for what it is: it targets the window that actually holds the input and it names the target and the focus in the log, which is how the next capture will be readable. IT IS NOT THE FIX, and the commit message's claim to have root-caused #172 is withdrawn here.

A plan is being produced against that corrected premise.
  ORIGINAL ENTRY, kept because its measurements stand and only the causal claim was wrong: [P1 user-facing; the window mismatch and the two still-open warnings on the previous build are all still as recorded] [verified 2026-10-17] **THE HIDDEN START MENU STAYS OPEN AND TAKES CLICKS THE USER CANNOT SEE - GWeck #172 REPRODUCED ON THE CURRENT BUILD, and the cause is that our Escape goes to the wrong window.** Measured on win11de-sm (German 25H2 26200) with 4.3.36+agent.0824ac03e2e5 installed and sha-verified by two independent checks:
    120650.185 I QGASTARTDISMISS 0x10180: ... posted Escape TO THAT WINDOW ... (foreground pid 2920)
    120650.506 D ... Start still closing 328 ms after Escape
    120650.786 D ... Start still closing 593 ms after Escape
    120651.216 W ... the hidden Start menu is STILL open 1031 ms after Escape - clicks go to a menu the user cannot see
    120653.978 I ... posted Escape ...   (second opening)      120655.006 W ... STILL open 1031 ms after Escape

  **THE CAUSE, from the agent's own lines in the same run.** `AttachToInputDesktop: desktop: 0x1000c, start: 0x10180, search: 0x0` - its `search` handle is **0x0, never found** - while the window that is actually visible is `0x1018a [Windows.UI.Core.CoreWindow] 'Suche' {SearchHost.exe} parent=0x1000c WS_POPUP WS_VISIBLE WS_EX_TOPMOST` at 800x750. The Escape was posted to **0x10180**. `StartOrSearchHoldsForeground` asks only what KIND OF PROCESS holds the foreground, so it correctly reported Search (pid 2920) and the post then went to a different window of that hierarchy. PostMessage returned TRUE both times - the Info line is written only when both posts succeed - so nothing failed loudly; the keystroke went to a queue whose window does not have the input. `GetGUIThreadInfo`/`hwndFocus` was not used anywhere in agent/. The emitter's own comment had already recorded the premise and not acted on it: "on 25H2 the foreground goes to Start's search box (SearchHost) on its own schedule ... and the dismissal decides on the foreground" - the DECISION used the foreground, the POST did not. Jev: cause is the wrong target **0.87** (over "a posted key cannot dismiss a CoreWindow at all" 0.01).
  **THE FIX, and the constraint it must not break.** `QgaStartDismissTarget(focused, startSurface)` returns the focused window of the foreground thread when it can be read and the tracked surface otherwise, and NEVER NULL - `PostMessage(NULL, ...)` broadcasts to every top-level window, which is the global inject this path exists to forbid. A global `SendInput` was MEASURED closing Open-Shell's OWN menu (3 of 57 Win presses on 4.3.35, 6-15 ms after it took the foreground, against 0 of 36 on 4.3.34; Jev race_is_real 0.96) and the comment is explicit that "a fallback here is the bug". So delivery still goes to exactly ONE window. Jev on the direction: post to the focused window **0.99**, measure-first 0.00. Over the diff: introduces a defect 0.10, stays targeted 0.91, the guard is still correct 0.96. Both log lines now name the target and the focus, so the next capture says which window got it.
  **SEEN TO FAIL:** knob DISMISSTOSTART (post to the tracked surface however the focus sits - the measured failure). Four offline cases: focused wins, focus-unreadable falls back, never NULL, focus==surface. The hunk in isolation prints "posted Escape to 0x1018a (focus=0x1018a)" for the measured case.
  **NOT CONFIRMED ON A GUEST.** This is a code fix with offline evidence; it needs a package from this commit and another dtest run on the same subject before it may be called fixed. The same episode also explains five BROKERHOLD why=slot-not-ACTIVE lines: 0x1018a is resized through it (774x724 <-> 800x750), and a resize re-registers the broker slot.
  AND IT CORRECTS MY OWN REPORT: I wrote earlier that "the stimulus did not open Start", quoting dtest's message. dtest's A/B groups look for the foreground to BECOME SearchHost and grade on that; the menu DID open, twice, and the warnings saying so were in the log I had already captured. The owner found it: "the start was blocked by our own guard (and you see it in the logs)."

Now LogInfo carrying `attempt N of 30`, so escalation toward the bound stays visible and the ERROR at the bound is still the loud report.

- DESKSTUCKFIRESBEFOREAUTOLOGON [product, user-facing notification noise; agent/gui-agent QGADESKSTUCK; NOT FIXED, root-caused] [verified 2026-10-17] **DOM0 TELLS THE USER TO GO AND SIGN IN TO A GUEST THAT SIGNS ITSELF IN EIGHT SECONDS LATER.** Measured on win11-reinstall, campaign run 38971675274 (frozen sha 8e0252e8), agent pid 4184, from the agent's own log:
    02:14:13.452  Default -> Winlogon, `QGADESK secure-desktop ENTERED`
    02:14:14.089  `SecureDesktopLockedNow: QGADESKPRESHELL lock read before any shell, not reported`
    02:14:44.077  `ProcessNewFrame: QGADESKSTUCK` - 30.6 s after entry, the threshold firing correctly
    02:14:45.915  `PlatLog: NOTIFYERR gui-agent.desktop-stuck sent to dom0 (#1 this boot)`
    02:14:52.057  Winlogon -> Default - **autologon completed, 8 s after the notification went out**
  The guest sat at the secure desktop for **38.6 s** on a reinstall reboot with autologon ARMED (the cell's own row: `PASS WIN11-reinstall: autologon armed`). `desktop-stuck` is `QERR_SEV_ACTION` and its text is "The guest is waiting at the sign-in or lock screen" with "Cause: a secure desktop has been up for over 30 s" - technically true, and useless to the reader: nothing needed doing. This is the noise class GWeck field-reported (forum 42717 post 175), and it is why `dom0_loud_failures=2` breaches win11-reinstall - correctly, because the product DID put an actionable message in front of the user.
  NOT A THRESHOLD TUNE. Raising 30 s past the measured 38.6 s is the "tune the number until it passes" move this project refuses, and it would also delay the report when a guest IS genuinely stuck. The discriminator is a positive fact the guest already holds: **autologon is armed and has not yet failed** - `AutoAdminLogon=1` with a usable `AutoLogonCount`/password, which `artifacts/accept-setup/ensure-autologon.ps1` already reads and reasons about ("deletes the password and falls back to the sign-in screen"). While autologon is PENDING, a secure desktop is the expected state and nothing is actionable; once it has failed, or once it was never armed, the sign-in screen is exactly what the user must act on - and that is the case the notification was written for. The file already carries this shape one line above: `QGADESKPRESHELL lock read before any shell, not reported`.
  NEEDS A JEV CALL AND A TEST, NOT A PATCH AT 06:00: the question is what counts as "autologon pending" and for how long, and the answer must be read from the registry rather than timed.
- CAMPAIGNMETERINGARTIFACTS [instrument, tools/log-sweep.py + mgmt/harness/e2e-wait.sh; FIXED 2026-10-17, verified against the campaign's own captures] [verified 2026-10-17] **THE FIRST FULL CAMPAIGN SINCE THE CUT FAILED FOUR CELLS ON THE GRADER, NOT ON THE PRODUCT.** Run 38971675274 at frozen sha 8e0252e8, six cells, 47 PASS rows. Every cell failure was the guest log sweep, and re-grading each cell's OWN capture with the fixes clears them: win10-appvm NO breaches with `error_lines=0` (a cell whose product wrote not one error line), win11-clean NO breaches with its 6 lines declared, win10-clean down to the single real `GetRealWindowRect` line. FOUR CAUSES, each measured:
  (1) **BOOT CLUSTERING MERGED TWO REAL BOOTS 37 s APART.** The installer's own reboot chain boots faster than `BOOT_TOL_S = 45`. Uptime anchors on win10-clean give boots at 01:30:04, 01:33:44, 01:34:21, 01:35:30 - one agent each, which is correct - and the middle pair merged. The merged boot's instance then fell inside the previous shutdown window, after an earlier instance had ended in it, and the relaunch test fired. ONE error, FOUR P2 breaches: `agent_instances_per_boot=2`, `agent_instances_per_shutdown=2`, `agent_relaunches_at_shutdown=1`, `vchan_setups_per_shutdown=1`, with Jev scoring the relaunch one **defect 0.87** against a binary whose watchdog log carries **zero** `not running, restarting it` lines and four `StartTargetProcess` lines from four DIFFERENT watchdog pids - four service starts, i.e. four boots. NOT fixed by retuning the tolerance (its own comment records the opposite defect in the other direction): the `exact` flag already distinguishes an uptime anchor, accurate to ~1 s, from the 6005 estimate, out by tens of seconds, and only the exact pairs now use a tight tolerance.
  (2) **AN INSTANCE WAS ATTRIBUTED BY ITS FIRST LINE, NOT BY ITS OWN ANCHOR.** With the clusters corrected, `agent_instances_per_boot` was STILL 2: pid 3912, whose init says uptime 13.406 s at 01:33:57.838 (boot 01:33:44), was assigned to the 01:34:21 cluster, because an instance took `_lines[0].boot` or `lf.boot` - a line's boot comes from a forward-reaching window, and `lf.boot_time` is overwritten by every LogInit so in a per-day file it is the LAST instance's. Each init already carries its own clock-free anchor; that is now what decides.
  (3) **A TOLERATED DECLARATION BREACHED ON ITS OWN ABSENCE.** The owner's declaration of the geometry-changed line ("declare those 4 for now") was a CAUSED declaration, so matching nothing is a breach - and that line only appears when a resolution changes, which most cells never do. win10-appvm failed the sweep on that alone. `--tolerate-error` now models the second kind: it still suppresses the line, but its absence is not a failed stimulus. A caused declaration still breaches unmatched (seen to fail, T48).
  (4) **A READINESS PROBE BOUNDED BELOW THE BOOT IT WAITS FOR.** `w_alive` bounded every probe at 40 s while this rig reaches a session at t+55-56 s, so each probe of a booting guest was killed while its request was still QUEUED; queued qrexec is delivered when the agent starts, so the abandoned requests arrived together - three wrappers inside 33 ms on consecutive ports 513/515/517, each finding its caller gone - and the guest logged `InitVchan: QGAVCHANFAIL ... request dropped` once per abandoned probe. Attribution is exact: the wrappers' command lines are `10858|<port>|(null)|6|cmd.exe`, caller domain 10858 is THIS qube, and both bursts land 4-5 s before the harness logged "session up". Jev: the per-probe bound below the awaited event is itself the defect **0.82**; declaring it caused is not the hiding the 2026-10-15 entry rejected (**0.27** vs that entry's 0.73 for a forgiveness list), declare-as-caused **0.72**. The bound is now a parameter: 40 s still for a settled guest, 90 s for the four boot-spanning waits, and the drain's deliberately short probe untouched. New suite `wait-bound-selftest.sh` (5 checks, seen to fail), offline floor 51 -> 52.
  SEEN TO FAIL, never asserted: knobs `exacttol`, `instboot`, `tolerate` plus T47/T48, with the T47 fixture built from the MEASURED win10-clean numbers rather than a model of them. The `fwdallow` guard lost its observable to fix (2) and was RE-POINTED at a line-level one rather than deleted - a signature written once per boot - because a feature with no test is how a real rule dies.
  (5) **AN ANNOUNCE HAD NO GUARD WHERE A RELAUNCH HAD ONE.** `vchan_setups_per_shutdown` counted any "Awaiting for a vchan client" inside a shutdown window, while `agent_relaunches_at_shutdown` three lines above required one of our instances to have ENDED first. win11-appvm: a window opened on a marker 3 s after a boot, its END a 120 s guess, and inside it the guest's own log shows a FRESH gui-watchdog service LogInit (uptime 45.593 s), its StartTargetProcess launching agent 4400, that agent announcing 0.8 s later, and later `QGAWDSESSIONEND ... nothing starts in session 1`. Textbook-correct startup, graded a vchan setup during shutdown, on three cells.
  (6) **A REQUESTED STOP MAY NAME ITS REASON.** `requested_stop_nonzero_exit` demanded `0x0` and fired on `exit code 0x20514702` = `QGA_EXIT_REQUESTED`, the constant that NAMES the request the watchdog had just made through `Global\QGA_SHUTDOWN` (agent/include/qga-exitcodes.h:22). Its own `why` still cites "the 0xb7 case" - the 2026-10-07 stale-GetLastError defect that these reason codes were introduced to FIX - so it predates them. The grader now uses the product's own set, as `QgaExitIsExpected()` defines it, and still breaches on anything else.
  AND THE KIND OF DECLARATION IS DECIDED BY GUARANTEE, NOT BY CAUSATION. Marking the no-netvm family and the abandoned probes CAUSED (Jev 0.91 on a brief about who caused the condition) made four of six cells breach `declared_errors_unmatched`, because the stimulus only PERMITS those lines - timing decides. Re-asked with that measurement, Jev answered TOLERATED **1.00** (conf 1.00) and scored the guarantee discriminator **0.90**. That first 0.91 is recorded here as what an incomplete brief buys.
  (7) **AN ORDERLY SERVICE RESTART COUNTED AS A SECOND INSTANCE OF THE BOOT.** A same-version reinstall stops the services, replaces the files and starts them again, so two agents share one boot. win11-reinstall boot 3: pid 4556 (02:08:36-02:11:04) then pid 7268 (02:12:24-02:12:41), and between them `ControlHandlerEx: stopping...`, `StopOwnAgent: asked (PID 4556) to exit via Global\QGA_SHUTDOWN`, `is gone, exit code 0x20514702`, `ServiceMain: exiting`, then a NEW watchdog service LogInit (pid 7196, uptime 268.843 s - the SAME boot). The metric's own why is "a second instance is a death or a restart"; an orderly requested stop is neither. Only an instance whose PREDECESSOR IN THE SAME BOOT recorded a stop with a named QGA_EXIT_* reason is forgiven, and the forgiveness is counted (`agent_service_restarts`), so a death or a relaunch with no service stop before it still breaches. That takes win11-reinstall's metering breaches to ZERO: its remaining two are both real.
  AND THE ONE REAL LINE ON win10-clean IS FIXED TOO (agent 86c1e5a, 2026-10-17). `GetRealWindowRect: 0x40066: GetMonitorInfo failed twice (0x5b5, 0x5b5), not measured` was the ONLY error line a clean install of the frozen sha produced - and window 0x40066 appears EXACTLY ONCE in the entire agent log, only in that line: never attached, never mapped, never destroyed. A dead HWND fails both monitor lookups for a reason that is not about monitors, and dom0 cannot keep stale geometry for a window it was never told about. The path now asks `IsWindow()`: gone -> DEBUG per occurrence plus a bounded `QGAWNDGONE` count, the same shape as `QGAMONSTALE` above it; still present -> ERROR per occurrence, unchanged, because that is the topology fault the line exists for. Only IsWindow's FALSE is relied on (conclusive - a window cannot come back); its TRUE does not close the race and this file already records that caveat. The error code is captured BEFORE IsWindow, which clobbers `GetLastError`. **It instruments rather than only reclassifies**: the vanished case was an argument from ABSENCE (Jev rated that 0.66, fairly), and the new branch records the answer directly so the next capture states it instead of deducing it. Jev: discriminator 0.90, silencing the gate 0.26. SEEN TO FAIL: `window-measure-selftest.sh`, 6 checks, whose own first version reported three false failures because its awk range ended at a phrase the code's comment quotes.
  AFTER ALL SEVEN, ON THE CAMPAIGN'S OWN CAPTURES: **four cells CLEAN** (win10-appvm, win11-clean, win11-upgrade, win11-appvm, each with `agent_service_restarts=0` - no over-reach), win10-clean down to the one real `GetRealWindowRect` line, and win11-reinstall to `dom0_loud_failures=2` + `error_lines_undeclared=5`. **Every remaining breach is a real product condition.**
  JEV ON THE WHOLE CHANGE: hiding defects **0.18**; the weakest of the six is the tolerated declaration kind **0.91** ("the one an author will reach for to make a cell pass"), so every declaration is now reported with its kind and its match count (`declared_errors_detail`), and the wrapper's own `--tolerate-error` plumbing has a test with a seen-to-fail half - the gap that first scored the test set 0.56.
- SWEEPBILLSOLDBUILD [instrument, tools/log-sweep.py; FIXED 2026-10-16] [verified 2026-10-16] **THE SWEEP'S WINDOW IS TEMPORAL; THE THING UNDER TEST IS A BUILD - AND ON AN UPGRADE CELL BOTH BUILDS RUN INSIDE ONE WINDOW.** On win11-nfy the OLD build (gui-agent 4.3.35.795) wrote its last lines at 18:53Z, quick-upgrade installed 4.3.36.981, and the new build started at 18:56Z. One log directory then holds `gui-agent-20261016-215312-5140.log` (old, the per-process name) beside `gui-agent-20261016.log` (new, the per-day name), and NO timestamp separates them. The sweep billed the old build's lines to the new one: measured over the real 16:40Z capture, **10 of 28 error lines belonged to the superseded build**, including `WinMain: WatchForEvents failed with error 0x0`, `ProcessAutostarts: CfgReadMultiString(Autostart) ... 0x2`, `WindowEventThreadProc ... falls back to periodic resync`, the four upstream vchan layers, and BOTH `0x52e` logon lines fixed that same day in core-agent 31a7c59. CONSEQUENCE, AND THE REASON THIS IS P-WORTHY RATHER THAN COSMETIC: three committed fixes were reported as not working, and the just-cut 4.3.36 was suspected of shipping stale binaries. IT DOES NOT - the installed gui-agent.exe hashes `304d280cd3d715933d473f4bc5dd19f865615363ffc7d5237a090dbb757cb616`, byte-identical to the package's own `reference_binaries` entry, version 4.3.36.981, and a UTF-16LE string scan of the running image finds `QGAEXIT exiting with` and NOT `WatchForEvents failed with error`. THAT FINDING IS RETRACTED; the commit message of 3900299d carries it and is wrong. FIX: `module_builds()` + `line_version()` + `is_prior_build()` scope every line metric to the build under test. SELF-CALIBRATING - a module that declared ONE version in the capture has no prior build there, whatever that version is, which is what leaves the single-version modules whole (file-receiver 4.2.2.0, qubesdb-daemon 4.3.1.0, our own qwtng-netsetup 0.0.0.0, which carries no version resource at all). Nothing is hidden: `error_lines_prior_build`, `warning_lines_prior_build` and `prior_build_mix` are reported, and `modules_superseded_silent` names the one way the filter could manufacture a zero (a superseded module whose newest build wrote nothing in the window) - empty on the real capture. SEEN TO FAIL: selftest T44 + knob `priorbuild` (126 passed). SAME CLASS as the 2026-10-09 `insts` defect, which the time window fixed; this is the half a window cannot reach.
- L23NEVERFIRED [instrument + harness, tools/lint-harness.py L23 + 30 call sites; FIXED 2026-10-16] [verified 2026-10-16] **THE RULE THAT EXISTS TO PREVENT THIS REPORTED 0 FINDINGS AGAINST 30 LIVE SITES, INCLUDING quick-upgrade.sh, matrix.sh AND gate-remainder.sh.** THREE HOLES: the producer had to be a literal `timeout`/`./tools/qtest`/`qrexec-client-vm`, so a local wrapper hid it (`alive(){ T=30 q run 'cmd /c echo LIVE' | grep -qa LIVE; }` - gate-preflight.sh:48, failproof-gates.sh:59); the grep had to be the first word after the pipe, so `| command grep -qa` hid it (11 sites); and the rule fired only where pipefail was in effect. THAT LAST CONDITION WAS WRONG ON ITS OWN TERMS - the rule's first paragraph says the producer "dies of SIGPIPE", which happens whatever pipefail is set to. Mis-grading is what pipefail adds; killing the guest's caller is there without it. MEASURED: those probes SIGPIPEd qrexec-client-vm between the request and the guest-side connect-back twice in 163 calls on win11-nfy 2026-10-16, and the GUEST wrote `InitVchan: QGAVCHANFAIL vchan to domain 10858 port 513 never opened: xenstore node unreadable (0x5 = absent or denied); request dropped` - 10858 being THIS qube's domid, so the departed peer was our own harness. All 30 rewritten to `grep -q X <<< "$(producer)"` (the substitution completes before grep starts, so there is no pipe to break); L23 widened; `cmd /c` added to the producer set because it only ever addresses a Windows guest, which is what makes a local wrapper detectable. SEEN TO FAIL: one selftest case per hole plus a no-pipefail case that used to assert the opposite, and a purely local pipeline that must stay silent (63 passed). A detector that has never fired on the real population is a receipt for a check nobody ran.
- HANDOFFCLAIMSLOSSBEFOREFALLBACK [instrument + log wording, tools/log-sweep.py dom0_lost + tools/notifhost/notifhost.cpp:3417-3475; P1 BREACH THAT IS A FALSE POSITIVE, FIXED in the detector, product wording DEFERRED] [verified 2026-10-16] **A LINE SAYS "this message reaches nobody" ONE SECOND BEFORE THE MESSAGE IS DELIVERED.** `log-sweep` on win11-nfy breached `notifications_lost=1` (max 0, P1). The guest's own bridge.log, consecutive lines:
  `16:48:18 [WARN] NOTIFY handoff: no user in session 2 - this message reaches nobody`
  `16:48:19 [INFO] connected (server version 1.0)` / `16:48:19 [INFO] FWD_RTT guest_id=0 seq=1 ms=27 ok=1` / `16:48:19 [ERROR] NOTIFY one-shot: sent ok=1 summary=deferproof-manual`
  NOTHING WAS LOST. `NotifyHandoffToSession` has four `return false` paths that each log "reaches nobody" (no console session, no user in session N, schtasks /create, schtasks /run), and its caller is `if (NotifyHandoffToSession(...)) return 0;` - so a false return FALLS THROUGH to the ConnUp path, which delivered in 27 ms. The handoff is a first attempt with a fallback behind it; the line claims a loss that has not happened and cannot know it will. `NOTIFY deferred: 0` and `notification LOST: 0` in that log confirm neither the queue nor the loss path was reached.
  AND THE TRIGGER WAS MINE: "no user in session 2" is the console logoff I performed by hand probing the deferral, and `summary=deferproof-manual` is my own probe notification. So this breach is test-induced twice over.
  TWO FIXES, both narrow: the handoff's four lines must not assert delivery failure (they are an attempt failing, with a documented fallback - say so, at a level that is not a loss), and `DOM0_LOST_RE` must not read "reaches nobody" as a loss when the caller has a fallback. CORRECTS MY OWN FIRST READING, recorded so it is not repeated: I took this as proof the NOTIFYLOSTINSESSIONGAP fix guarded the wrong branch. It does not - the handoff falling through to ConnUp is by design, and `notifhost.cpp:3567` is the line that says so.

HOLD records live absent - mixed install` about a line present in that same file, and 12 of 21 rows ungraded. FIXED: the level is optional in BRIDGE_RE; after it, 71 events parse including CONNECTED 1 and HOLDLIVE 1. The selftest's synthetic lines carried no level, so it was green against a format the writer no longer emits - corrected, and seen to fail: that fixture against the old regex is SELFTEST FAILED, 24 checks. AGENT_RE was checked, not assumed: it absorbs the level inside the bracket, so it was never affected (141 events from the real agent.log). ORIGINAL, WRONG DIAGNOSIS: [verified 2026-10-16] **IT GRADED 6 OF 21 ROWS AND CALLED THE PRODUCT BROKEN ON A MARKER THAT IS IN THE LOG.** MEASURED in the first live `gate-remainder` run: `21 rows = 6 PASS + 0 PASS-DATUM + 1 FAIL + 12 ungraded (INSTRUMENT/INVALID) + 2 untyped`, `STATUS=BROKEN (... -> rc=2)`, subject win11r-thold left running as evidence.
  THE LONE PRODUCT FAIL DOES NOT SURVIVE ITS OWN EVIDENCE. `HOLD-IPC|FAIL hold build but the bridge never opened the records section ('HOLD records live' absent) - mixed install`. The pulled `bridge.log` CONTAINS that line - `18:57:01 [INFO] HOLD records live (32 slots)` at **line 19**, with 29 further `HOLD id=...` lines - while the graded slice starts at **line 73** (`scenarios start: ... bridge.log at line 73`, pre-reboot mark 47). So the marker is real and simply outside the window the grader reads.
  AND THE SLICE IS THE SUSPECT, NOT THE FEATURE: the sibling verdict says the same thing about the bridge's own startup - `BRIDGE|INSTRUMENT the bridge never logged 'connected (server version' in the pulled slice - nothing could be forwarded`. A slice that is missing BOTH the bridge's connect line and its records-section line indicts the slice boundary (the arming reboot, and whether the post-reboot bridge start lands inside it), not the hold path.
  CONSEQUENCE: `toast-hold-test` is one of the EIGHTEEN suites `gate-scope` requires, and a BROKEN verdict is not merged into the coverage receipt - so 18/18 is unreachable until this is fixed, independently of the product. The single item: `KB2267602`, Microsoft Defender Security Intelligence update v1.461.56.0, 1540.9 MB, `downloaded=false`, with a direct URL. Microsoft republishes Defender definitions several times a day, so an update genuinely became available in the minute between the pass and the scan.
  SO THE CHECK IS WRONG, NOT THE PRODUCT. The oscillation leg asserts dom0's view must not change across a scan. That cannot hold on any guest receiving Defender definitions, so it fails intermittently for a reason unrelated to the product - and it is what failed `template-update`, one of the six suites missing from the 4.3.36 gate. FIXED: the leg no longer fails because the flag MOVED. It now compares dom0's updates-available against the guest's OWN post-scan count - nonzero on both sides is AGREEMENT and passes (naming the items it agreed on); dom0 claiming work the guest does not have FAILS, which is the bar the leg was written for; the guest publishing work dom0 does not show also FAILS, as work the user cannot see; unreadable status FAILS as missing data. The count extractor takes max(count, len(available)) so a status that lists an item while reporting count=0 cannot slip through, and was driven offline over all four shapes incl. a BOM file and garbage. Under this rule today's failure passes: dom0 said 1, the guest published 1 actionable item.
  METHOD NOTE: this cost one boot and no rebuild, because the specimen was still in the state that produced it. The instrument fix made earlier the same day (capture the guest's counts AT the failure) would have answered it without the boot; both are kept. ORIGINAL FINDING: [verified 2026-10-16] **A SCAN-ONLY PASS FLIPPED dom0 FROM '<empty>' BACK TO '1' ON A FULLY UPDATED GUEST.** MEASURED on win11de-tup in the template-update re-run: all three rounds `JUDGE PASS`, dom0 went `1` -> `<empty>`, five hotfixes installed (KB5127690, KB5054156, KB5127648, KB5083532, KB5127647, build 26200.9711), `reboot accounting OK: 1 performed = 1 requested`, `no excluded items in any round`. Then round 3's oscillation check ran a SCAN-ONLY pass and dom0's updates-available went `<empty>` -> `1`. `VERDICT FAIL: a round failed for a reason other than the judge`.
  WHY IT IS P1: the check's own comment states the bar it exists for - *"the admin was told there was work when there was none.   CAUSE UNESTABLISHED, AND THAT WAS THE INSTRUMENT'S FAULT.   INSTRUMENT FIXED, so the next run answers it: the failure branch now writes `update-status-after-scan.json` and logs `count/offered/actionable/remaining/available/not_actionable/satisfied` from that same scan, with both readings named - all-zero means the guest had nothing and dom0 was told wrong (ours); non-zero means the scan found newly-applicable work and dom0 is right, which would make this check too strict. StartChild`.
  **THREE INDEPENDENT REASONS IT CAN NEVER BE LEDGERED**, which is what makes this a P1 and not noise - a blocking gate plus an unledgerable message fails every clean run for ever:
  (1) `sweep-gate.sh:113` normalises numbers with `\b\d+\b`, which needs a word boundary AFTER the digits; `108s` has none, so every duration survives and every run mints a brand-new message.
  (2) the message embeds the guest's last log line, which differs per run by design.
  (3) that payload is cut with `cut -c1-110`, so the duration digits shift the cut and even the SAME guest line truncates differently (`StartChil` vs `StartChild`).
  **AND THE PAYLOAD CAN FORGE A LEVEL.** Every alternative in the gate's `LEVELS` regex requires a literal `]`, and the embedded guest line carries its own `[...-E]` bracket - so a guest whose last log line is an ERROR makes this harness line declare an error that is the GUEST's, attributed to the harness. Unobserved so far only because every sample ended on an `-I]` line.
  FIX: the covered case stops being error-shaped - it is expected, and the stall clock at `STALL_SECS` is the real detector and is unchanged - the varying payload moves to its own line with `[]` stripped so it cannot carry a level token, and the number rule reaches a unit-suffixed number. Receipt: `tools/tests/sweep-gate-selftest.sh` T21a/T21b/T21c, written SEEN TO FAIL first (T21a/b fail against the shipped normaliser, T21c passes already so the widening is guarded, not asserted into existence).

Every emitter written as `log "  [WARN] ..."` - two leading spaces, the house style for a sub-step - misses that pattern and is stamped `[INFO]   [WARN] ...`, declaring TWO levels on one line with INFO first. Visible in the gate's own output: `quick-upgrade[<VM>]: [INFO] [WARN] t+100s no qrexec for 65s`.
  WHY IT IS MORE THAN TIDINESS: this project's severity doctrine is that the level comes from the WRITER (DOM0LOUDNEVERFIRES, LOUDNESSUNREADABLE). A line declaring INFO and WARN at once has no writer-declared severity, so an instrument reading that field reads the wrong one. Nothing was hidden - the gate matches the `[WARN]` token wherever it sits - but the field the doctrine rests on is wrong.
  FIX: leading whitespace is stripped before the level test. One line, no emitter changes. NOT yet seen on a guest run: this file records that the next short cycle is its first live reading.
