That's it. After you apply, there should be ZERO unaccounted errors."* Campaign 20261018-083319 read **17 unaccounted**; it is now **7** on that same archived capture, and six of those seven are fixed in code the capture predates. **WHAT I DID WRONG FIRST, because it is the whole lesson:** I wrote receipted LEDGER ROWS for our own emitters - bookkeeping that accounts for noise instead of removing it - and separately re-listed messages as "needing judgement" that had ALREADY been judged individually, with verdicts sitting in the wire log (every one defect 0.00-0.03, most noise 0.68-0.83). Both rows and re-listing are reverted. **REPORTED (a real condition gained a real detector):** BROKERHOLD's per-frame WARNING was acting as a starvation detector, which Jev rates 0.15 at that job; `QgaHoldStarveDue` now runs from the later of hold-start and last painted content and raises `QGAHOLDSTARVED` at ERROR past `QGA_HOLD_STARVE_MS`, armed per RUN of holds so a window that starves, recovers and starves again is reported BOTH times - which the one-shot could not. The per-frame line is then Info (Jev no_concealment 0.74), meeting the condition its own comment had set twice: *"until that exists this line is not quieted"*. **SILENCED (no condition):** A6LEAK's skip IS the designed outcome of the guard that avoids the revoke-vs-unmap race; the session helper "still running - taken as applied" is a success stated at its own site; the installer's qubesdb-clock line and its GUI-restore skip both announce a deferral the code then performs, the latter already recorded in `Result.detail.gui_restored` (Jev on the four: no_concealment 0.85). **EXCLUDED (vendored):** `tools/upstream-emitters.py` derives 34 functions that emit a declared level in read-only code AND emit nowhere in ours - the SET DIFFERENCE is load-bearing, because `upstream/ro` also held copies of our own forks whose functions (`WatchForEvents`, `CaptureThread`, `Init`, `GetWindowData`) emit in both trees and a path test would have silenced OUR code; it reported 114 such overlaps and kept every one graded. Owner: *"we have forks, but you keep dead code in the tree. please dont"* - those copies are deleted, and the difference now reports 0. A vendored RISE still blocks (Jev graded the bare exclusion partial 0.71 on exactly one risk: our code feeding a vendored function garbage where the only line is `QpsWrite`'s), baselined in `mgmt/harness/upstream-counts.txt`. **AND THE GATE NO LONGER EATS ITS OWN TAIL:** matrix.log carries the harness's WARN-stamped quotation of this gate's verdict, so once it failed its own failure sentence was unaccounted for ever and it could never clear. **MY FIRST VERSION OF THAT EXCLUSION CONCEALED TWO REAL LINES AND `mgmt/harness/error-corpus.txt` CAUGHT IT, NOT ME** - it hid `[ERROR] classes: product-FAIL=2` (two product failures; recall fell 20/20 to 18/20) and then `product-FAIL=0 INVALID-*=1` (a cell that never ran). Only an ALL-ZERO footer and the gate's own result sentence are excluded; recall is 20/20, precision 15/15, suite 46/0. **ONE SILENCING WAS REFUSED BY JEV AND REVERTED:** a shape-based demotion of QGAVCHANFAIL scored **conceals 0.67**, so that line keeps ERROR and the CAUSE was removed instead - see QGAVCHANUNDECLARED: `w_alive` wrapped its qrexec probe in `timeout`, leaving the request queued with no caller, and the guest was correctly reporting our abandonment. `e2e-wait.sh` now issues ONE probe for the whole wait, polled, re-started only after the previous call has ENDED (Jev removes_the_cause 0.95). Build 39118710991 on c0989656 carries all of it; the campaign against that package is what demonstrates zero, and nothing here claims zero before it runs.
- RESWAITSTALLONSECUREDESKTOP [P2 product, agent/gui-agent/resolution.c; **FIXED 2026-10-18 - the pre-flight is skipped on the secure desktop; campaign 20261018-183821 rc=0**] [verified 2026-10-18] **THE IDD DID NOT OFFER A MODE FOR TWELVE SECONDS ON THE SECURE DESKTOP, THE DEADLINE EXPIRED, AND THE AGENT APPLIED IT ANYWAY.** Campaign 20261018-123812, one occurrence, and none in the three campaigns before it - rare, not routine. The line and its neighbours, same millisecond: `RESWAIT 5120x1440 not offered within 12000 ms (deadline; 74 polls), no current mode yet, applying anyway` (W), then `SetVideoModeInternal: New resolution: 5120 x 1440`, then `QGADESK secure-desktop LEFT (input desktop 'Default') - window mapping resumes`. So the stall spanned a Winlogon secure-desktop session and ended as it was left. **FOR SCALE: a healthy run of the same function reads `M0BLINK mode-offered polls=2 sinceobtain=125 ms` then `applied sinceobtain=140 ms` - 125 ms against 12000 ms here, two polls against 74.** This is a FALLBACK FIRING, which this project treats as an anomaly to diagnose and never as routine, and Jev agrees: **defect 0.58**, handled 0.32, overlogged 0.02 - so it does NOT meet the ledger's policy (defect <= 0.20) and must not be dispositioned. **IT IS THEREFORE THE THING BLOCKING "next release passed acceptance test"**: the campaign is 42 passed / 1 failed with product-FAIL=0 and all three guest cells swept CLEAN, both cold-qube caller legs PASS, QGAVCHANFAIL now carried by a CAUSED declaration from the harness that causes it, and QGAHELPERTASK accounted with a receipt - leaving this one row, correctly, because the gate is doing its job. NOT INVESTIGATED YET and deliberately not guessed at: what is owed is why the IddCx mode arrives 100x late under Winlogon, which touches docs/ADR-display's replug path and the arbitrary-resolution work. Nothing here claims it is benign; it recovered on this occasion, which is not the same thing.
- CIELIGIBILITYMEASUREDWRONGPLACE [gate coverage; P2, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-15] **I MEASURED WHICH SUITES CI CAN RUN BY RUNNING THEM ON THE DEV QUBE.** The curated list for the new `offline-suites` CI job was built by excluding suites statically and then RUNNING the survivors here - and its first CI run came back 36 green, 7 red. The dev qube has a vendored upstream mirror, a different grep and a rig; a GitHub runner has none of them. "Measured" has to mean measured WHERE IT RUNS.
  Six of the seven cannot run on a clean checkout: `one-log-per-module`, `pipe-client-null`, `pipe-connect-order` and `pipe-eof` apply patches against `upstream/ro/qubes-windows-utils`, which `.gitignore` excludes outright ("Vendored read-only upstream checkouts"), so it never exists in CI - no coverage is lost, because `build.yml` clones windows-utils fresh at `WINDOWS_UTILS_REF` and applies the same patches there; `stock-ab` needs rig paths. The seventh was my own bug: `clock-convention-selftest.sh` pinned `/home/user/.local/pwsh/pwsh`, this qube's tarball path, instead of looking on PATH. List 43 -> 37, floor with it, each exclusion named with its reason in the list file.

- QDBREADERNAMEWRONG [product; P1, FIXED IN SOURCE] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE INSTALLER CALLED A FUNCTION THAT DOES NOT EXIST, AND BLAMED DOM0 FOR THE RESULT.** `Set-GuestTimeZoneFromHost` read dom0's zone with `try { $iana = Get-QubesDbValue '/qubes-timezone' } catch { }`. `Get-QubesDbValue` is **not defined anywhere in Install-QwtImproved.ps1** - the name belongs to `guest/qubesdb-read.ps1`, which is not loaded there and is mentioned only in a comment a hundred lines above the reader that IS defined, `Read-QwtQubesDbValue`. So every install raised CommandNotFoundException, the bare `catch { }` swallowed it, `$iana` stayed `$null`, and the installer logged `clock: dom0 publishes no /qubes-timezone - leaving the guest timezone alone`.
  **DOM0 PUBLISHES IT.** Measured on win10-acc with the shipped `qubesdb-cmd.exe`: `/qubes-timezone` = `Europe/Sofia`, which the very next lines of that same function already map to `FLE Standard Time`. The feature never worked once since it was written, and its WARN named the wrong party every time. Jev: `reader_fix_is_the_cause` 0.96.
  **FIX:** call `Read-QwtQubesDbValue`, and tell the two cases apart - `$null` from that reader means "unreadable" (no daemon, no pipe, dom0 not connected, OR key absent), so an install running before our own qubesdb service is up looks identical to a dom0 that published nothing. `/name` is always present once the daemon is connected, so it answers which happened. A WARN that names the wrong party is worse than no WARN: this one hid a real defect for the life of the feature.
  **THE LESSON IS THE EMPTY CATCH.** `catch { }` around a call turned a programming error into someone else's fault, silently, for months. An empty catch has no business wrapping a call to our own code.

- HARNESSFORCEDTHECONVENTION [harness; P1, FIXED - this was the root cause] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE HARNESS WROTE THE SETTING THE GATE WAS MEANT TO MEASURE, AFTER EVERY INSTALL.** `mgmt/harness/rtc-lib.sh`'s `w_rtc_utc` did `reg add ... RealTimeIsUniversal /d 1` inside the subject, and `prime-run.sh:534` called it the moment qrexec answered - i.e. after the install had finished. So whatever the installer and the per-boot task derived was overwritten on every primed subject. MEASURED on run 11: the installer logged `clock: RealTimeIsUniversal set to 0 for the next boot (was 1)` and the boots that followed ran with **1** and a three-hour error. `mgmt/harness/set-rtc-utc-golden.sh` did the same thing to a sealed golden, so every subject cloned from it inherited a forced value as well.
  **AND THAT IS WHAT THE CLOCK BREACH WAS.** `clock_boot_correction_s` is not a timezone artefact - I thought it was and was wrong. `detect_clock_offset` finds a BACKWARD JUMP between consecutive lines of one log file, which only a real mid-boot correction produces. Forcing 1 on a qube whose RTC holds dom0 local makes the guest boot dom0's offset ahead; the puller corrects it back; that correction IS the jump. Jev: `harness_forcing_was_the_root_cause` **0.88**.
  **FIX: THE HARNESS REPORTS, THE PRODUCT DECIDES.** `w_rtc_utc` is replaced by `w_rtc_report`, which only queries the registry and the zone and prints both so a cell's verdict can be read against them; `set-rtc-utc-golden.sh` refuses with a pointer to the product path. Only one of the two values is correct for a given qube and which one depends on a dom0-side feature set at runtime when the GUEST reports in, so a harness cannot know it - and a harness that sets what the gate measures makes the gate blind. Jev: `report_not_decide_is_right` **0.94**, `anything_still_deviating` = `nothing-deviates` **0.93**.
  **FOUND BY ENFORCING A TARGET STATE INSTEAD OF PATCHING.** Owner, 2026-10-15: *"you have the target state, find what deviates and enforce systematically, stop trying 'one fix at a step'"*. Writing the invariant down and grepping every file that touches the convention found SEVEN deviations across five harness files, a golden-baking script, two selftests and three stale comments - where chasing symptoms had found one per campaign for three campaigns. The target state and the deviation list are in `scratchpad/clock-target-state.md`; one of its own entries (T6, that the sweep misread local stamps) was withdrawn by the same exercise.

- CLOCKWITNESSMISSEDONFIRSTBOOT [product; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE DETECTOR REFUSED ON THE FIRST BOOT WHILE A USABLE WITNESS ALREADY EXISTED.** Run 13 cell 1, in order of the guest's own stamps: `[14:18:14.305] SETTIME clock set to 2026-10-15T11:18:14.0000000Z (was -5.2 s out, now 0 s)` and then, **174 ms later**, `[14:18:14.479] CLOCKCONV no usable SETTIME witness from this boot, so the RTC convention was NOT decided`. The witness line was written before the reader looked at it.
  **CAUSE, TWO OF THEM.** (1) **The pull is fire-and-forget.** The qrexec client writes the request to the guest's own agent and exits; dom0 then pipes its date into a SEPARATE `set-time.ps1` process, which writes the SETTIME line whenever it gets there. Reading the log 174 ms later is a race the reader always loses on a cold boot, and "no witness exists" is a different fact from "it has not landed yet". (2) **The reader picked by file order, and the wrong end of it.** It sorted `set-time.ps1-*.log` by `LastWriteTime` DESCENDING and then took `Select-Object -Last 1` - the LAST match the pipeline emits, i.e. the newest line of the OLDEST file. It also anchored freshness on guest file mtimes, which this very script moves backwards by hours when it corrects the clock.
  FIX: `Get-ClockWitness` now picks the newest line BY CONTENT (dom0's own UTC, carried inside the line, is the only monotone reference available in the guest), and `Wait-ClockWitness` waits for it - bounded, with freshness judged on content against a clock re-read on every attempt, and three exits: a fresh witness, the deadline with nothing on disk, the deadline with only a stale line. The last two are reported, never guessed around. Covered by `clock-convention-selftest.sh` PART H (stale refused), PART H2 (fresh accepted) and PART I (content beats file order, with the wanted line planted in the file with the OLDEST mtime).
  **AND THAT IS THE BOOT THAT MATTERS MOST** - the first after the install, where the convention is still whatever the answer file and installer left. On the two later boots the reader worked and the derivation was correct: `RealTimeIsUniversal=1 matches what the RTC held` (effective offset 03:00:00), then `was 1 and this qube's RTC says 0 ... corrected`. So the OFFSET fix (CLOCKBASEVSEFFECTIVEOFFSET) landed - where run 12 said "fits NEITHER", run 13 identifies a dom0-local RTC and writes 0.
  **THREE CANDIDATES, AND `catch` MAKES THEM INDISTINGUISHABLE:** the `LogDir` registry read failing and the fallback (`$tools\log`) being empty; `Select-String`/`Get-ChildItem` unable to open a file the product logger holds open; or the logger's write not yet visible to another process. `Get-ClockWitness` wraps the lot in a `try`/`catch` that returns `$null`, so all three produce the identical message - the same defect class as QDBREADERNAMEWRONG, where an empty catch turned a programming error into someone else's fault.
  NOT GUESSED AT. The probe that settles it is one call on a live guest: read `LogDir` from the registry, list `set-time.ps1-*.log` there, and run the reader against it. Deliberately deferred to after the campaign rather than patched on a hypothesis.
  ALSO MEASURED, and still unexplained: the clock was corrected on THREE boots of that cell (`was -5.2 s`, `-10808.6 s`, `-10813.2 s`), so the flip written at the second boot did not stop the third needing a correction. Whether the registry value survived to that boot is exactly what the probe above will show, and no previous run recorded it.

- CLOCKBASEVSEFFECTIVEOFFSET [product; P1, FIXED IN SOURCE] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE WITNESS REPORTS THE ZONE'S BASE OFFSET, AND THE DECISION NEEDS THE ONE IN EFFECT.** `set-time.ps1` logs `offset $((Get-TimeZone).BaseUtcOffset)` - for `FLE Standard Time` that is `02:00:00` all year, while the offset in effect under DST (October) is `03:00:00`. The per-boot detector used that field, so a genuine three-hour displacement matched NEITHER hypothesis, the convention was never flipped, and the clock puller went on correcting every boot. MEASURED on run 12's first cell, in the detector's own words: `CLOCKCONV the RTC fits NEITHER convention unambiguously: it held 2026-10-15T13:34:15.1Z, dom0 said 2026-10-15T10:34:06Z, this guest's offset is 02:00:00`.
  **THIS IS THE LAST LINK OF THE CHAIN, and the harness enforcement is what exposed it.** With `w_rtc_utc` gone, the `RTIU=1` the guest carried was the PRODUCT's own value (prime-run's report: `RealTimeIsUniversal=1 on win10-acc`, zone `FLE Standard Time`), so the question became "why did the derivation not flip it" rather than "who set it" - which is answerable. The detector's refusal to guess is what made it answerable at all: the earlier form would have reported "matches" and said nothing.
  **FIX:** derive the offset locally for the instant dom0's value belongs to - `[TimeZoneInfo]::Local.GetUtcOffset($w.Dom0Utc)` - which is DST-aware by construction, and print BOTH the effective offset and the zone's base in every CLOCKCONV line so the two can never be confused again. The anomaly line also now carries the skew: without it the arithmetic in the measured line above could not be reconstructed, which is a gap in a message I wrote.
  Receipt: `tools/tests/clock-convention-selftest.sh` 16 checks, including a case built from that exact measured line - a witness reporting the BASE offset must still resolve - and seen to fail.

- CLOCKDETECTORVACUOUS [product; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE DETECTOR COMPARED THE GUEST'S CLOCK WITH ITSELF.** `CLOCKCONV` decided the RTC convention by comparing the clock BEFORE the time pull with the clock AFTER it, concluding "fits" whenever they agreed. But this script cannot see whether the pull did anything: qrexec hands dom0's date straight to `set-time.ps1`, whose exit code does not come back through the client - the pull's own comment says so. A pull that changes nothing makes both readings the same number and the test cannot fail. MEASURED on run 11: `CLOCKCONV RealTimeIsUniversal=1 matches this qube's RTC convention` **four times** on a guest that was three hours out.
  **FIX: AN INDEPENDENT WITNESS.** `set-time.ps1` DID see dom0's value and logs it: `SETTIME clock set to 2026-10-15T09:24:50.0000000Z (was -10821.2 s out, now 0 s; guest zone FLE Standard Time, offset 02:00:00)`. That line carries dom0's UTC, the pre-set skew and the guest's offset, all measured BEFORE the clock was touched, which is exactly what the decision needs. No witness, or a zero offset (where the two conventions are the same arithmetic), means NO decision and a loud line. Jev: `detector_is_now_sound` **0.84**. Receipt: `tools/tests/clock-convention-selftest.sh`, 14 checks, running the shipped text with the registry and log stubbed, including the no-witness case the old form got wrong and a seen-to-fail part that breaks the branch the first case actually reaches.

- CLOCKPREFLIGHTTOOEARLY [product; P1, FIXED IN SOURCE - awaiting a fresh-install measurement] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE INSTALLER SET THE GUEST'S TIMEZONE BEFORE QUBESDB EXISTED, SO IT NEVER SET IT.** Run 10, a fresh install from a package carrying the reader fix, logged `clock: qubesdb is not readable yet (/name empty too) - the guest timezone is left alone`. That is the HONEST version of the failure the undefined-function bug (QDBREADERNAMEWRONG) had been masking: `Set-RtcIsUtc` and `Set-GuestTimeZoneFromHost` are called from the stage-1 PRE-FLIGHT, before anything is installed, and the Qubes services that serve qubesdb do not exist yet. The new message was right and my own wording was wrong - it promised "the per-boot clock task sets it", and the per-boot task only derived `RealTimeIsUniversal`; nothing set the zone.
  **AND WITHOUT THE ZONE, NEITHER VALUE IS CORRECT AND THE DERIVATION CANNOT DECIDE.** With the guest on UTC its offset is zero, so `(RTC - offset)` and `(RTC)` are the same arithmetic: 0 is exactly as wrong as 1, and CLOCKCONV's two hypotheses are indistinguishable. Measured consequence: `clock_boot_correction_s = 10800` on run 10's clean cell, from a package that carried every other part of the fix.
  **FIX: a LATE settle, where qubesdb is finally readable.** `Set-GuestClockForNextBoot` runs on the success path after the device work - the installer's own log there says "qrexec answers in this boot" - retries the zone from `/qubes-timezone`, and only THEN writes `RealTimeIsUniversal=0` for the next boot, which is the first with a dom0-local RTC. That next boot is then correct from its first instant with nothing to correct. Jev: `late_site_has_qubesdb` 0.87, `gating_on_zone_is_required` 0.92, `closes_the_structural_window` 0.62.
  **A ZONE THE USER CHOSE IS LEFT ALONE.** Jev named overwriting it as the one thing this makes worse (`user-chosen-timezone` 0.81), so the zone is only set when it is still our shipped default (UTC); otherwise the guest keeps its zone AND its previous convention, and the puller corrects it as before. The withheld 0 is the correct consequence, not a gap: 0 is right only when the guest's offset equals dom0's.
  **THE ONE-BOOT WINDOW WAS NOT INEVITABLE AFTER ALL.** I had put the structural question to Jev (`one_boot_window_is_structural` 0.67) and was about to rescope the metric - `breach-only-on-recurrence` scored 0.73, but `would_that_be_hiding` came back **0.52**, a coin flip, which is not a mandate to weaken a gate. Re-reading the measurement instead showed the window closes if the zone is set LATE rather than early. A 0.52 is a signal to look again at the problem, not to change the threshold.

- DOM0LOUDNEVERFIRES [gate, tools/log-sweep.py; P1, FIXED - the check keys on the PATH now, and has been seen to fire] [verified 2026-10-15] **`dom0_loud_failures` IS P1 WITH max 0, AND IT CANNOT COUNT THE THING IT EXISTS FOR.** Its own definition: *"our own code told dom0 to show the USER that something of ours failed - a line on the dom0 notification path that its own writer declared W or E. A failure the user can SEE is P1 on this project; the severity comes from the writer and the path comes from our own protocol tokens, so neither is guessed."* The severity coming from the writer is exactly the hole: our notify path announces a sent failure at **INFO**.
  MEASURED on win10-clean, package 38771352401, 2026-10-15 - the guest's own log: `20:14:21 [INFO] NOTIFY one-shot: sent ok=1 summary=The PV NIC setup task failed`. dom0 was told, in words, that a component of ours had failed; the user sees that. The sweep for that same cell reported `dom0_loud_failures=0`, `error_lines=0`, `error_lines_undeclared=0`, and that cell's battery PASSED - read from the report at the time.
  **THAT LAST CLAUSE IS NOT RE-VERIFIABLE AND IS CORRECTED HERE.** The report was written under `/tmp`, which is RAM on this qube, and the subject was removed at 17:17, so nothing on disk now carries that cell's verdict; and the two halves of this bullet are not even the same subject - the metrics above are win10-clean, while the Jev verdicts quoted below are the 17:15 win10-acc sweep. What IS still on disk and was re-read 2026-10-15: that win10-acc sweep's answers are **defect 8, noise 76, expected 67, insufficient-evidence 160**, top defect confidences 0.84 / 0.72 / 0.67.
  AND A JUDGED DEFECT IS NOT ADVISORY, which corrects a second thing I wrote and then checked: `log-sweep.py:2377` is `elif cmp["breaches"] or judged_defects: status, rc = "FINDINGS", 1`, and `matrix.sh:243` turns that rc into `no "$c: the guest log sweep found a breach or a defect"`, i.e. a FAILED cell. So "the signature only sat in a section nobody opens" is wrong wherever `grade_cell_logsweep` ran. The open question is therefore narrower and sharper than the one first written: **which runs actually pass through `grade_cell_logsweep`** - a `prime-run` measurement cell does not - and the acceptance campaign is what settles it, because it does. Nothing here should be read as "the gate ignored a defect" until that run says so.
  **SO THE SWEEP DID NOT DO WHAT IT IS FOR.** A sweep exists to CONTRADICT the run that produced it. This one had the contradiction in its input and dropped it on the floor, then returned a metric of 0 that reads as a positive assurance. A detector that reports zero because it is not measuring is worse than an absent one: it is a receipt for something nobody checked. Same shape as the clock, where the analyser SHIFTED 2457 lines to hide a three-hour jump and raised nothing.
  FIX (made 2026-10-15): the loudness decision comes from the PATH, not the writer's chosen severity. `dom0_loud()` keeps the level test for lines that merely MENTION the path, and adds `DOM0_SENT_RE` (`sent`, `send ok`, `user notification sent`) which qualifies on its own at any level - a line recording that a notification was SENT is a user-visible failure by construction, whatever level its emitter felt like using. The emitter was not asked to change its level, because a detector that can be defeated by an emitter's mood is the defect.
  SEEN TO FIRE, which it never had been: `tools/tests/loudness-channel-selftest.sh` was rewritten around the measured line. It plants the INFO-level `NOTIFY one-shot: sent ok=1` and requires the metric to RISE (LOUD=5, SUCCESS_EXCLUDED=0), keeps a case asserting the level still qualifies non-send lines, and carries a seen-to-fail case that re-plants the level gate and requires the INFO send to be MISSED. Its predecessor asserted the opposite - `"a SUCCESSFUL send is not loud" "1"` - against a fixture it supplied itself, and asserted that the source still contained `not in ("W", "E")`: a test pinning the defect in place.
  AND THE PV NIC FAILURE ITSELF WAS NOT A FAILURE - see REAPEDTASKFRAMEMISMATCH. The task's work was done and its own log says so; the reporter mis-framed a UTC instant against a local one and could not see the shutdown that had reaped it.
- SWEEPGATESNOPRODUCER [gate, tools/log-sweep.py; P1, CLOSED - all 14 gates now fire and breach] [verified 2026-10-15]
  **UPDATE 2026-10-15, same day: ten of the fourteen now FIRE AND BREACH**, in `tools/tests/gate-producer-selftest.sh` (12 checks, registered in the offline battery, floor 36 -> 37). Each plants the line or event that should trip one gate and requires the metric to rise ABOVE its threshold, and a final check requires all ten to appear as BREACHES rather than merely be counted - a metric that rises without being compared is the same silence in another place. The seen-to-fail case runs the same analyzer over a corpus without any of them and requires all ten to read zero. Closed: `service_failures`, `wer_crashes`, `death_events`, `death_record_mismatch`, `unexpected_shutdowns`, `bugchecks`, `fallbacks_fired`, `stale_error_lines`, `handshake_refusals`, `helper_deaths` - the whole death-and-crash family.
  **AND THE LAST FOUR TOO, same day: ALL 14 NOW FIRE.** `vchan_setups_per_shutdown`, `agent_ends_unrecorded`, `helper_relaunches` and `watchdog_fast_death_backoffs` cannot be tripped by one planted line - they need agent and watchdog INSTANCES, a shutdown window and a capture time - so the suite carries a second corpus that supplies exactly that: an agent whose log STOPS at 10:12:30 with no requested stop and no death record against a 10:20:00 capture time (`agent_ends_unrecorded`), `WGCBROKER launched` twice inside that one instance (`helper_relaunches`), a vchan announcement at 10:12:30 falling inside the window a User32 1074 at 10:12:00 opens (`vchan_setups_per_shutdown`), and a watchdog recording the agent dying 500 ms after its launch (`watchdog_fast_death_backoffs`). 16 checks, 0 failed. Needing structure was the reason they were skipped, and skipping them is how they stayed unproven for as long as they existed.
  TWO THINGS THE PRODUCERS ESTABLISHED THAT THE AUDIT COULD NOT. (1) `service_failures` is NOT blind: our real services are `QdbDaemon`, `QrexecAgent`, `QubesGuiWatchdog` and `QwtngNetSetup`, and `OUR_SERVICES_RE` matches all four - an earlier suspicion that it missed a `QubesService` was wrong, that string being a PowerShell function name (`Set-QubesServiceRecovery`), not a service. (2) `death_record_mismatch` stayed at 0 on the first run, and that was the FIXTURE, not the gate: the planted 4001 said "gui-agent.exe (pid 4242) died with ..." where `EV4001_RE` requires "(gui-agent.exe, PID 4242) exited without being asked to - exit code 0x...". The invented text parsed to exe="?" and the gate could not count it - a fixture that does not carry the real shape measures nothing while looking like a pass, which is the same error one level down. **THE SWEEP HAS 33 GATED METRICS AND 14 OF THEM ARE NOT MENTIONED BY ANY OFFLINE TEST.** Not one fixture anywhere can make them non-zero: `agent_ends_unrecorded`, `vchan_setups_per_shutdown`, `handshake_refusals`, `helper_deaths`, `helper_relaunches`, `stale_error_lines`, `watchdog_fast_death_backoffs`, `service_failures`, `wer_crashes`, `death_events`, `death_record_mismatch`, `unexpected_shutdowns`, `bugchecks`, `fallbacks_fired`. All 14 are LIVE CODE - each is computed on every run and compared to a threshold - so each is a candidate DOM0LOUDNEVERFIRES: a counter that may be structurally unable to see its target, reporting 0, where 0 reads as a pass. A detector nobody has seen fire is not evidence, and a battery of them is a receipt for something nobody checked.
  THE DEATH AND CRASH FAMILY IS THE WORST OF IT: `death_events`, `death_record_mismatch`, `service_failures`, `wer_crashes`, `bugchecks`, `unexpected_shutdowns` and `fallbacks_fired` are precisely the gates that should have had something to say about the false `The PV NIC setup task failed`. `fallbacks_fired` is gated at 0 on the standing rule that a fallback firing is an anomaly to be diagnosed loudly - and nothing has ever demonstrated it counting one.
  METHOD, so this is not re-derived by hand next time: enumerate `DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS`, then search `tools/tests/` for each name. A name-based scan UNDER-reports (it called `dom0_loud_failures` unproducing while that suite demonstrably breaches it), so 14 is a floor, not a ceiling. The fix is one planted fixture per gate that drives the metric above its threshold, and a seen-to-fail case where it is not met - the same shape the loudness suite now has. Same lesson as the 18-suite gate with 12 producers.
- SWEEPGATEADVISORY [gate, tools/sweep-gate.sh + mgmt/harness/quick-upgrade.sh; P1, FIXED - it gates, and its first verdict was against ME] [verified 2026-10-16] **NOW GATES, AND THE RECEIPT IS THAT IT BLOCKED A RUN I WANTED TO PASS.** Owner 2026-10-16: *"the sweep gate SHOULD be fixed. it is the launcher that we defer."* `quick-upgrade.sh`'s `finish` runs the sweep before it writes its own verdict line and raises rc to 3 on findings, through `sweep_gate_rc` (`mgmt/harness/run-lib.sh`), which reads the gate's STRUCTURED `SWEEP-GATE-RESULT` line rather than the shape of its prose, counts the BLIND case (`undeclared_ours>0`) as a finding, and discounts this harness's own graded verdicts so a failing run is not counted twice. SEEN TO FAIL in the field, not in a fixture: `notify-errors-guest-test` exited 1 over `11 passed, 0 failed` the first time it ran. That verdict was WRONG on its merits (SWEEPWARNUNLEDGERABLE, at the head of this file) - but a gate that cannot change an outcome could not have produced it. ORIGINAL FINDING: **THE SWEEP GATE JUDGES 6 LINES IN 65 AND ITS VERDICT DOES NOT COUNT.** Two measurements from the light run and a feature run:
  `SWEEP-GATE-COVERAGE lines=65 with_declared_level=6 without=59 third_party=0` and `lines=64 with_declared_level=4 without=60 third_party=0`. By its own accounting **all** the unjudgeable lines are OURS - `third_party=0` - so it is blind to about 91% of what it reads. It prints `N line(s) carry NO declared level, so this tool cannot judge them. That is the gap, named.` and `nolevel` never reaches its exit code: `tools/sweep-gate.sh` exits 1 only on `newn > 0 || rose > 0`.
  AND WHAT IT DOES FIND IS NOT ACTED ON. In that same light run it reported two UNACCOUNTED lines - `[ERROR] t+75s no qrexec for 35s - and the log volume could not be read either` and `[WARN] log-volume bind failed - logs stay on C: and a wedge loses them` - and its own text says "Each is a real error our code declared. It is a defect to fix... Reading it and moving on is not one of the options." `quick-upgrade.sh` then returned **rc=0, "11 passed, 0 failed"**, because `mgmt/harness/quick-upgrade.sh:167` says in so many words: `REPORTS, DOES NOT GATE - yet ... the decision to let it raise rc is a separate one, taken when its coverage and its judge's score support it.`
  SO THE TWO HALVES LOCK EACH OTHER: it cannot gate until its coverage is credible, and its coverage is 9% because our own harness narration carries no level tag. The owner's rule is that the EMITTER declares; the 59 undeclared lines are harness progress narration, which is not an error but is also not declared, so it counts against the coverage that gating waits on.
  DELIBERATELY NOT FLIPPED TONIGHT, and this records the choice rather than hiding it: at 01:25 with a campaign queued, the two findings above are instrument plumbing (LOGVOLBINDSENTZERO below), so gating now would block the campaign on the instrument instead of on the product. The fix is in two parts and both are owed: give the harness narration a declared level so coverage is real, then let the gate raise rc.
- DEATHNOTIFIEDTWICE [product, guest/qwt-report-death.ps1; P1, FIXED IN SOURCE AND IN THE TESTED PACKAGE - the condition did not recur, so it is not independently proven] [verified 2026-10-16] **THIS ENTRY SAID "FIX (not yet made)" WHILE THE FIX WAS ALREADY IN THE PACKAGE UNDER TEST.** `Write-QwtDeathWatermark` now has TWO callers - the live path records the record it just notified (`guest/qwt-report-death.ps1:1403`) and the catch-up carries those forward (:1514) - landed in 8748dcf2 ("no duplicate toast, no silently dropped death"), which `git merge-base --is-ancestor` confirms is in 8fe8828a, the commit the 4.3.36 package was built from. NOT PROVEN THOUGH, and for the same reason as the notify deferral: the 4.3.36 campaign reports `death_events = 0` and `notifications_lost = 0` on win10-reinstall, the very cell that produced the double - so the condition never occurred and the dedup path was never exercised. Absence of the symptom on a run that produced no deaths is not evidence about the fix. ORIGINAL FINDING: [verified 2026-10-16] **THE SAME DEATH RECORD WAS NOTIFIED TO THE USER TWICE, ACROSS A REBOOT.** MEASURED on win10-reinstall, acceptance run 38787325142: the deaths log carries
  `20:04:59 [ERROR] DEATH #1 NEW Microsoft-Windows-TaskScheduler/Operational/201#345: The Windows Update scan task failed`
  and the identical line again at `20:09:51`, same channel, same EventRecordID 345, each announcing itself as NEW and as death 1 *this boot*. A double notification is P1 on this project by standing rule ("no doubles, nothing lost").
  CAUSE, from the structure rather than the timing: `Write-QwtDeathWatermark` has exactly ONE caller, `guest/qwt-report-death.ps1:1370`, inside the boot catch-up pass. The LIVE subscription path reports a death and never advances the watermark. So a death notified live leaves the watermark where it was, and the catch-up pass on the next boot re-reads from that older point, finds the same record and notifies it again. The per-boot ledger cannot stop it - it is keyed `boot=<token>` on a VOLATILE registry key and resets by design at every boot, which is correct for "death N this boot" numbering and useless for cross-boot dedup. The watermark is the only cross-boot memory, and the live path does not write it.
  NOT the clock and NOT a mint race, both excluded: the per-boot token is minted once into a volatile key, so the 960 s clock correction on that same cell cannot change it, and the two reports are five minutes and one reboot apart rather than concurrent.
  FIX (not yet made): the live path must advance the watermark for the record it just notified, so the catch-up pass starts after it. The watermark is already documented as "per channel and monotonic, so a record is never reported twice" - that promise is only kept for records the catch-up pass reports.
- REAPEDTASKFRAMEMISMATCH [product, guest/qwt-report-death.ps1; P1, FIXED - a reaped task was reported to the user as a failure] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE SHUTDOWN PROBE COMPARED A LOCAL INSTANT WITH A UTC ONE, SO NO SHUTDOWN COULD EVER BE FOUND.** A task instance killed by a shutdown leaves result `0x8007050B`, which looks exactly like a failure, so the reporter checks whether a shutdown was under way before telling the user anything. That check requires TWO positive findings: the result code names the shutdown (`0x8007050B` is on the list) AND a System 109/1074/6006 record sits near the instant. The second could not succeed. `$at` is UTC - `ConvertFrom-QwtDeathEvent` parses `SystemTime` with `AdjustToUniversal` - while `Get-WinEvent`'s `TimeCreated` is LOCAL, and `DateTime` subtraction ignores `Kind`.
  MEASURED on win10-acc, 2026-10-15, from the cell's own records: `\QubesPvNic` instance `{76474875-9292-45ca-914e-91031a339c05}` ended `20:10:54.329` with result `2147943691`, while its three sibling runs that boot (`20:07:44`, `20:11:44`, `20:12:52`) returned 0; `\QwtDeathReporter` was reaped the same way in the same window. Session records bracket it - logoff `20:09:32`, logon `20:12:31` - and `\QubesPvNicRearm`, whose only trigger is User32 1074, fired at `20:10:52.884`, i.e. a shutdown record 1.4 s before the reap. The payload's own log for every run of that boot reads `qubesdb up, /qubes-ip absent, no vif device: no netvm, nothing to apply`, which is a correct no-op on a netvm-less guest, and its `Ok()` exits 0. The work was done; the process was killed; dom0 was told `The PV NIC setup task failed`. Reproduced offline: local `20:10:52.884` minus UTC `17:10:54.329` is **10798 s** against a window of `[-30, +180]`.
  **AND OUR OWN WRONG CLOCK CONVENTION WAS CONCEALING IT.** While the guests ran a forced-UTC RTC, local == UTC, the offset was zero and the broken subtraction gave the right answer. Correcting the clock (CLOCKFEATURESETLATE) is what made this live - the same 10800 s that metric reported. A latent defect of ours was invisible for as long as another defect of ours was present.
  FIX: the window IS the query - `Get-WinEvent -FilterHashtable` with `StartTime`/`EndTime` derived from `$at`, so the frame conversion belongs to the cmdlet and no cross-kind arithmetic is left in our code. That also closes a second hole in the same body: it fetched the **20 newest** matching records and hoped the window was among them, but one shutdown cycle writes several (xenagent, `shutdown.exe` and winlogon each log a 1074, plus 109 and 6006), so in a multi-reboot install the relevant record scrolled out of reach while the reporter, itself reaped, read the backlog on a later boot. `ConvertFrom-QwtDeathEvent` now also pins the parsed instant to UTC, because a `SystemTime` without the trailing `Z` parses to `Unspecified` and the two consumers disagree about what that means.
  **DECIDED: the standard query, not a flag of ours.** (Still in force, and NOT reversed by the boot boundary above: that compares a BOOT IDENTITY - the volatile token the notify route already mints - to decide whether a watermark belongs to the running boot. It records nothing about a shutdown and depends on no writer of ours firing at shutdown, which is the property the rejection below turned on.) Writing a shutdown stamp at shutdown and clearing it at boot was considered and rejected - a boolean cleared at boot is already gone by the time the reporter reads the backlog, and a stamp carrying the instant would depend on a writer we have MEASURED to be unreliable (`guest/pvnic-selfprime.ps1:372`: the 1074-triggered task "does NOT reliably fire on a Qubes shutdown"). The documented `Get-WinEvent` filter answers the question directly and needs no state of ours to stay correct.
  WHY NO TEST CAUGHT IT: all 13 sites in `tools/tests/death-reporter-test.ps1` replaced the probe scriptblock with a constant, so its body - the retrieval, the record limit, the subtraction - was never executed by any test. The suite tested everyone who CALLS the probe and nobody who IS it. There are now 10 checks that run the real body with only `Get-WinEvent` stubbed, on a fixture taken from the records above, with the local/UTC pair built literally so it cannot pass on a UTC-zone build host, plus seen-to-fail cases for both the old body and the kind pin.
- CLOCKFEATURESETLATE [product + harness; P1, FIXED AND MEASURED - clock_boot_correction_s = 0] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE QUBE'S `timezone` FEATURE IS SET WHEN THE GUEST REPORTS, WHICH IS ONE BOOT TOO LATE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE CLOCK DEFECT.** `qubes/ext/windows.py` sets `timezone=localtime` only `if guest_os == "Windows" and qrexec` - both read from what the GUEST reports - so it cannot be set before the first install. A qube created without it runs Windows Setup and the entire install on a TRUE-UTC clock, and then, at the next domain creation, Xen starts presenting dom0's LOCAL time instead. The content of the clock moves by dom0's offset BETWEEN two boots, with no signal inside the guest, and the guest's only source of truth - dom0's time, fetched by the boot-time pull - arrives ~60 s in, after the clock has already been read wrong.
  MEASURED on a clean cell, 2026-10-15, from a base rebuilt with no forced value and the guest's zone already dom0's: `RTC held 15:35:55Z / dom0 15:35:46Z` (true UTC, guest 3 h BEHIND, skew +10790.9 s), then `RTC held 18:41:06Z / dom0 15:41:03Z` (dom0 local, guest 3 h AHEAD, skew -10803.2 s). That is `clock_boot_correction_s = 10800` against a max of 60.
  FIX: every path that CREATES a guest sets `qvm-features <vm> timezone localtime` before its first boot - `mgmt/reprovision-usb.sh`, `prime-run.sh`, `quick-upgrade.sh` and both create paths in `matrix.sh`. The window is removed rather than managed: the clock holds dom0 local from the first instant of Setup, Windows reads it as local (its default - we declare nothing), and the guest's zone is dom0's throughout. Nothing to converge.
  **AND EVERYTHING BUILT TO MANAGE THAT WINDOW IS DELETED.** `RealTimeIsUniversal` is written nowhere - not in the four answer files, not by the harness, not by the installer, not by the per-boot task, which is back to stock's shape of pulling the clock and deciding nothing (325 -> 142 lines). Stock QWT sets neither a zone nor a convention: its entire time handling is `update-time.bat`, one line asking dom0 for the date. Enforced by lint L26 (no writer anywhere, no `--unset timezone`) and `rtc-utc-selftest.sh` (the absence, the feature on all five create paths, both planted cases seen to fail). `clock-convention-selftest.sh` is DELETED with the decision block it tested and the offline floor moved 37 -> 36 in that commit.
  **MEASURED, same cell, six hours apart.** `win10-clean` against package 38771352401 (head 3ca82a2c), clean-installed from the rebuilt `win10-base`: `clock_boot_correction_s = 0` (max 60), P1 breaches 0, where the morning run read 10800. The sweep's own pre-sync probe read `skew -6 s` against ±10800 before; guest local 20:09:26 vs dom0 20:09:34; zone `FLE Standard Time` = dom0's Europe/Sofia; `RealTimeIsUniversal` absent (`ERROR: ... unable to find`); installer RESULT ok:true, no error-class flags. Clause 2 also passed on that cell for this package: updater channel aligned, start-menu aligned, and start-menu aligned FROM A COLD QUBE. The cell still FAILS on four P2 agent-lifecycle breaches at shutdown (agent_relaunches_at_shutdown 0.84, vchan_setups_per_shutdown 0.72, agent_instances_per_shutdown 0.64, agent_instances_per_boot 0.28) - a different defect family, untouched by this work. **ONE OF THE FOUR IS NOW DISPROVED AS A PRODUCT DEFECT: `agent_instances_per_boot` was the GRADER.** Measured on win10-clean under package 38857675399: the cell's own `report.json` lists FOUR boots (09:10:09, 09:13:43, 09:14:45, 09:15:40) and the agent log holds FOUR instances, each starting ~10 s into system uptime (LogInit uptimes 14.125, 10.750, 9.453, 10.093 s) and each exiting `QGAEXIT 0x20514703 ... an expected exit, not a failure` - one agent per boot. `boot_for()` admitted any boot up to **150 s in the FUTURE** and kept the last one admitted, and those boots are **55 and 62 s apart**, so boots 2, 3 and 4 each claimed the instance that ran before them and three of four landed in boot 4. A second defect in the same path: windows-utils writes `Log started` -> `System uptime` -> `process ID`, and the analyser OPENED the instance record on the last of those three, so each instance's boot estimate was written onto the PREVIOUS instance's record and the first instance's was dropped - a per-day log under-counted its boots by one. Both fixed with knobs seen to fail (`GUARD:fwdallow`, `GUARD:initopen`); the forward allowance is now derived from the measured boot spacing rather than a constant.
  **THE COST, recorded because it is the lesson:** five successive designs over two weeks - forced constant in the images, harness write after every install, installer constant, installer derivation from QWT presence, installer write on the clock gate's failure branch plus a per-boot convergence from a SETTIME witness - each reasoned, Jev-scored and selftested, and each managing consequences of the first write rather than its cause. The owner ended it with one question: *"why cannot we set timezone=localtime at qubes creation?"*
- RTIUFORCEDINIMAGES [harness/images; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-15] **THE VALUE THE GATE WAS MEANT TO MEASURE WAS BAKED INTO THE IMAGES AND THE GOLDENS.** All four `mgmt/autounattend*.xml` wrote `reg add ... RealTimeIsUniversal /t REG_DWORD /d 1 /f` in the specialize pass, so every image carried it, every golden sealed it and every clone inherited it. `mgmt/goldens/win10-base.json` records the reseal reason "rebuilt from vendor media 2026-10-14 with the updated answer stick (RealTimeIsUniversal=1 in the specialize pass)"; `win11de-qwt.json`'s note is literally "RealTimeIsUniversal=1". Together with HARNESSFORCEDTHECONVENTION that is three layers forcing one product setting, each with a comment explaining why it was right.
  **AND THE PREMISE WAS WRONG.** Windows' own default - the value absent, the RTC read as LOCAL time - is already the half that PAIRS with the `timezone=localtime` Qubes gives every Windows qube. The forcing did not fix a defect; it broke a working pairing, and the +3 h it produced is what every campaign since has been chasing. Jev, premise-framed on the measured mechanism: `defaults_are_already_consistent` 0.79, `forcing_vs_product_write` = stop forcing and keep a derived product write, 0.92.
  FIX: the write is out of all four answer files (the comment that replaces it says why, and `installer-clock-gate-selftest.sh` strips XML comments before asserting no file declares a convention); `mgmt/harness/set-rtc-utc-golden.sh` is DELETED; the four create-time `qvm-features --unset <vm> timezone` calls are gone; the goldens are REBUILT from vendor media with the corrected answer files rather than patched in place (owner: "redo from scratch, dont reuse"); and `tools/lint-harness.py` L26 refuses a new writer anywhere outside the product's two derived sites, seen to fail on a planted answer-file write.

- RTIUWRONGDEFAULT [product; P1, FIXED IN SOURCE - awaiting a fresh-install measurement from a REBUILT golden] [verified 2026-10-15] **WE DECLARE THE HARDWARE CLOCK HOLDS UTC, AND ON A QUBES WINDOWS QUBE IT DOES NOT.** `Install-QwtImproved.ps1` sets `RealTimeIsUniversal=1`. dom0 sets the qube's `timezone` feature to `localtime` for every `os=Windows` qube when it receives `qubes.NotifyTools` - our `advertise-tools.c` sends a `(null)` payload and cannot influence it - and that makes the RTC hold DOM0 LOCAL time. So the value we write is false in the environment the product ships into. MEASURED on the guest's own installer log: `clock: RealTimeIsUniversal already 1 (hardware clock is UTC)` and `clock: dom0 publishes no /qubes-timezone - leaving the guest timezone alone`, with `clock_boot_correction_s = 10800` in the sweep.
  **AND THE WRITE WAS INERT FOR THE CASE IT WAS ADDED FOR.** Its comment cites "Measured 2026-10-14: with it unset and the qube additionally given `timezone=localtime`, every acceptance guest ran 10800 s ahead". Windows computes UTC as `(RTC - guest_TZ_offset)` when the value is unset and as `(RTC)` when it is 1. These subjects have guest TZ = UTC (offset 0) because dom0 publishes no `/qubes-timezone`, so BOTH branches give `UTC = RTC = real + 3h`. The two configurations are arithmetically identical on the measured guest; the fix changed nothing it was introduced to change. Jev: `rtiu_fix_was_inert` 0.65, `rtiu_one_is_wrong_default` 0.75.
  **THE COMMENT ALSO CONTRADICTS THE MEASUREMENT.** Line 5282 reads "Windows with RealTimeIsUniversal unset - which is every image here", and the owner-facing refusal text at 5324-5327 is built on that premise - while the same installer sets it to 1 a hundred lines below and the guest reports it as already 1.
  **ONLY TWO CONFIGURATIONS GIVE A CORRECT CLOCK:** feature UNSET (RTC = true UTC) with `RealTimeIsUniversal=1`; or feature `localtime` with `RealTimeIsUniversal=0` AND the guest's own timezone set to dom0's offset. The second is unreachable as shipped, because dom0 publishes no `/qubes-timezone` here and the guest has no other way to learn dom0's zone - except from the real UTC it already pulls at every boot. FIX, per Jev (`best_guest_side_fix` = `derive-offset-set-tz` 0.69, vs `keep-rtiu-one-and-correct` 0.21): on the first successful time pull, derive dom0's offset as `(RTC reading - real UTC)`, set the guest's timezone to it and `RealTimeIsUniversal=0`, so every later boot is correct with no mid-boot correction. NOT APPLIED: it needs a new release package and a full re-acceptance, and `blocks_this_release` came back `block` 0.67 at conf 0.56 with `needs-owner` present - a release-scope call, not one the evidence settles. The DST question in picking a Windows zone from a derived offset is unresolved and is part of that design.

- CALLERCHECKATENDONLY [acceptance gate; P1, FIXED - the warm checks run per cell; verified in the source 2026-10-15] [verified 2026-10-15] **FIX APPLIED.** `grade_dom0_callers()` (`matrix.sh:917`) now runs both warm checks INSIDE the cell and is called from `accept_grade` at `matrix.sh:971`, i.e. next to every other per-cell assertion while the guest is still up; it reports `guest not answering` only when that particular cell's guest is really down. The cold variant keeps its end-of-campaign placement at `matrix.sh:2336`, deliberately halting one subject, because it is the clause that REQUIRES a Halted guest. This header read OPEN until 2026-10-15 although the code already carried the fix and its comment already cited run 9 - the same stale-status failure GOALVERIFY1014 was written about, and it would have been read as a reason the campaign could not settle the goal's second clause. The receipt that it works is the acceptance run, not this entry. **THE DOM0 CALLER CHANNEL IS CHECKED AFTER THE CAMPAIGN, SO ALMOST NO CELL IS COVERED.** `vmexec-channel-check.sh` is called from the END-OF-CAMPAIGN collection loop (`matrix.sh:2254`), guarded by `[ "$(w_state "$_slv")" = Running ] && w_alive "$_slv"`. Cells park, halt or remove their subjects as their last act, so by the time that loop runs only the final cell can still have a live guest; every other cell takes the `[WARN] guest not answering; the dom0 caller channel went unmeasured for this cell` branch. MEASURED: run 8's matrix log contains ZERO lines matching `VMEXEC` - the run was stopped in cell 2, before the collection phase, so the check that exists for the release goal's own clause had not executed once in eight campaign runs. The release goal is *"qrexec behavior is aligned with dom0 callers: updaters and start menu, from cold qube as well"*; a gate that measures that channel for at most one cell out of eight cannot settle it, and the same placement is waiting for `dom0-callers-check.sh`. FIX (not applied - a campaign was mid-flight and a running script is never edited): call both checks INSIDE the cell, while the guest is up and before the park, where every other per-cell assertion already runs; keep the end-of-campaign call only for the cold variant, which needs a Halted guest.

- STALEWORKTREEHIDSUITES [instrument; P2, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-15] **TWO SUITES THAT GUARD SHIPPED PATCHES COULD NOT RUN, AND READ AS PRODUCT REGRESSIONS.** `pipe-eof-selftest.sh` (4 passed, 5 failed) and `one-log-per-module-selftest.sh` (7 passed, 15 failed) reported `patch failed to apply` and `the unconditional read-failed warning is still in the patched source` - the shape of a real regression in windows-utils. Cause: `upstream/ro/qubes-windows-utils/.git/config` carried `core.worktree = /tmp/claude-1000/.../856e545c-.../scratchpad/out-m10/all`, a SESSION-SCOPED path from a long-dead session, so every `git` call against the mirror died with `fatal: Invalid path` - including `git config --unset`, which is why it had to be edited out of the file. With the line gone: 9/0 and 22/0. Nothing was wrong with the patches. A session-scoped path written into a tracked-adjacent checkout outlives the session that made it; the vendored mirror must stay self-contained.

Thirteen host-side stamps outside matrix.sh were still local: e2e-lib.sh:71's `[shot]` line written INTO the campaign log, release-acceptance.sh:283-284's `cells: feature-` footer, logvol-chain-check.sh:38, stability-e2e.sh:29, burst-quiet-ab.sh:83/86/357, seal-qwt-golden.sh:21, wedge-hunt.sh:77/95, accept-clean.sh:17's evidence dir name, e2e-wait.sh:456's `# captured` line. And "a campaign's own artefacts share one scale with the guest-side logs" was false: the guest stamps GUEST-LOCAL - the collector's BEGIN line says `tz=+03:00` on win11de-tup and `tz=+00:00` on win10-acc - which is how a stick file 42 s older than its bind read as three hours newer (AUTOPLAYTOASTTIMESTHESHELL, correction 3), and mtools writes dom0 local into the same listing (`LOGVOL TAG 2026-10-15 1:50` beside `win10-acc 2026-10-14 22:59`: nine minutes apart, three hours "later"). The same family inside the guest: run 8's health.json `"boot_skew_s":-10720, "boot_stamp_usable":false` is the guest's own clock flipping ~3 h when sync-clock-from-dom0 lands (health-check.ps1:334-337: "measured skew +103 s and +54 s before it, -10734 s and -10689 s just after"; reported, used by nothing) - the RTC read in the wrong zone, CLOCKFEATURECLEARHIDESIT's subject, not these stamps, not touched here. **FIX, moving no anchored field:** every host-side `date` stamp in mgmt/harness, tools and the e2e library is `date -u` (a VALUE change - the `[HH:MM:SS] ` and `HH:MM:SS name[x]: ` shapes that sweep-gate.sh:143/:364 anchor on are byte-identical), and the four campaign loggers (matrix.sh say, quick-upgrade.sh log, accept-clean.sh log, release-acceptance.sh say) write `clock: stamps here are UTC (<iso>Z; host local is UTC+hh:mm); guest log stamps and file names are guest-local - convert before comparing` as their FIRST line, through their own logger so it is a declared [INFO] line. Consumers enumerated and re-matched: sweep-gate.sh:143 (prefix strip), :364 (coverage census - the header counts as declared; a level-less one is seen to count as undeclared), tools/tests/sweep-gate-selftest.sh:485/:591 (say() slices end at `tee -a`, before the header), tools/stall-reference.py:34-35 and stall-matrix.py:60 (anchored on `run marker`/`install.cmd` text the header lacks), record-acceptance.sh:61 (reads only the `=== MATRIX:` footer; its selftest fixture carries no stamp). Original finding:  **ONE CAMPAIGN, TWO LOGS, THREE HOURS APART, AND NEITHER LINE SAYS WHICH.** `tools/release-acceptance.sh:75` stamps `date -u` (UTC); `mgmt/harness/matrix.sh:112` stamps `date` (local, EEST +0300). Measured side by side on the 2026-10-14 campaign: the driver wrote `[20:29:12] PASS release package verified` and the matrix wrote `[23:29:13] Gate-0 ... PASS` for events seconds apart.
  **WHY IT IS WORSE THAN UNTIDY.** Three hours is the exact magnitude of CLOCKRTCLOCALTIME, the guest-clock defect this same release spent an afternoon on, so a reader correlating the two logs sees what looks precisely like that skew. It is the same failure as CPUTWOREADERS in another place: a number whose unit is not written down is not evidence.

SERIAL by design - BLOCKCLAIMSELFTESTRACES. Two guards: a FLOOR on the suite count, so emptying the list fails instead of reporting "all passed", and an EMPTY rule, so a suite that exits 0 while reporting no assertion at all is a failure - that is how a deduped RESULT line once turned a suite into a no-op. WARM `qubes.GetAppmenus` answered 25339 bytes in 8 s, rc=0, **78 entries all parsing as receive.py parses them**, every line matched, every entry carrying a Name, no two reading alike, and both of dom0's fixed launchers reported. The policy caveat is DISPROVED by probe rather than left standing: this qube may call the service, rc=0. COLD, after a clean shutdown with Halted asserted as a positive fact: 25339 bytes in 92 s, the call itself brought the qube up (Halted -> Running), inside `qrexec_timeout=6000`.
  THE UPDATER HALF HAD NO COLD CHECK AT ALL, which is the gap this entry's own wording hid: `vmexec-channel-check.sh` was called from one place (`matrix.sh:960`) and only warm, so "from cold qube as well" read as covered because the START MENU had a cold path. Added `--cold` to that check, wired at `matrix.sh` beside the cold callers check, and measured: COLD grandchild stdout delivered, 18 bytes in 78 s, rc=0, Halted -> Running, inside qrexec_timeout; then all four channels warm (stdout, stderr, exit code 7, grandchild). **Only the FIRST call to a Halted qube is cold**, so the single cold call is the updater's GRANDCHILD shape - `powershell` under `cmd`, two deep, the measured cause of this defect - and the other three are labelled warm in the log rather than implied cold. Each caller needs its own halt, so the campaign now pays two boots once per campaign. A cold run against a Running guest is REFUSED, not relabelled. 28 selftest checks, 8 of them new and each required to distinguish a hang from a refusal from a silent empty stream.
