- CONNECTORDER [windows-utils pipe server; P1, FIXED IN SOURCE - unbuilt, unrun on a guest] [verified 2026-10-14] **A CLIENT WAS PUBLISHED TO THE SERVER'S LIST *AFTER* ITS THREADS WERE ALREADY RUNNING, AND EVERY ERROR PATH ABANDONED THE HALF-BUILT CLIENT.** The order is: malloc the client, create both buffers, set the pipes, `CreateThread` the reader, `CreateThread` the writer, and only THEN `InsertTailList`. A failure between the first `CreateThread` and the insert does `LeaveCriticalSection` and `return` - leaving the `PIPE_CLIENT`, its two `CmqCreate` buffers, its initialised `CRITICAL_SECTION`, the thread's `THREAD_PARAM` and the thread handle all unfreed, and a live reader thread looking up an id the list will never hold. Found while reviewing QREXECAGENTAV; it is the second way into that crash, because a thread started before the insert looks up an id that will never appear.
  **THE ORDER WAS THE DEFECT, SO THE FUNCTION IS REWRITTEN, NOT GUARDED.** My first attempt bolted six `goto fail` sites and an unwind onto the existing order. Each review round moved a different claim without settling the shape - `no_double_free` fell 0.49 -> 0.45 -> 0.34 as I "simplified" - and the owner stopped it: *"stop iterative fixing, it is hopeless. rewrite properly"*. He was right, and the scalar was not even tracking the question: asked to NAME the path on which something is freed twice, Jev answered `none_no_double_free` at **0.96** and preferred the local-ownership form at **1.00**.
  **THE NEW ORDER** (`patches/windows-utils-pipe-connect-rewrite.patch`): build everything that can fail while none of it is reachable -> create both threads `CREATE_SUSPENDED`, because creating a thread is not the same as letting it run -> publish the client (list insert + count) under the server lock -> resume. A thread therefore cannot run before its own client is findable: the window is not guarded, it does not exist. One failure path, reachable only while nothing is published and no thread has executed an instruction, which is what makes `TerminateThread` acceptable there and nowhere else. The server lock now covers the publish instead of spanning two mallocs, two `CmqCreate` calls and two `CreateThread` calls.
  **THE ONE NEW FAILURE MODE, FOUND BY THE REVIEW AND FIXED RATHER THAN EXPLAINED.** `ResumeThread` can fail, and a published client whose pumps never run is a silent hang - worse to diagnose than a crash. `resume_cannot_be_missed` scored 0.62 until both resumes were checked, reported at ERROR and followed by a disconnect; 0.71 after. Final per-claim: `discarding_suspended_threads_is_safe` **0.89**, `ordering_removes_the_race` **0.82**, `narrowed_lock_is_safe` **0.76**, `resume_cannot_be_missed` **0.71** - against the bolt-on draft's 0.34 / 0.45 / 0.52 / 0.55.
  **IT DOES NOT REPLACE THE NULL GUARDS.** This closes the never-published window; QREXECAGENTAV's measured crash came from the OTHER one, a client unlinked by a disconnect while its threads run, which the guards still cover. Receipt: `tools/tests/pipe-connect-order-selftest.sh`, 42 checks - the ordering invariant asserted in the source AND in both CI producers, every marker also run against unpatched source where it must fail. Unbuilt; nothing has run on a guest.
- CLOCKSYNCRC2TERMINAL [guest clock, every boot; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-14] **MY OWN REGRESSION: A FEATURE THAT HAD WORKED FOR WEEKS DIED THE DAY I MADE IT LOCALE-INDEPENDENT.** 7b7636cf replaced the puller's text matching with numeric codes - right idea - and put `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND (2)` in the terminal `dom0-refused` branch with a `break`. But 0x2 there is `QpsConnect` failing to open the **LOCAL** agent pipe (`core-agent/src/qrexec-client-vm/qrexec-client-vm.c:104`, `return win_perror("open agent pipe")`): this guest's own qrexec agent is not up yet, which is the ORDINARY state early in boot. The loop left on its first attempt, the clock was never pulled, and the ~3 h boot skew came back - **and the log line said "dom0 refused" while quoting `open agent pipe failed with error 0x2` as its own evidence.** The owner spotted it from the skew in a log I had just called a known trap: *"WHAT THE FUCK YOU FIXED THE CLOCK ISSUE WEEKS AGO"*. A working component that stops is a failure, not a capability.
  **AND THE CLIENT CANNOT REPORT A dom0 REFUSAL AT ALL.** Reading every return path in that file: 87 for its own usage errors (lines 64, 82), 14 for OOM (86), and otherwise a win_perror pass-through from converting its arguments, opening the agent pipe (104) or writing the request into it (110-134). It then **exits at line 140 without reading a response** - whether dom0 allows or refuses the service happens afterwards, between the agent and dom0, where this process cannot see it. So `rc=5 -> dom0-refused` was claiming what the source cannot support, and both that arm and the never-set `setter-refused` arm are removed. The only terminal condition left is the one that means THIS SCRIPT is wrong (87 / our own `wmain: Usage:` literal); every other rc is "the agent is not reachable yet", retried to the deadline, which reports the rc and the client's output.
  Proof it is a race and not a refusal, from ONE build on 2026-10-14: one boot logged `CLOCKSYNC dom0 refused ... open agent pipe failed with error 0x2`, another logged `CLOCKSYNC the clock was pulled from dom0 at boot`.
  **THE SUITE WAS GREEN THROUGHOUT (18/18)**, because it checked that the exits are NAMED and that failure is LOUD and never which CODE leads to which exit - and two of its checks *required* the two unreachable arms, holding the puller to claims it could not produce. Now 23 checks pin each code to its exit, assert no exit claims a dom0 refusal, assert no code is classified without a receipt in the client's source, and read the script's CODE rather than the comments describing it (two of them first passed on my own comment quoting the shape it replaced - the same trap as a scripted edit landing inside its own explanation).
  **This shipped in the release package under acceptance** (7b7636cf is an ancestor of ffd22d94f5d1), so the campaign was validating a build with this regression in it; it was stopped, and the package must be rebuilt before acceptance means anything.
- SWEEPUTF16BLIND [error gate; P2, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-14] **A UTF-16 LOG IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY REGEX, AND WINDOWS WRITES IT BY DEFAULT.** PowerShell 5.1's `>` redirection produces UTF-16LE, so a log the harness pulls that way reads as binary in `tools/sweep-gate.sh` - each character followed by a NUL, no pattern matching. 3 of 86 preserved files on the 2026-10-14 campaign were UTF-16, including `bind-dirs.log`, which is ours; none happened to carry a declared error that run, which is the only kind of hole worth closing before it has something in it. Decoded by BOM at every read site now. The ANALYZER path was never affected: `log-sweep-collect.ps1` reads guest files through PowerShell, which honours the BOM, and the collected copies arrive as UTF-8 - confirmed by `bind-dirs.log`'s content appearing correctly in a real report.
  Found while attributing the gap the gate names: of 3555 unlevelled lines, **3047 are msiexec's own verbose log** and only 508 are ours. "3558 lines carry no declared level" read as 3558 lines of our own undisciplined logging; the coverage line now separates third-party from ours, by the file's own header rather than its name.
  Two more in the same pass: the census disagreed with the tool on EVERY real guest log (awk 52 distinct vs 48) because `[ \t]` is not `\s` and the guest's logs are CRLF - a cross-check that always says "one of them is wrong" cannot report a real disagreement, and T14 passed throughout on a fixture of mine with Unix newlines. And a UTF-8 BOM in front of a timestamp kept both sides' prefix rules from anchoring. Both fixed; the two implementations now AGREE on 47 distinct declared lines over a real campaign.
- PIPEFAILGREPQFILEREAD [harness; P2, FIXED - all 6 sites] [verified 2026-10-14] **A FILE READ PIPED INTO `grep -q` RETURNS 141 UNDER pipefail, NOT grep's STATUS.** (Verified by reproducing the mechanism in sweep-gate.sh and reading each site.) `grep -q` exits on the first match, the upstream reader dies of SIGPIPE, and pipefail hands the pipeline that 141 - so the caller reads "no match" on a file that matched. It fires only when the upstream has more to write than the pipe buffer holds AND the match comes early, which is why it hides. L23 forbids this shape for a GUEST PROBE (where it graded a live guest dead 86/200); it is not specific to guests, and I reintroduced it myself today in `sweep-gate.sh`, where it silently reported 0 of 3047 third-party lines and stopped the tool skipping its own reports - on a 1.5 MB log, with every fixture small enough to pass. Fixed there.
  ALL SIX NOW FIXED, each read before being changed: `p3a-etw-gate.sh:530`, `:532`, `:607`, `:751`, `quick-upgrade.sh:607` (`log_has_progress_after`), and `run-lib.sh:194` - the job-lifecycle library every harness inherits, where `run_verified` waits for a start marker in a log that is STILL BEING WRITTEN, so an early match made a job that HAD started report as never having started. Each now counts with `grep -c`, which reads to EOF and closes nothing. Receipt: `tools/tests/pipefail-grepq-selftest.sh` demonstrates the mechanism rather than describing it - the same pipeline returns 141 with `grep -q` and 0 when counted, on a fixture past the pipe buffer - and asserts the shape is gone from all three files. **A lint rule for the general shape was written and WITHDRAWN**: measured 1 true positive, 2 false, and 5 real sites missed - a rule that wrong is worse than this list, and tuning its regex to make the count look better is the thing not to do. Fix the six, then decide whether a rule can be made precise.
- MATRIXMARKERNORETRY [acceptance harness, every reinstall/upgrade cell; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-14] **THE RUN-MARKER STEP FAILED A CELL ON THE FIRST SILENT ANSWER, AND READ ITS OWN ECHO.** `matrix.sh` wrote a unique marker into the guest log, read it back once, and gave up; it also took the answer with `grep -ao 'PRESENT\|ABSENT' | head -1`, and `qtest run` echoes the prompt line WITH THE COMMAND ON IT - that command text contains both words, so the read reported PRESENT off the echo whenever the guest answered at all, and empty when it did not. Wrong in both directions.
  `quick-upgrade.sh` - the SIBLING implementation of the same step - has carried both fixes since 2026-09-14, after this read got 'no answer' while the guest measured cpu_time +66935 over 20 s, busy mid-install. Neither was ported. COST, on the 2026-10-14 acceptance campaign: WIN10-reinstall took 'no answer' three seconds after a successful guest read (`release disc verified at E:`), refused to run the install, and then graded itself INVALID-INSTRUMENT twice - over an install that had never been attempted. Fixed: one shared `_mark_seen` reader that strips the banner and takes the guest's last word, plus the retry-while-cpu_time-moves loop with a FLAT exit. Receipt: `tools/tests/matrix-runmarker-selftest.sh`, 8 checks, the first of which asserts the OLD reader's false PRESENT.
- LOGVOLLEVELSDISCARDED [harness instrumentation, 5 call sites; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-14] **A DECLARED SEVERITY SENT TO /dev/null DECIDES NOTHING.** `logvol-lib.sh` now declares `[ERROR]`/`[WARN]` at every failure site so `tools/sweep-gate.sh`, which reads only declared severity, can see them - and `_lv_say` writes to STDERR, which `matrix.sh` and `quick-upgrade.sh` threw away at 5 of 6 call sites (`logvol_create`, `logvol_attach`, `logvol_pull`). So every level declared in those functions was inert, and a cell recorded "log volume not attached" while the reason was discarded. Same shape as a predicate fed a constant: the code reads correct and decides nothing. Found by asking Jev whether the level reaches the reader (0.34), not by reading the diff. Fixed: each site captures into a `.out` under the campaign dir, which the end-of-campaign glob already reads, and the reason is quoted in the line. Also fixed: 6 failure returns in that library that logged NOTHING at all, including two where the guest's own non-ok bind receipt was dropped - the LOGVOLBINDUPGRADE shape exactly. Guest text in a severity-declaring line is now flattened and de-bracketed (`_lv_guest`), because an untrusted reply carrying `[ERROR]` or a newline could forge or mask a level. Receipts: `tools/tests/logvol-level-reaches-gate-selftest.sh` (7 checks, library -> capture -> glob -> gate), `tools/tests/logvol-guesttext-selftest.sh` (9).
- SWEEPGRADEDDOUBLE [acceptance harness tally; P2, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-14] **ONE FAILURE, TWO TALLY ENTRIES, AND THE WRONG CLASS ON THE SECOND.** `no()` increments the campaign tally and writes the failure into the campaign log as `[ERROR] FAIL  <what>` in the same breath; the end-of-campaign gate then swept that log, read our own graded verdict as an UNACCOUNTED error, and raised a second entry classed INVALID - which asserts that nothing graded it. Measured on a fixture. The fork was put to Jev with the project's rules as premises: `drop` 0.36, `keep` 0.01, **`keep_but_reclass` 0.63** - still print the line, because a declared error that stops being shown is how the original incident happened, but do not move the counter for a verdict already graded. Implemented as Jev chose; the committed corpus entry asserting these lines MUST be detected still passes. Receipt: `tools/tests/matrix-sweep-tally-selftest.sh`, 7 checks.
- LOGSWEEPOLDEMPTY [log analyzer; P2, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-14] **AN EMPTY ROTATION PLACEHOLDER IS NOT MISSING DATA.** `guest/provision-etwproxy-account.ps1` PRE-CREATES both `etw-proxy.log` and `etw-proxy.log.old` as empty files, because the ACE for the rotation rename needs DELETE on each name; the logger only rotates past ~1 MB (`qtb_shared.h`). `tools/log-sweep.py` read the 0-byte `.old` as missing data, so EVERY cell of the 2026-10-14 campaign graded rc=3 DATA - over one placeholder, while 37 files and 2631 QWT-format lines sat in the same directory. A standing false alarm on every cell is how a gate stops being read. Fixed with a rule, not a name allowlist: exempt only when the live counterpart was collected AND its GUEST-side size is under the threshold; an empty `.old` beside a live log already past it, or with no live counterpart, or whose size is absent or unparseable, still fails. Receipt: `tools/tests/logsweep-rotation-placeholder-selftest.sh`, 7 checks pinning both directions.

- HARNESSLOGUNSWEPT [harness instrumentation, every suite; P1, FIXED for the short cycle] [verified 2026-10-14] **NOTHING SWEPT THE HARNESSES' OWN LOGS, SO ERROR LINES WERE GRADED BY WHOEVER HAPPENED TO READ THEM.** `mgmt/harness/log-sweep.sh` does this properly for the GUEST's logs - and **50 of the harnesses that drive a guest never call it**, including `matrix.sh`, `quick-upgrade.sh`, `wu-e2e.sh` and `template-update-test.sh`, which is every harness that grades a release. Measured by counting `vm_lock` callers without a `log-sweep` reference.
  WHAT THAT COST, 2026-10-14: an upgrade log carried `WARNING: ... records no sealed_version - ordering unchecked` and four lines of `no qrexec for 135s (cause UNKNOWN - this sampler cannot see why)`, and I called the first unimportant and the second "expected installer behaviour" WITHOUT checking either. The install was in fact fine - which is luck, not method. The owner's words: *"you ignored the error message. NEVER ignore error messages"*, then *"WTF RATIONALIZED! HARDWIRE ERROR LOG SWEEP"*.
- VMEXECNOSTDOUT [qrexec transport, ALL dom0 tooling; P1, FIXED ON A GUEST 2026-10-14 - see GOALVERIFY1014 below; the release-wide receipt is the acceptance run] [verified 2026-10-14] **`qubes.VMExec` ON WINDOWS RUNS THE COMMAND, RETURNS ITS EXIT CODE, AND DISCARDS ALL STDOUT AND STDERR.** Measured on win11de-tup (German 25H2, 26200.9711), four ways:
  (1) `qubes.VMExec` + `cmd /c echo HELLO-VMEXEC` -> rc=0, stdout `b''`, stderr `b''`; the same echo over `qubes.VMShell` returns it correctly.
  (2) `cmd /c exit 100` over VMExec -> **rc=100**, so our exit-code propagation works and the handler definitely ran.
  (3) THE DECISIVE ONE: `cmd /c echo PROBE-TO-FILE > C:\vmexec-probe.txt & echo PROBE-TO-STDOUT` -> rc=0, stdout empty, and the FILE afterwards contains `PROBE-TO-FILE`. The command executed; only its output vanished. The child was cmd, not PowerShell, so this is not language-specific.
  (4) The service's own command line (`powershell -noninteractive -file VMExec.ps1 "<encoded>"`) run BY HAND over VMShell prints its output normally - so PowerShell and our `VMExec.ps1` are not at fault.
  NOT OURS: `C:\Program Files\Qubes Tools\qubes-rpc\qubes.VMExec` is **byte-identical** to the `.qwt-stock` backup beside it, so this is stock QWT behaviour that our fork inherited (do-not-chase-stock-bugs applies only until a stock bug blocks us - this one does).
  **CAUSE CONFIRMED 2026-10-14: a52441a's `DETACHED_PROCESS`, but ONLY once the child has a GRANDCHILD.** The wrapper's child is always `cmd.exe /c powershell.exe ...`, i.e. two processes deep. Measured by P/Invoke on the guest, mirroring QwCreateProcessAsSessionUser exactly (console-session token, `CreateProcessAsUser`, pipes, `STARTF_USESTDHANDLES`), with the creation flag as the ONLY variable:
```
cmd /c echo        (builtin, no 2nd process)  NO_WINDOW 16-21 B ok   DETACHED 21 B ok
cmd /c cmd /c echo (a grandchild)             NO_WINDOW 21 B    ok   DETACHED  0 B, rc=0xC0000142
```
  With no console on the parent, the grandchild either fails DLL init (STATUS_DLL_INIT_FAILED) or is given a FRESH console - and a new console OVERRIDES inherited std handles. The guest confirms the second shape directly: a diagnostic run as the service child reported `file_type=2` (FILE_TYPE_CHAR, a console) for its own stdout, `WriteFile_ok=True wrote=22` - it wrote 22 bytes successfully into a console nobody reads, which is why the wrapper's pump logged `StdoutThread: sending stdout EOF` with `msg 0x191, size 0` and never a data message.
  TWO EARLIER ATTRIBUTIONS OF MINE WERE WRONG AND ARE RETRACTED: "stock QWT behaviour that always existed" (the service file is stock, the HANDLER is ours, and 4.3.36 is the first release carrying the change), and then "DETACHED_PROCESS is exonerated" - which came from a probe whose child was a cmd BUILTIN with no second process. Jev tracked both: 0.92 for the flag on the first state, 0.00 and `insufficient-evidence` 0.88 once the builtin probe was in evidence, and the grandchild measurement is what settles it. EXCLUDED BY MEASUREMENT, not argument: `CreateProcessAsUser`/session-token (delivers output), stdin EOF timing (DEVNULL and held-open-12s both 0 bytes), pump threads failing to start (they run and close cleanly), pipe creation and ACLs.
  IT DOES NOT ALIGN WITH DOM0'S UPDATE CONTRACT ON ANY CHANNEL BUT THE EXIT CODE: dom0 takes `stdout = logs` (our shim's own header cites vmupdate/agent/source/common/exit_codes.py), parses bare float lines 0..100 on STDERR for the Qubes Update GUI's progress bar (measured: `progress floats seen on stderr: []`, so a Windows qube shows no progress at all), and cats `/var/log/qubes/qubes-update/update-agent.log` as its final step while `update_manager.py` says "critical -> always write agent logs". We deliver none of the three.
  WHERE IT IS NOT: `core-agent/src/qrexec-wrapper/qrexec-wrapper.c` looks correct on inspection - `piped = !!(flags & 0x02)` and the wrapper log shows flags `6`, so piped is TRUE; `CreateChildPipes` runs; `QwCreateProcessAsSessionUser` sets `STARTF_USESTDHANDLES` with all three handles and `inheritHandles=TRUE`; and the parent closes its copies of the child's ends after launch. So the loss is NOT the handle wiring. The remaining suspects are the `DETACHED_PROCESS` flag that path passes (the SYSTEM path uses `CreatePipedProcessAsCurrentUser` instead, and SYSTEM calls do return output) and the vchan pump for the piped case. NOT YET LOCATED - do not claim it is.
  WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND THE UPDATER: dom0 drives the agent run over `qubes.VMExec` with no feature check and no fallback, so NO text can reach dom0 from a Windows guest on that path - not the updater's log, not the shim's own lines (`mkdir`, `tar`, `rm` all write output and all of it was absent from dom0's transcript), and not dom0's step-6 `cat` of the agent log. Any dom0 tool that reads VMExec output is affected.
  OUR SIDE ALREADY IMPLEMENTS ALL THREE CHANNELS - THE TRANSPORT IS THE ONLY BLOCKER. Verified in source 2026-10-14: `guest/wu-update.ps1`'s `Prog` writes `$Err.WriteLine([string]::Format([Globalization.CultureInfo]::InvariantCulture,'{0:0.0}',$p))` - bare floats on STDERR, locale-proof (a German guest's "75,0" would make dom0's `float(line)` raise, and that was already fixed); the pass's log goes to stdout; and the shim's `cat` branch answers dom0's agent-log read from our log plus `update-status.json`. So nothing new has to be written for dom0 - two channels are discarded in transit and the third was mis-routed.
  THE MIS-ROUTING IS FIXED (84117a38): `guest/VMExec.ps1`'s predicate now also matches `qubes-update[/\]update-agent\.log`, matched on the LOG FILE rather than the directory because the shim's default branch is a no-op and a wide predicate would silently swallow a caller's command. `tools/tests/vmexec-dispatch-test.ps1` reads the pattern out of the file so it cannot drift: 9/9 over dom0's five routed steps plus near-misses that must still reach cmd, and with `-Defect` it fails exactly the agent-log case.
  THE COLD-QUBE CLAUSE, NOW VERIFIED ON THE CURRENT BUILD (2026-10-17, win11de-cold = win11de-qwt
  upgraded to 4.3.36+agent.bbf26ae, `mgmt/harness/dom0-callers-check.sh --cold`): `qubes.GetAppmenus
  answered: 26173 bytes in 83s (cold, rc=0)`, the cold call STARTED the qube (Halted -> Running),
  79 entries, every line parses as receive.py parses it, every entry carries a Name, no two read
  alike, and both of dom0's fixed launchers are reported - `the start-menu caller is aligned
  (cold, 79 entries, 83s)`. Warm on the same guest: aligned, 7s. The updater caller on the same
  build (`vmexec-channel-check.sh`): stdout 19 B, stderr 20 B, exit code passed through, and stdout
  delivered FROM A GRANDCHILD - the updater's own shape - all four channel checks passed.
  THE SAME CHECK FAILED AN HOUR EARLIER AND THAT IS WHY THIS ONE COUNTS: run against a clone of the
  4.3.29 golden it returned `0 bytes with a SILENT stderr (cold, rc=46, 58s) - dom0 would show an
  empty menu`, plus a duplicate menu label warm. That golden was tagged 2026-09-12 and the fix
  (core-agent 9ad4dfc, since renamed QwWaitForInteractiveLogon) landed 2026-10-11, so the failure
  was a month-old build, not the product - my measurement, caught by checking the subject's version
  AFTER reporting it as a product defect. The wait is present in the SHIPPED wrapper, read out of
  the binary rather than the source: "waiting for interactive logon (%lu of %lu ms)" /
  "no interactive logon after %u ms; service '%s' as '%s' is not started".
  NOT COVERED BY TODAY'S RUN: vmexec-channel-check.sh has no cold mode, so the updater's COLD leg
  still rests on the 2026-10-14 reading (`COLD VMExec rc=0 elapsed=85.9s stdout=14`).
  PREVIOUS STATE, kept because it is what the entry was opened for: core-agent `9ad4dfc` (2026-10-11) added `QwWaitForConsoleLogon`, a 90 s wait before a user-context service, which is the shape `STARTAPPCOLDSILENT` needs - and `qrexec_timeout` is 600 s on these guests (6000 on win11-app), so dom0 was never the party giving up at 70 s and the wait has room to finish. Whether a cold app-menu launch now succeeds is UNMEASURED; `scratchpad/cold-start-test.sh` grades it on rc, elapsed time, whether stderr carries anything, and whether the wrapper logged the wait instead of failing silently.

  A SECOND, SMALLER DEFECT THAT IS OURS, found alongside: `guest/VMExec.ps1`'s dispatch predicate is `/run/qubes-update|\run\qubes-update|entrypoint\.py`, which does NOT match dom0's step 6, `cat /var/log/qubes/qubes-update/update-agent.log`. Proven by the guest's own `vmupdate-shim.log`, which records mkdir, tar, entrypoint and rm for the round and **no `cat` entry at all** - so the shim's purpose-built `cat` branch (the one that emits our log AND `update-status.json` to dom0) has never once run. It is a one-line predicate fix, but it is moot until VMEXECNOSTDOUT is fixed, because its output would be discarded too.

- WUFIRSTCONTACTSILENT [updater, dom0-facing; P1, FIXED ON A GUEST 2026-10-14 with its root cause VMEXECNOSTDOUT - see GOALVERIFY1014 below] [verified 2026-10-14] ROOT CAUSE IS VMEXECNOSTDOUT above, not the updater: the reason IS produced and is simply unable to cross the transport. **ON FIRST CONTACT THE UPDATER DIAGNOSES ITS OWN FAILURE PERFECTLY AND TELLS DOM0 NOTHING.** Measured on GWeck's exact environment (env-assert passed on every asserted fact: TemplateVM, de-DE, build 26200.8037, netvm='', no `user` account, Open-Shell, service.enableWinKey=1), round 1 of 3 in `mgmt/harness/wu-e2e.sh`.
  WHAT THE GUEST KNOWS, from `C:\ProgramData\Qubes\wu\agent.log`: *"Windows Update failed with 0x8024402C - it could not resolve its service-registration host - while this pass PROVED the update proxy usable at that same moment: WinHTTP through http://127.0.0.1:8082 reached the same endpoint and returned HTTP 200. So Windows Update did not use the configured proxy for that call. Nothing was searched and no update state was reported to dom0. A RESTART CLEARS THIS IMMEDIATELY (measured), so this pass requests one"* - plus `ERROR-SITE line 848` (`$se.Search`) and a full stack. The restart was requested, taken, and round 2 searched normally; rounds 2 and 3 PASSED with oscillation OK and reboot accounting exact (2 performed = 2 requested).
  WHAT DOM0 RECEIVES: `[entrypoint] rc=1`, **EMPTY stdout, EMPTY stderr** (`progress floats seen on stderr: []`), and an **EMPTY** `/var/log/qubes/qubes-update/update-agent.log` - empty in all three rounds, pass and fail alike. dom0's `updates-available` was unchanged before and after. dom0's documented reading of rc=1 is "a KB failed", so dom0 recorded a failed KB with no reason attached.
  THE CONTRACT IS OURS AND WE WROTE IT DOWN: `guest/vmupdate-shim.ps1`'s header states *"The contract we must honour (verified in vmupdate/agent/source/common/exit_codes.py): progress = bare float lines 0..100 on STDERR, stdout = logs"*, and the shim mentions the dom0-facing log only in that header - it never writes it (no redirect, Out-File, Set-Content or Add-Content to it in 144 lines). dom0's side is not in doubt: `upstream/ro/qubes-core-admin-linux/vmupdate/update_manager.py` carries *"critical -> always write agent logs"* and `agent/source/log_config.py` sets `LOG_FILE = "update-agent.log"`.
  NOT A 4.3.36 REGRESSION, AND THAT IS THE POINT: template-update was recorded COVERED(pass) **8 times between 2026-10-08 and 2026-10-10 with the Open-Shell assertion already in place** - those runs never exercised first contact. The gap is long-standing and was exposed the first time a run hit it, which is what the suite exists for. Jev: `defect_class=product` **0.87** (an earlier pass, before dom0's code and the empty-stdout fact were measured, said instrument 0.67 - the flip came from reading `update_manager.py`), `severity_p1` **0.73**, `blocks_the_cut` 0.37. It is filed P1 under the owner's standing rule: the updater is NOT fixed while it conceals.
  THE GATE REFUSED CORRECTLY: the acceptance was not recorded, `template-update` is uncovered, and `gate-scope check` fails - so `tools/cut-release.sh` will not publish 4.3.36.
  SELF-INFLICTED HALF OF THE SAME EVENING: the FIRST acceptance run failed this suite as rc=2 INSTRUMENT, not for this defect, but because `tools/purge-run-bulk.sh` had deleted `scratchpad/openshell/OpenShellSetup_4_4_198.exe` - an `.exe` under scratchpad, past the age guard, matched by no keep rule - so the reporter environment could not be built and a required gate suite was taken out by a disk cleanup. The installer is re-fetched (sha256 a4d2d4459de55b5e962ba2a14f7bb794170511649138173dfa72949837b48c3f), `scratchpad/openshell` is now a keep path, and `--apply` writes a manifest of what it deleted so the question is answerable from a record rather than by inference.

- PROBESIGPIPE [harness liveness, every suite; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-14] (by measurement off-rig; not yet exercised on a guest) **A GUEST THAT ANSWERED WAS GRADED DEAD, INTERMITTENTLY, BY EVERY LIVENESS PROBE IN THE RIG.** `producer | grep -qa MARKER`: grep -q exits on the first match, the still-writing producer dies of SIGPIPE, `timeout` reports that as 141, and `set -o pipefail` makes 141 the status of the whole pipeline. Counts on a HEALTHY producer, same machine: **86 of 200** in collect-failure's probe shape, **41 of 300** and **8 of 150** in two arrangements of `w_alive`. The same command without the pipe: rc=0 on 60 of 60. Pipeline statuses observed were exactly {0, 141}.
  WHAT RESTED ON IT: `w_alive` (`mgmt/harness/e2e-wait.sh`) is the project's canonical liveness predicate, and `w_session`, `w_install`, `w_halt_stable` and a 10-minute `TERMINAL` verdict all call it. e2e-wait.sh sets no shell options itself - it inherited pipefail from `matrix.sh`, `prime-run.sh`, `quick-upgrade.sh` and `collect-failure.sh`, all of which set it. When the collector graded a healthy guest unresponsive it classified a wedge and fetched a **multi-GB memory image of a healthy guest**.
  FIXED at 11 sites by capturing and matching - `grep -qa X <<< "$(producer)"`, or a variable plus `case`: e2e-wait.sh (w_alive), collect-failure.sh, prime-run.sh (x3), stability-e2e.sh, failproof-classpairs.sh, sg1-u2-coldboot.sh, ipi-storm-hunt.sh, idd-cycle-hunt.sh, upgrade-loop-hunt.sh, tools/netvm-bootwatch.sh. Measured after: **0 of 300** and **0 of 200**. The substitution completes before grep starts, so there is no pipe to break; every producer is already `timeout`-bounded, so the wait is bounded too. Not a latency cost either - against a producer that answers then writes for 6 s, the pipe form took 6011 ms AND returned 141, the capture form 6013 ms and rc=0.
  CAPTURED AND LARGE OUTPUT IS NOT A CONCERN: 1 KB 11 ms, 1 MB 25 ms, 20 MB 340 ms, all rc=0; these probes emit a banner and one word.
  **IT WAS FOUND IN THIS REPO ON 2026-08-31 AND NEVER SWEPT FOR.** `tools/tests/lint-selftest.sh`'s own `expect_fires` helper carries the note - *"CAPTURE FIRST ... `lint | grep -q` reports the LINT's status, not grep's - so every case 'failed' while the lint was in fact firing correctly"* - and the identical shape sat in the liveness probes for six weeks. Now gated: **lint rule L23** refuses a guest probe piped into `grep -q`, scoped to files where pipefail is in effect (set directly, or inherited by being sourced from a file that sets it), with three self-test cases - fires on the defect, silent on capture-then-match AND on the same pipe where pipefail is ABSENT (there the status is grep's own and the shape is correct), and fires through a sourced library. No baseline entries were added; the rule is clean.

- PWFUSE42 [rig images, blocks acceptance; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-11] **EVERY WINDOWS IMAGE CARRIED A 42-DAY PASSWORD FUSE, AND THE WIN10 ONE WENT OFF TODAY.** Windows' default `Maximum password age` is 42 days; the account each image is built with inherits it, so every image holds an expiry fixed at build time. MEASURED: win10 password set 30/08/2026, machine policy `Maximum password age (days): 42` -> due **11/10/2026**. On that boot Windows sits on "Your password has expired", autologon cannot proceed, and although the qrexec agent runs as a service every USER-context service fails - the guest is effectively unreachable. The owner hit it as *"win10-acc stuck on Your password has expired"*, then *"on second run it logged in"*: a boot-time task asserts PasswordNeverExpires afterwards, so it costs exactly ONE stuck boot per image, on a date nobody chose. `win11-tpl` carried the same 42-day age, so it was never Win10-specific.
  **IT WAS A KNOWN, DATED FUSE THAT NOTHING ACTED ON.** `guest/set-autologon.ps1:176` recorded it a month ago, to the day: *'win10 test image (net accounts "Maximum password age (days): 42"; net user: set 30/08/2026, expires 11/10/2026)'*. A private work-queue note carried "base password fix by 10-11". It went off on schedule.
  WHY THE EXISTING SCRIPTS DID NOT SAVE IT: `set-autologon.ps1:186` and `ensure-autologon.ps1:67` both set PasswordNeverExpires, but they run AFTER QWT is installed - far too late to beat the logon screen on a first boot. And the clean-install path CLONES a base and boots it rather than reinstalling, so a blown fuse in a base breaks every cell entering through it.
  FIXED in three places, all with the same sequence: `mgmt/autounattend*.xml` (x4, FirstLogonCommands - so new images carry no fuse), every `mgmt/prime-jobs/*/onboot.cmd` (x5 - that hook runs as SYSTEM at boot, the only moment that beats the logon screen), and `guest/clear-password-fuse.ps1` shipped in a dedicated `pwfix` job for the QWT-less bases.
```
net accounts /maxpwage:unlimited
per enabled account:  net user <n> /logonpasswordchg:no   THEN   Set-LocalUser -PasswordNeverExpires $true
```
  THREE ERRORS JEV CAUGHT BEFORE ANY OF IT REACHED A GUEST, each of which would have shipped a fix that did nothing:
  (1) `wmic useraccount ... set PasswordExpires=FALSE` scored **0.00** - wmic is deprecated and ABSENT from Windows 11 26100/26300, so it would have SILENTLY no-opped on exactly the images that matter (risk 0.87). `win11r-base`'s own seal note says the same thing independently: *"its WinPE has no WMIC"*.
  (2) the latched "must change at next logon" is a SEPARATE state the never-expires flag does not reliably undo - named the most likely thing to leave a guest stuck (**0.82**).
  (3) ORDER: "must change at next logon" and "password never expires" are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE, so `Set-LocalUser -PasswordNeverExpires` THROWS while the latch stands - with the original order an account sitting at the wall took the throw and KEPT its fuse (`order_is_right` 0.89 after correcting).
  On the owner's question - does it cure a wall that is already up, not merely prevent one - `flag_cures_age_expiry` **0.81**. For the residual, the script re-sets an account that survives all three levers to the password the image ALREADY stores (Winlogon `DefaultPassword`), which moves pwdLastSet; it never guesses a credential, because setting one the image does not expect would break autologon outright.
  VERIFICATION IS BAKED INTO THE FIX, not bolted on: it writes BEFORE/AFTER - machine age and every enabled account's PasswordExpires - to `C:\qubes-pwfix.log` inside the image, so the evidence travels with the image instead of living in a harness.
  SEALS: applying this BOOTS the images, and `mgmt/golden.sh`'s rule is absolute - *"a golden that has been booted is not a golden any more ... re-seal it deliberately or rebuild it"*. `win11r-qwt`, `win11de-qwt` and all four bases are re-sealed with a note naming the fuse and the date. Without that the custody check fails CLOSED and every clean-install cell dies with "failed its seal check" - a cause that looks nothing like a password fuse.

- GOALVERIFY1014 [qrexec/app-menu, user-facing; THE WHOLE QREXEC CLAUSE OF THE GOAL IS CLOSED - updaters on all three channels, and the UWP start menu warm AND cold] [verified 2026-10-14] **ALL FOUR CLAUSES ARE VERIFIED ON THAT GUEST.** This sentence read *"the transport clauses are verified; the APP-MENU clause is NOT"* until 2026-10-15, when the owner asked whether VMEXECNOSTDOUT had been closed. It had - by point 4 of this very entry, which measures the menu launching a UWP app warm AND cold and says in its own heading that the earlier negative was MY measurement rather than the product. The opening sentence was simply never updated as the measurements landed, and two issue headers above still read OPEN because of it. A status that contradicts the evidence under it is worse than no status: it was cited as a live P1 a day later. Short cycle `quick-upgrade.sh ~/rel/setup-38550854756 win11de-goal win11de` over the sealed win11de-qwt golden (4.3.29 -> 4.3.36, in-place MajorUpgrade, 11 passed / 0 failed), then `scratchpad/goal-verify.sh`:
  **1. UPDATERS - FIXED, measured.** All five of dom0's steps over `qubes.VMExec` return rc=0 WITH output: `[mkdir] [cat>tarball] [tar] [rm] [cat log]`. The last one is the step-6 agent-log read that had NEVER run (it fell through to cmd.exe, which has no `cat`). That closes VMEXECNOSTDOUT on a guest. **ALL THREE CHANNELS NOW MEASURED, including the one I first reported as merely un-exercised.** The owner pushed back on that framing - "no floats means it is still broken, irregardless of how you dress this up?" - and he was right that an undemonstrated channel is not a verified one. Re-run WITH `--with-entrypoint`, which drives a real pass: `progress floats seen on stderr: ['0.0', '1.0', '100.0']` - the bare 0..100 lines dom0's progress bar parses. And `[cat log] rc=0` returned the whole update-status JSON including `"error": "Windows Update failed with 0x8024402C - it could not resolve its service-registration host - while this pass PROVED the update proxy usable at that same moment"`. That is WUFIRSTCONTACTSILENT's exact complaint - the updater diagnosing itself perfectly and telling dom0 nothing - and dom0 is now told. Previously: rc=1, EMPTY stdout, EMPTY stderr and an EMPTY agent log in all three rounds.
  (The first measurement of this said "progress floats seen: []" because the replay skipped the entrypoint, and the first re-run threw the answer away: I piped it through `tail -20`, which cut off the very line being measured.)
  **2. COLD QUBE - the transport and the diagnostic both work.** From Halted: `COLD VMExec rc=0 elapsed=85.9s stdout=14` - output delivered from a cold qube, where STARTAPPCOLDSILENT used to be rc=46 after 70 s with EMPTY stderr. `COLD StartApp rc=1 elapsed=6.3s stderr=406` carrying the precise diagnostic, in 6 seconds instead of 70 of silence.
  **3. THE HKLM MIRROR WORKS.** After running the guest side of the sync: `HKCU: entries=73` and `HKLM: entries=73` - identical, so [[APPMAPWRONGHIVE]]'s fix does what it claims and a TEMPLATE sync will now reach its appvms through the inherited hive.
  **4. THE MENU LAUNCHES A UWP APP, WARM AND COLD - the earlier negative was MY measurement, not the product.** Invoked the way dom0 does (`qrexec-client-vm win11de-goal qubes.GetAppmenus`, i.e. the user context dom0 requests, NOT my SYSTEM qtest path): rc=0, 284 lines, 38 desktop entries, including
```
Rechner.desktop:Name=Rechner
Rechner.desktop:Exec=qubes-rpc-multiplexer qubes.StartApp+Rechner
Rechner.desktop:Comment=Microsoft.WindowsCalculator_8wekyb3d8bbwe!App
```
  So `SweepStoreApps` enumerates AppsFolder correctly and the AUMID lands in the map. Then the launch, and PIXELS rather than an ack: WARM `qubes.StartApp+Rechner` rc=0 -> `CalculatorApp.exe` pid 1996 session 1 -> 1 mapped window. COLD, from Halted: rc=0 in **59 s** -> `CalculatorApp.exe` pid 5548 session 1 -> **1 mapped window**. That closes [[APPMENUNOUWP]] and the start-menu clause of the goal, from a cold qube as the owner specified.
  The entry is **Rechner**, not Calculator: my first check grepped for "alculator" on a German guest and reported `calculator=False`, which is the same locale trap the owner had just ruled out of the product. And `AppsFolder items: 0` was measured under SYSTEM via VMShell - a context dom0 never uses - so it said nothing about the product, exactly as it was flagged at the time rather than afterwards.
  ONE REAL CONDITION REMAINS AROUND IT, not a defect in this path: the first per-window capture after the warm launch came back EMPTY, which is the shape a missing `win-idd-testbed` tag also has. Excluded by checking: the subject carries the tag, and a re-capture a moment later returned 1 window - the UWP window simply had not mapped yet.
  **4b. SUPERSEDED - what this bullet said before the dom0-context test.** `calculator=False` in both hives after the sync, and only THREE `shell:AppsFolder\` targets in 73 entries (MicrosoftWindows.Client.CBS, CoreAI, immersivecontrolpanel). Measured directly: `(New-Object -ComObject Shell.Application).NameSpace("shell:AppsFolder").Items().Count` returns **0** in the context the sync ran (SYSTEM, via qubes.VMShell). So `SweepStoreApps` enumerates nothing from that context and the three entries it did write come from some other path. NOT ESTABLISHED, and it is the next question: dom0's own `qubes.GetAppmenus` call may run as the USER rather than SYSTEM, in which case the enumeration might succeed there and this measurement says nothing about it. [[APPMENUNOUWP]] stays open with this as its sharper cause.
  **5. PACKAGING: ours ships, resolution still open.** Both binaries are installed - `bin\get-image-rgba.exe` is ours (9AA1277E) and `qubes-rpc-services\get-image-rgba.exe` is stock (262BD94D). `qubes.GetImageRGBA` runs the BARE command, so which one dom0 resolves is still undetermined; presence is proven, resolution is not.
  **6. THE SWEEP GATE EARNED ITS KEEP ON ITS FIRST LIVE RUN.** That 11-passed/0-failed cycle carried three unaccounted declared errors in its own log, which the gate surfaced: `[WARN] log-volume bind failed - logs stay on C: and a wedge loses them` (LOGVOLBINDUPGRADE recurring on an upgrade cell, live) and two `[ERROR] no qrexec for 35s/68s - and the log volume could not be read either`. All three are visible only because their emitters now declare a severity; before 2026-10-14 every one of them was invisible to any reader.
  A STALE VMLOCK was left behind by that successful run (`/tmp/qwt-vmlock-win11de-goal`, all three recorded pids dead - pid/holder/sentinel). quick-upgrade.sh has no `vm_unlock`; its sentinel died without removing the file, and the next VM job would have been blocked. Removed by hand after verifying the pids. P3, not filed separately.

- STARTAPPCOLDSILENT [app menu, user-facing; P1, FIXED and receipted on a guest] [verified 2026-10-11] **A COLD QUBE LAUNCHED FROM THE APP MENU FAILS IN TOTAL SILENCE: no window, no error, nothing anywhere the user or dom0 can see.** Measured on win11-app, the real user path and nothing else - guest Halted, then the one call dom0's menu makes:
  `qrexec-client-vm win11-app qubes.StartApp+calculator` -> **rc=46 after 70 s, stderr EMPTY**, and 40 dom0 captures over 2 min all `windows=0`. The qube started; nothing else happened.
  THE CONTROL, same call, same id, same guest, WARM: **rc=1 after 26 s** with a precise diagnostic - `qubes.StartApp: 'calculator' is not a built-in and has no AppMap entry under HKCU:\Software\Invisible Things Lab\Qubes Tools\AppMap (HKCU of the service's user) - re-run qvm-sync-appmenus, or the shortcut is gone`.
  THE CHAIN, established from the guest's own logs (both my earlier readings - "stderr lost", then "child setup failed" - were WRONG):
  the cold call NEVER REACHED THE GUEST. `start-app.ps1-20261011.log` holds exactly ONE entry, `[20261011.090513.471]`, which is the WARM call. `qrexec-wrapper-20261011.log` holds exactly ONE StartApp dispatch, at `uptime 89.687 s` = 09:05:00 (`qrexec-wrapper.exe 10858|515|user|6|cmd.exe /c powershell ... start-app.ps1 "calculator"` -> `StartChild: Running ... as user 'user'`), which is also the warm call. For the cold call at 09:02:58 there is NO wrapper entry and NO service entry at all.
  Boot timing, from the same log (the guest clock flips +3 h mid-boot): `uptime 57.859 s` at `12:04:34` puts the start of boot at ~09:03:36, so at 09:04:08 - when rc=46 came back - the qube had been up ~32 s and qrexec was not yet serving. dom0 started the qube and the caller gave up before the service could ever be dispatched, returning a bare Win32 error with no text.
  So: the app menu starts the qube, the launch is abandoned mid-boot, and nothing - not dom0, not the guest log, not the user - records that anything was attempted.
  NOT A HARNESS ARTEFACT: no launcher of ours is involved, nothing touched the guest before or after, and the warm control differs only in power state.
  **REPRODUCED WITH THE CALLER DOM0 ACTUALLY USES, and my doubt about that was wrong.** I had begun to walk this finding back on the grounds that `qrexec-client-vm` from a management qube is not dom0's path. The owner was blunt that the failure is real, and he is right: `qvm-run --service --autostart win11-app qubes.StartApp+Calculator` - the exact binary and form `qubesappmenus/receive.py:270` writes into the .desktop (`Exec=qvm-run -q -a --service -- %VMNAME% qubes.StartApp+<name>`) - gives `Running 'qubes.StartApp+Calculator' on win11-app / win11-app: command failed with code: 46`. The guest's `Exec=` line is irrelevant here: dom0 constructs the service call itself and supplies `%VMNAME%`; the guest value is used only on the legacy path.
  WHERE IT DIES, from the guest's own logs on that boot: `qrexec-agent-20261011.log` HAS the request - `[20261011.134940.715] ParseUtf8Command: Command: user:QUBESRPC qubes.StartApp+Calculator win-idd-mgmt` - and then NOTHING about it: no child, no error, no line. `qrexec-wrapper-20261011.log` has no dispatch for it at all, while 20 s later at uptime 65.9 s it launches `notifhost --relay` **as user 'user'** perfectly well, so a user-context service is serviceable by then. A SYSTEM request on the same boot logs `NormalizeUserName: user 'SYSTEM' is this service's own account`; the `user:` request logs nothing after being parsed.
  So the request reaches the agent and is dropped there, silently, and the caller gets a bare code 46. `qrexec-agent.c:758-780` normalises the account, builds the `qrexec-wrapper.exe` command line and launches it with `CreateNormalProcessAsCurrentUser`, returning its status - and the only line covering that stretch is a `LogDebug`, which this log does not carry.
  **ROOT CAUSE, measured.** `LogLevel=4` was set in HKLM on the TEMPLATE (the AppVM's C: is volatile, so it must be set there to survive a cold boot) and the cold start repeated through `qvm-run --service --pass-io --autostart`. rc 46 again, 67 s, and NOTHING on either stream. The wrapper's own log then gives it outright:
```
[20261011.140851.224-D] CreatePipedProcessAsUser: user 'user', cmd '...start-app.ps1 "Calculator"', interactive 1
[20261011.140851.224-D] CreatePipedProcessAsUser: current session: 1, console session: 1
[20261011.140851.245-D] GetLoggedOnUserToken: no user is logged on
[20261011.140851.332-E] CreatePipedProcessAsUser: LogonUser failed with error 0x52e: The user name or password is incorrect.
[20261011.140851.339-E] StartChild: CreatePipedProcessAsCurrentUser failed with error 0x52e
[20261011.140851.340-D] VchanSendExitCode: Sent exit code 1326
```
  On a cold start the request arrives BEFORE the interactive logon completes. `GetLoggedOnUserToken` finds nobody, the wrapper falls back to `LogonUser` with stored credentials, and that returns **0x52e = 1326 = ERROR_LOGON_FAILURE**. It sends exit code 1326, and **1326 mod 256 = 46** - the "code 46" dom0 reports is a truncated Win32 error, not a qrexec status. The stderr EOF is sent empty, which is why `--pass-io` showed nothing and why the app menu can show nothing.
  TWO HALVES, both in `qrexec-wrapper`: (1) it attempts a user-context launch before logon instead of waiting, bounded, for the session - which exists ~20 s later on the same boot, where `notifhost --relay` runs as user 'user' without trouble (uptime 65.9 s); (2) when it fails it returns only a numeric code that is then truncated, so nothing reaches the user, dom0 or the guest log that says what happened. `start-app.ps1` has exactly such a bounded wait ("no interactive session after 60s - refusing to hang") and never runs, because the wrapper fails first.
  SEPARATELY WORTH CHASING: the `LogonUser` fallback FAILS with ERROR_LOGON_FAILURE, i.e. the stored credential does not authenticate - that touches the autologon / `DefaultPassword` LSA-secret path, and would still be a latent defect even once the wait is added.
  THREE WRONG GUESSES ARE RECORDED ABOVE on the way to this ("stderr lost", "child setup failed", "the wrapper died before LogInit"), plus a walk-back of the whole finding. The measurement that settled it was one log-level change.
  **FIXED** in core-agent `9ad4dfc2` (parent `984e2759`, package build 38134950481): `QwWaitForConsoleLogon` polls `WTSQueryUserToken` for up to 90 s before a user-context launch, and a session that never arrives is reported to the CALLER'S STDERR in words, after the hello and before the exit code. It POLLS rather than retrying `CreatePipedProcessAsUser`, because a retried `LogonUser` hammers credentials already refused and failed logons count toward ACCOUNT LOCKOUT - on these guests autologon is what recovers a lockout.
  **GUEST RECEIPT, same guest and same day as the failure, so it is a true before/after.** The new wrapper (sha256 F07AD7A478E2DD85, 42232 bytes; old BBDA7B1F954AD729 kept) was installed on the TEMPLATE, because the AppVM's C: is volatile. Both qubes halted, then one action - `qvm-run --service --pass-io --autostart win11-app qubes.StartApp+Calculator`:
```
BEFORE  [20261011.140851.332-E] CreatePipedProcessAsUser: LogonUser failed with error 0x52e
AFTER   [20261011.142709.482-D] QwWaitForConsoleLogon: waiting for console logon (0 of 90000 ms)
        [20261011.142715.824-I] QwWaitForConsoleLogon: console logon appeared after 6000 ms
        [20261011.142715.824-I] StartChild: Running '...start-app.ps1 "Calculator"' as user 'user'
```
  rc 0 after 94 s, and dom0 shows the window: `win-0.png 1202x934 mean=0.950`, stable over three captures, visually confirmed as Calculator fully rendered. The request beat the logon by 6 s; without the wait that is precisely the 0x52e -> 1326 -> 46 -> silence path. `LogonUser` was never reached, so no lockout exposure.
  STILL OPEN, and only MASKED by this fix: the `LogonUser` fallback FAILS with ERROR_LOGON_FAILURE, i.e. the stored credential does not authenticate. The wait means it is no longer reached on a normal cold start, but any path that does reach it still breaks, and that is the autologon / `DefaultPassword` LSA-secret path.

- APPMENUNOUWP [app menu, user-facing; P1, FIXED IN SOURCE 2026-10-11 (core-agent 0258ce2), UNVERIFIED on a guest] [verified 2026-10-11] **The app menu cannot launch any UWP app, which on a stock Windows 11 guest is most of what the OS ships.** `get-appmenus.ps1` enumerates Start Menu `.lnk` shortcuts plus a fixed built-in list (notepad, explorer, settings, cmd/cmd-admin, powershell/powershell-admin, edge, and the two dom0 ids `qubes-run-terminal` / `qubes-open-file-manager`). A UWP app has no `.lnk` - it exists only in AppsFolder under its AUMID - so Calculator, and every other modern app, is absent from the menu entirely. MEASURED on win11-app 2026-10-11, the guest's actual Start Menu: **41 shortcuts, no Calculator.** `C:\ProgramData\...\Start Menu\Programs` has 28 (Microsoft Edge, Remote Desktop Connection, Steps Recorder, Windows Media Player Legacy, Character Map, Component Services, Computer Management, dfrgui, Disk Cleanup, Event Viewer, iSCSI Initiator, Memory Diagnostics Tool, ...) and `C:\Users\user\...\Start Menu\Programs` has 13 (Administrative Tools, File Explorer, OneDrive, LiveCaptions, Magnify, Narrator, On-Screen Keyboard, VoiceAccess, Command Prompt, Control Panel, Run, Windows PowerShell (x86), ...). Every one is a legacy or admin tool; not one modern app. Meanwhile `Microsoft.WindowsCalculator_8wekyb3d8bbwe!App` is `STATUS Ok`, `USER user state=Installed` - installed and runnable, just unreachable from the menu. The owner put it plainly: *"there is no calculator entry in start menu, to begin with"*. `start-app.ps1`'s own header already concedes the shape of this - *"A fresh Windows guest has almost nothing in its Start Menu"* - which is why the built-in list exists at all; the built-ins are a patch over the symptom, not a fix.
  **THE SCAN AND THE ACTIVATION BOTH EXIST, and the register said OPEN without saying so** (corrected 2026-10-14): core-agent `0258ce2`, 2026-10-11 12:18:28, added `SweepStoreApps` - called unconditionally beside the two `.lnk` sweeps - which enumerates AppsFolder and writes `shell:AppsFolder\<AUMID>` as the map target, and `start-app.ps1:241` activates that moniker. So the design gap this entry describes is closed in source; what it is NOT is verified on a guest.
  WHY THE OWNER STILL SAW IT FAIL AT 13:29 THAT DAY, 70 minutes AFTER the scan landed: the appvm's map was synced BEFORE the scan existed, which this entry already records ("93 entries ... from before the store-app scan existed"). A re-sync would have fixed it - except that dom0 syncs a template-based qube in its TEMPLATE, writing the template's hive, while the handler reads the appvm's own. That is [[APPMAPWRONGHIVE]], filed 2026-10-14, and it is why the stale map could not be refreshed. Its fix (mirror the map into the machine hive, which the appvm inherits; both readers try HKCU then the mirror) is in core-agent ed9fcea, also UNVERIFIED on a guest.
  SO THE START-MENU CLAUSE OF THE CURRENT GOAL HAS NO OPEN DESIGN GAP LEFT, only two unverified fixes. I nearly re-implemented the AppsFolder scan from scratch today without grepping for it first - the rule that a recorded "must" is a TODO cuts both ways, and an entry marked OPEN has to be checked against the source before it is worked.

  SCOPE, so this is not read as excusing [[STARTAPPCOLDSILENT]]: the cold call died BEFORE the service ran (no wrapper dispatch, no service log entry), so the silence is independent of the id being unlaunchable - a valid id would have been just as silent. Hand-making a `.lnk` would manufacture a pass and is explicitly NOT the fix (owner 2026-10-11: *"ANYTHING 'AROUND' == FAILED TEST"*); the fix is for the appmenus scan to enumerate AppsFolder entries and for StartApp to resolve an AUMID.

- CIREDUNREAD [process/CI; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-11] **`build.yml` was RED for 14 consecutive runs over ~15 h and I reported a green build the whole time, because I was reading a different workflow's conclusion.** Last green `5d1d741b` 2026-10-10T10:29Z; first red `7641aa7e` 16:00Z; still red at `291ef154` 2026-10-11T07:36Z. Every receipt and status in that window cited `release-package` runs (e.g. "38119590694 green"), which does not run the offline test batteries. CAUSE, agent side: `d7b820d8` ("every message plain and short") reworded the fail-open messages while three checks in `notifyerr_test.c` matched fragments of the old prose - `LogCount("notifytexts.h")` (survives only in a comment), `LogCount("NOT PRESENT")` (lowercased), `LogCount("CreateProcess")` (gone). A rename breaks every consumer, and the consumer here was a string. CAUSE, process side: nothing asserts that a SHA is green, only that one named run succeeded - `verify-release-package.sh:1028` checks the conclusion of the run it is handed and no other. FIXED: needles repointed at the emitted text, tag-prefixed where the bare phrase is also a window reason string (it counted 3, not 1); `CheckCount` prints the count and says "was NEVER logged - reworded out from under this check?" on zero, which is what diagnosed the third one; `tools/ci-state.sh` reports every workflow's latest conclusion for a sha and exits 1 if a required one (`build.yml`, `test.yml`) is red or has no run for it, rc 2 for unfinished/unreadable; the receipt refuses a sha it does not pass. Selftests: `ci-state-selftest.sh` 8/8 with the incident as its first arm, `notifyerr` 121 clean + every defect arm. CLOSED the same day: `tools/hooks/release-cut-gate.sh` now runs `ci-state.py` on HEAD after the coverage check and blocks a cut whose required workflows are not green - coverage says the right SUITES ran on a guest and says nothing about whether the code compiles, and nothing else in the cut path asked. `release-cut-gate-selftest.sh` 9/9: `cut-ci-red` (full coverage + a red required workflow is blocked, naming it), `cut-ci-running` (unfinished is no verdict, not a pass), `cut-satisfied` still allows, and both new arms are seen to fail under `RELEASE_CUT_GATE_DEFECT=1`.

- BROKERLATEREGLOUD [dom0 notifications; P2, FIXED OFFLINE, UNVERIFIED on a guest] **My own noise fix was half inert, and the receipt I took said so while I read it as a pass.** `40389c2` made `BROKERHOLD` INFO on a cold boot and claimed the same for `BROKERLATEREG`; the guest receipt (3 cold boots on win11-app, build `38119590694`) shows `BROKERHOLD` at `-I]` and `BROKERLATEREG` at `-W]` on all three. CAUSE: the gate was `QgaHoldIsStartup(FALSE, g_BrokerReadyAt != 0)`, and late registration only SUCCEEDS once the broker is ready, so that argument is TRUE at every call there and the quiet branch was unreachable - a predicate fed a fact that is constant at its call site, the third instance of that mistake in two days. FIXED agent `9e93f02`: `QgaLateRegIsStartup(refusedWhileUp, hadSlotBefore)` asks the two facts that do vary and are true at the moment the window went without a slot - `PwRegFailLogged` (an ACTIVE broker had no slot or arena room: the capacity fault `QGABROKERREGFAIL` already shouts about) and the new sticky `PwBrokerEverSourced` (it held a slot and lost it, i.e. the broker died). Neither = nothing was missing but a broker that had not started: INFO. Both snapshotted before the retry, which clears one and sets the other. Offline: 104 checks clean, knob `LATEREGALWAYSLOUD` fails as required, plus a shape check pinning the level to the snapshot because `PwRegFailLogged`'s value is now load-bearing for a LEVEL (Jev `worst_residual` reused-flag 0.28). Jev on the diff: `quiet_branch_reachable` 0.94, `fault_still_loud` 0.95, `snapshot_correct` 0.94. INSTRUMENT: the receipt no longer grades by eye - `tools/grade-noise.py` computes it and fails on absent tags. **UNVERIFIED on a guest:** the offline suite and Jev verify the PREDICATE, not the level a guest writes; the receipt is pending a green `build.yml` (see CIREDUNREAD, which blocked it).

- DIRECTSUPPRESSEARLY [dom0 notifications; P1, FIXED] [verified 2026-10-11] **UN-RETRACTED: it IS reachable, it fires a FALSE dom0 popup, and it is now measured end to end.** I filed this, then retracted it the same day on the argument that a window raised before the broker is ready cannot be slice-fed and so cannot reach the suppression arm. WRONG: `perwindow.c:526` sets `sliceFed = !PwWindowEligible(entry) || DirectRequired()`, so on a direct-required guest EVERY window is slice-fed the instant it attaches, and the arm is reachable whenever the broker takes longer than the two graces. MEASURED on win11-app with a window opened ~29 s before broker-ready: `09:38:09.121 BROKERHOLD hwnd 0x10094 ... brokerActive=0 slot=-1` (held, correctly), then `QGADIRECTSUPPRESS hwnd 0x10094 (class Notepad, 1428x783) NOT SHOWN: never received a frame on the required direct per-window path ... (sliceFed=1 brokerSourced=1 slot=0 brokerActive=1 DirectSuppressed=1)` at ERROR, then `DirectSuppressNotifyUser: user notification sent (1 suppressed window(s))` - and **11 s later dom0 displayed that same window at 1428x783 with mean 0.9906, white and correct, never black**. So dom0 got "a window could not be shown - the per-window display path is not working" about a window that was fine, and `DirectSuppressed` (which acceptance asserts is 0) was incremented on a healthy guest. CAUSE: the per-window grace runs from REGISTRATION, and the window was registered into a broker that had only just gone active and was feeding nothing - its 10 s was spent before the broker could serve it. FIXED agent `40389c2`: `QgaWindowFaultDue` runs the clock from the LATER of registration and broker-ready; with no broker ever ready it runs from registration as before, so a broker that never arrives is still reported (and `QGADESLICEDOWN` names that case). Offline: clean 100 checks 0 failed, knob `FAULTFROMREG` fails the measured row. Sibling fix in `eb30619`: the normal pre-broker hold was logging three WARNINGs per cold boot (`BROKERHOLD` x2 + `BROKERLATEREG`) for the designed path - `QgaHoldIsStartup` makes those INFO while a broker that WAS ready and died stays loud. NO GUEST RECEIPT YET for either: owed is a cold boot with a window opened before the broker, showing no QGADIRECTSUPPRESS, no dom0 send, no WARNINGs for the hold, and the window delivered white.
- NOTFIXED [gui-daemon, dom0 visibility; the OWNER'S ORIGINAL REPORT] [verified 2026-10-10] **THE DEFECT HE REPORTED IS NOT FIXED, IT STILL REPRODUCES, AND NOTHING I DID TOUCHED IT.** Owner: *"so you think 'product has improved', but if you ask Jev it the bug has gone with fair facts you get FUCKING CLEAR NO, right?"* Asked with fair facts - including every measurement I had been citing as progress - Jev answered: `reported_defect_fixed` **0.03**, `symptom_still_reproduces` **0.97**, `fixes_address_the_report` = **adjacent-not-causal conf 1.00**, `blocked_by_owner_scope` **0.82**. **THE SYMPTOM REPRODUCED THE SAME NIGHT, INSIDE THE GATE:** `FAIL WIN10-appvm boot 3: INVALID-INSTRUMENT - the guest shows 1 notepad window(s) but the dom0 capture returned none on six tries`. A live guest with a window and dom0 showing nothing - which is the condition he reported. I had also measured it deliberately hours earlier (A/B, one guest, one variable: after an agent restart notepad had a VISIBLE top-level HWND, the agent sat on `Awaiting for a vchan client`, dom0 returned 0 PNGs; after a reboot the agent logged `A vchan client has connected` 0.32 s later and dom0 returned 1), and the agent says it in as many words: *"dom0's gui-daemon for this qube is gone and is not coming back on its own. The qube keeps running (qrexec works) and will show no windows until it is restarted."* That line was in the log he pointed me at. **WHAT THE NIGHT'S SIX FIXES ACTUALLY WERE:** a notification's gate, autologon handling, log levels on a transient capture retry, a helper's task registration, an installer step. Real defects, several of them shipping - the clean-install updater deploy failed on EVERY clean install - but **not one of them is on the path by which dom0 displays a guest's windows**, which is what `adjacent-not-causal 1.00` says. Reporting those as "the product improved" against his report was wrong and is retracted. **THE "CAUSE IS dom0-SIDE" PARAGRAPH THAT STOOD HERE IS RETRACTED (2026-10-10).** Owner: *"FUCK dom0 SIDE EXCUSES"*, and he was right twice over. First, the framing was an excuse: `DESIGN-gui-daemon-restart-survival.md` Sec. 1.2 itself records that every daemon death with an ESTABLISHED cause was an agent-side protocol violation - ours. Second and worse, **the symptom in this entry was never reported by anyone.** Both "reproductions" cited above are void: the gate line grades ITSELF `INVALID-INSTRUMENT - not a product verdict` (Jev `the occurrence is receipted` **0.05**) and its guest-side probe was only `Get-Process notepad | Where-Object { $_.MainWindowHandle -ne 0 }`, which reads no window class, cloak state, geometry or hung state; and the A/B turned on an AGENT RESTART, a path the owner closed the same day (*"guest daemon restart NEVER was the cure"*). Jev on this machine: `occurrence_measured` = **no-only-source-reading, conf 1.00**. What he actually reported are dom0 TOASTS - see `SHUTDOWNDEATHTOAST` and `SWEEPNOTREAD`. **THE HONEST SHAPE OF THE SESSION:** roughly ten real pre-existing defects fixed, against about eleven I created in the same session and then repaired (a hoisted trap that masked the real installer error, a bare `throw` in a trap that lost it, a patch script that injected code into a comment and parsed clean, four defects in a reviewer I wrote, a chain that triple-counted failures twice, the sealed golden passed as a churn subject, `os=win10` where no golden exists). And `condition_met`, which I quoted at him all night, measures Jev's confidence in the STATE FILE I WROTE, not the guest: the same build scored clause 1 at 0.71 on a thin brief and 0.05 on an honest one, and the overall figure FELL 0.05 -> 0.04 while the product improved, because I kept adding residuals. It is a detector of my own overclaiming and was never a scoreboard. **THE COLD-READ FILE THIS ENTRY ONCE POINTED AT IS DELETED** (2026-10-10): it described an unreported symptom, and four of its "measured facts" were my own text quoted back at me. The live entries are `SHUTDOWNDEATHTOAST` (what the guest's Event Log actually says) and `SWEEPNOTREAD` (the 13 breaches the logs held while I grepped single emitters).
- SHUTDOWNDEATHTOAST [notifications, watchdog/notifhost; OURS; owner named it 2026-10-10] [verified 2026-10-10] **EVERY SHUTDOWN TELLS dom0 A QWT COMPONENT DIED OF A MAJOR ERROR, AND IT IS JUST WINDOWS ENDING THE SESSION.** Owner, 2026-10-10, disambiguating two toasts I had conflated: *"there are TWO toasts we are talking about: the secure desktop one we fixed last evening with NEVERTHELESS WAS ALL OVER THE SCREEN this morning, and the one about gui agent dead ... which is this bug exactly"*. **MEASURED on win10-acc, Application log, source `Qubes Windows Tools`, read 2026-10-10T07:52:58Z:** four records, all level Error, **all four exit code `0x40010004` = `DBG_TERMINATE_PROCESS`, which is Windows' own session teardown** - `4001` 10-08 21:58:12 (gui-agent.exe PID 4028, ran 175546 ms), `4001` 10-08 21:58:13 (PID 2916, ran 563 ms), `4003` 10-10 04:40:37 (notifhost.exe PID 6852, ran 26875 ms), `4003` **10-10 07:39:29 (PID 2248, ran 27734 ms) - that one is MY OWN clean shutdown of the guest 13 minutes earlier in this session, so it reproduces on demand, at every shutdown.** Each is turned into a dom0 notification by `guest/qwt-report-death.ps1` (event-triggered, ids 1000/4001-4004/7024/7031/7034, one notification per death), carrying `deathevent.h:177`'s text: *"... exited without being asked to - exit code X - after running T. A Qubes Windows Tools component died; this is a major error."* **THE TABLE ALREADY HAS THE RIGHT ROW AND THE BRIDGE DOES NOT REACH IT:** `QGA_DECIDE_SESSION_END_REAPED` (`qga-lifecycle.h`) is exactly "the system ended it after an acknowledged notice and AFTER its orderly exit completed: INFO, no launch, no record", and the agent got that notice/ack/done handshake on 2026-10-07 - the last `4001` here predates it (10-08 is one day after, but this guest's only retained agent logs start 10-08 21:55, so whether the agent still does it is NOT settled). The `4003` bridge deaths are from TODAY and are supervised by the agent, not the watchdog, so they go through no such handshake: **the bridge is killed by session teardown at every shutdown and reported as a major error.** This is the same thing GWeck's screenshot `gw175-a` shows three times ("notification bridge exited unexpectedly ... exit code 0 ... ran 0:00:00"), there attributed to the singleton-duplicate path; this is a SECOND, different cause with a different exit code. **RETRACTED THE SAME HOUR, AND IT WAS MY ERROR:** I wrote here that this "cries loud to dom0 and keeps zero internal records" because `QGAWDDEATH` and four sibling tags were 0 across all 60 log files. Those are the WATCHDOG's decision tags, and `4003` is written by the **AGENT** - a wrong-tag false negative, the third of the day after `QGADESKSTUCK` (2026-10-09) and `NOTIFYERR` (this session). **MEASURED with the right tags, 2026-10-10T07:57Z: the internal records all EXIST** - `[20261010.073929.164-3472:6532-E] NotifBridgeSupervise: QGANOTIFBRIDGEEXIT notification bridge pid 2248 EXITED (exit code 1073807364)` i.e. 0x40010004 (`main.c:4331`, 3 occurrences), the Event Log record `Application/4003#779`, and `qwt-deaths.log`: `2026-10-10 07:39:33 [ERROR] DEATH #2 NEW Application/4003#779: The notification bridge exited unexpectedly`. `gui-watchdog-20261010.log` exists with 26 `QGAWD` lines. The single-path rule (one error path, some escalated to toasts, never the reverse) is SATISFIED here. The 0 count for those five tags is a RETENTION artifact: they are decision lines for the 10-08 `4001`s, and the retained watchdog log starts 10-10. **THE ACTUAL DEFECT IS MISCLASSIFICATION, IN THE REPORTER'S OWN WORDS:** `Cause: exit code 0x40010004 - not a code this reporter knows` (`guest/qwt-report-death.ps1:432`), so the commonest exit code at shutdown - Windows' own session teardown - is escalated to a dom0 toast reading "exited unexpectedly". `mgmt/harness/log-sweep.sh`'s own header records this condition from 2026-10-07: *"For days the GUI watchdog logged an agent death (0x40010004) and a relaunch into the ending session on EVERY shutdown, and nothing read it."* NEXT: a shutdown-and-read cycle on one guest, grading whether a `4003`/`4001` appears, is a ~3 minute measurement and the arm has already been seen to FAIL (it fired on this session's own shutdown).
- GATEWRONGBIN [gate; OURS - tools/gate-remainder.sh] [verified 2026-10-09] **THE GATE SPENT A WHOLE RUN GRADING A BINARY THAT WAS NEVER THE CANDIDATE, AND REPORTED A FIXED DEFECT AS PRESENT.** `gate-preflight` and `log-sweep` grade `$VM11` AS IT IS - neither installs anything - and nothing checked what was on it. At HEAD fe4337174ba8 that guest was still carrying agent **4.3.32.612** while the candidate package was **4.3.36+agent.eb24cb5115ba**. **THE RECEIPTS ARE IN THE SWEEP'S OWN ARCHIVED LOGS** (`~/qwt-accept/remainder-fe4337174ba8/work/log-sweep/logs/files`): every `LogInit: Module version` line reads `4.3.32.612`, in a file named `gui-agent-20261009-190318-3824.log` - the pre-4.3.33 `<ts>-<pid>` name format, which the candidate does not even use - and the two keyed-mutex `DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST` lines that sweep reported under KNOWN-DEFECT PRESENT (`AcquireNextFrame`/`ReleaseFrame` ... `error 0x887a0026: The keyed mutex was abandoned`, at `.190329.051-5308-E` and `.190651.904-5616-E`) were written by THAT agent. They could not have come from the candidate: the guard `status != DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST && status != DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED` is present THREE times in `eb24cb5115ba:gui-agent/capture.c`, which is the agent in that package - checked with `git show`. So ERRFAM2's open question is settled, and the answer is the unflattering one: **the defect was not back; the gate was measuring a build from before the fix.** Jev had scored `keyed_mutex_regression` = insufficient-evidence **0.98**, correctly - the state it was given could not tell the readings apart; the module versions can. **THE FIX:** `assert_candidate` hashes the package's `reference/gui-agent.exe` and compares it to the hash of the agent actually RUNNING on the subject; both as-is suites REFUSE rather than grade a mismatch. It refuses rather than installing, because the subject is prepared by the campaign and silently upgrading it here would hide which step failed to prepare it. The ACCEPT path cannot be driven on the rig without a guest that happens to run the reference, and pulling the guest's exe out to hash it locally CORRUPTS it (the VMShell stream carries cmd's banner - which is how my first attempt at this test "failed" its own good arm), so the guest call goes through `QTEST_BIN` and `tools/tests/gate-candidate-selftest.sh` drives all six arms off-rig through a stand-in that emits that banner too. **THE RULE THIS BREACHED IS ALREADY WRITTEN** (CLAUDE.md evidence rule 3: "the artefact under test is verified installed - running binary hash vs the manifest"); `toast-hold-test` was already doing it and naming the same reference hash `986cc2efa6fc` in its own log, which is how two suites in ONE run disagreed about what was installed. **HOW FAR DOES IT REACH? AUDITED, AND IT IS CONTAINED - but Jev was right to ask.** Asked whether any past verdict lacking an asserted running-binary hash is suspect, Jev answered `wrong_binary_severity` = **recheck-all-provenance 0.81**, so the audit was done rather than assumed. The campaign's twelve suites DO assert, centrally and fail-closed, in `mgmt/harness/matrix.sh`: it computes `ASHA` from `reference/gui-agent.exe`, REQUIRES it to agree with `MANIFEST.json reference_binaries`, refuses to start if either is missing - with the vacuous-match trap already guarded in a comment ("if it were empty that grep would degenerate to matching the bare key - i.e. ANY result passes without ever checking which build installed") - and `verify_installed` then grades each cell on `"installed_gui_agent_sha256":"$ASHA` with an explicit `installed agent is NOT the release binary` failure. `clean-boot-sweep.sh`, `toast-hold-test.sh`, `quick-upgrade.sh`, `grade-twostage.sh` and `vchan-collapse-cell.sh` assert too. **The two that did not were exactly the two that never install anything** - `gate-preflight` and `log-sweep`, reached only through gate-remainder - so they never ran `verify_installed` and nothing else stood in for it. That is the whole blast radius, and it is now closed by `assert_candidate`.

Sec. 1 also establishes there is NO reconnect race - `libvchan_client_init` polls with an INFINITE timeout and aborts only if the domain is dead - so a re-exec'd guid would have connected within ~1 s; it did not, therefore **guid did not restart**. That is the diagnostic Sec. 1.2 says settles class (i) vs class (ii), now measured. **NOTHING WAS DONE ABOUT IT AND NOTHING SHOULD BE FROM HERE:** it is dom0-side, in a component that is not ours, and the repo requires a design writeup, the owner's review and an upstream design issue before any such work; the two defects already qualifying for an upstream report (this write-path EOF, and the use-after-free if `execv` fails in `restart_guid`) still need the owner's approval of exact text. Jev on stopping here: `gui_daemon_scope` **0.93**. **WHAT WAS OURS WAS THE MISATTRIBUTION:** `gate-preflight`'s premise was "one agent restart and one screenshot", so on this rig it could never see a control and every run graded REFUSING "the harness control is not visible even with NO bit set" - blaming the rig for a connection the restart had dropped. It applies its bit across a REBOOT now (fresh agent AND fresh guid) and GRADES the connection as an instrument precondition, so it reports the missing gui-daemon instead of an empty window list. The fan-out was checked: of everything that restarts the agent through `lifecycle-lib.sh`, only `gate-preflight` then reads dom0 windows. **THE OWNER CLOSED THE LINE THE SAME DAY: "not touching dom0."** That is the SECOND time he has said it (2026-10-02, on viridian/Qubes #9821: *"we are NOT patching dom0"*), so it is a general rule and not a viridian one: a dom0-side defect is worked around ENTIRELY GUEST-SIDE or it is dropped. It also covers reporting and waiting - I had been carrying "the upstream text needs the owner's approval" as a pending item, which keeps a dom0 line alive on the board; it is not pending, and CLAUDE.md listing these two gui-daemon defects as QUALIFYING for a report does not make them so. Only the owner reopens it, in writing. **NOTHING IN THIS WORK TOUCHES dom0, checked rather than asserted:** the whole day's diff (ada534bc..02f2d3e0) is ten files - findings/issues.md, guest/restart-gui-agent.ps1, FIVE under mgmt/harness/ (gate-preflight.sh, lifecycle-lib.sh, quick-upgrade.sh, toast-hold-test.sh, updater-shutdown-notice-cell.sh), tools/errfam.py, tools/gate-remainder.sh and tools/tests/gate-candidate-selftest.sh - no path under `dom0/`, no policy file. The guest-side workaround is the whole of the response and it is sufficient: gate-preflight applies its bit across a guest REBOOT, which yields a fresh agent AND a fresh guid, and grades the vchan connection as an instrument precondition. A restart-only defect is never P1 here anyway (the Linux agent kills all GUI windows on restart and that is fine), so nothing is owed.
