- P2 [harness, log-sweep; OURS - tools/log-sweep.py] [verified 2026-10-09] **FIXED 5f406bad: THE --since WINDOW COMPARED A REAL-UTC WINDOW AGAINST PRE-CORRECTION STAMPS, SO THE OWNER'S GATE METRIC COUNTED 35 ERROR LINES FOR A WINDOW IN WHICH THE GUEST WROTE NONE.** Measured on the win11-acc capture: every one of the 35 error lines is stamped `045801`..`050338`, the window opened at `033824` real, and subtracting this guest's measured 10800 s correction puts all 35 at `015801`..`020338` - an hour and a half BEFORE the window. Checked in code, not by eye: 0 of 35 have a true time inside the window, 35 of 35 outside. So `error_lines = 35` and `error_lines_undeclared = 35` - the metric whose threshold is the owner's "a clean error log is THE gate condition" - were FALSE POSITIVES of the window filter, and the 27 of them written by the build under test happened during the campaign's install 1.5 h earlier, not in what this run was asked to grade. The analyzer already names this exact failure mode ("every line looked three hours newer than the window ... That is a DATA problem ... never something to absorb") and checks the guest's clock against the host's AT COLLECTION - but by collection the guest has been corrected, so that skew reads ~0 while the stored pre-correction lines stay 3 h ahead. The point-in-time check is necessary and not sufficient. **FIXED ON THE SIXTH ATTEMPT (5f406bad), and the five failures are recorded because every one was mine, not the problem's.** THE SHIPPED MECHANISM: a line cannot be stamped after it was read, so the collector's clock bounds the capture and anything later than it cannot be in the corrected phase; those lines move back by the measured offset. The anchor is the HOST's clock when `--host-utc` is passed, else the guest's own `now` (measured both ways, identical). `cluster_boots` is TOLD the offset and given 0 once normalisation has run, because its phase-merge and the normalisation answer the same question. EACH FIELD IS TESTED ON ITS OWN VALUE - an init's `boot_time` is `ts` minus its uptime, so for a long-running process the two sit either side of the anchor. The slack is 300 s and it is the only thing protecting a REAL finding: a line written while the collector ran is stamped slightly after the anchor, and shifting it would move it an offset into the PAST, out of the window - the direction that loses a finding rather than inventing one. MEASURED on the capture: boots=6 (matching an independent hand count from the agent's clock-free `LogInit` uptimes), shifted=4893, `error_lines=0`, **breaches=0**, data_problems=0, and the summary prints what it did; with the one-line `GUARD:phasenorm` defect, 35 / 35 / five breaches. Suite 86 -> 98 assertions, 0 failed, and normalisation is driven OFFLINE by a new `phaseflip` fixture (one log straddling the correction with an error line in the pre-correction segment, built so its stamp and its true time fall on opposite sides of a `--since` window): shipped 0, knob'd 1. THE FIVE FAILURES, so nobody pays for them twice: re-detecting the offset on already-shifted data (invented a 6120 s "correction" and merged the 03:39 boot into the 01:57 one); deriving phase from each file's own backward jumps (UNSOUND - a per-day log is written by many processes CONCURRENTLY, so its lines are not time-ordered, and it covered only 2611 of 4893 lines); mapping an init to its line by TIMESTAMP (a LogInit block's lines share one stamp, so membership is not an identity); by LINE INDEX (stale for a handful of inits, which then shifted twice and landed a day early); and a HALF-OFFSET threshold on the wrong reasoning that a pre-correction line is a full offset ahead of the ANCHOR - it is a full offset ahead of ITS OWN REAL TIME, and that version put `error_lines` straight back to 35. **I also reported the whole thing UNREPRODUCIBLE and declined to ship it; that was wrong - I had kept patching forward instead of returning to the first working shape, which gave the right answer first try when rebuilt.** The remaining limit is stated in the code: a capture reaching back more than ~3 h would mis-attribute its oldest lines, and every `--since` window this harness uses is minutes to an hour.

- P2 [netsetup, errors; OURS - guest/pvnic-selfprime.ps1's qwtng-netsetup] [verified 2026-10-09] **FIXED fc4c5654: NETSETUP DISCOVERED QUBESDB'S READINESS BY FAILING, AT TWELVE ERROR LINES A GO.** (RETRACTED from this entry's first wording, which called it a failure that is LOST: it was not. Its whole log on win11-acc is six `LogInit` lines and then **twelve ERROR lines in the SAME millisecond** (`QioWriteBuffer: WriteFile failed with error 0xe8: The pipe is being closed.` x6 and `send_command_to_daemon: write to daemon failed with error 0xe8` x6, at `050028.749`), and then the file ENDS: no retry, no diagnosis, no verdict, no "I gave up" line. The CAUSE is a race the installer creates: our stage-2 stops the qrexec agent (the install RESULT records `vchan_prestop: QrexecAgent=absent QdbDaemon=absent`) while this service, started 190 s into the boot, is mid-call - so the pipe closes under it. The caller is `qdb_open` via `qubesdb-client.dll`, so ONE failed open costs twelve lines. FIXED by asking the SCM whether QdbDaemon is Running before opening - tri-state, so a guest without that service behaves as before; bounded at 60 s so it never becomes a new way to be late; and it says so in our own log either way. Proven to BUILD (the Roslyn gate compiles the extracted service, 13/0, and the extracted source carries QdbState at line 46 and its use at 312); the behavioural claim is falsifiable on the next install cell, where those twelve lines must be gone. Note also that this binary reports `Module version: 0.0.0.0` - it is compiled on the guest and carries no version resource, so the sweep cannot attribute its lines to a release at all.

(ii) "the harness fires them by polling during boot": refuted too - the boot my own sweep covered on win10-acc was probed at t+10 s and produced ZERO error lines. The text comes from code in `upstream/ro/` (libvchan, the xeniface client) which we SHIP and our callers trigger but do not own. **THEY DO NOT OCCUR ON A NORMAL BOOT OF A PROVISIONED GUEST: zero error lines in both clean boots of win11-acc and in win10-acc's swept boot, on two different Windows versions.** So the disposition is narrower than first filed: an install-era transient, whose sanctioned route is for the install cell that CAUSES it to DECLARE it with `--declare-error` (policed by `declared_errors_unmatched`), never a baseline exemption - `tools/log-sweep.py` refuses that in code ("an exemption from the BASELINE would be hiding", `zero-except-declared-injection 1.00`). Not declared yet; that is clause 1's remaining debt.
- P2 [harness, log-sweep; OURS - tools/log-sweep.py] [verified 2026-10-09] **FIXED e524ee95: THE PROACTIVE SWEEP COUNTED ONE BOOT TWICE WHEN THE GUEST'S CLOCK FLIPS, SO ITS PER-BOOT METRICS AND SOME OF ITS BREACHES ARE ARTEFACTS.** First run of `log-sweep` on win11-acc (2026-10-09, window 03:38:24Z, 57 files, 14849 lines): it reported `boots=4` with the table `1@03:39:28, 2@04:57:18, 3@06:31:28, 4@06:39:31` - and **boot 1 and boot 4 are the SAME BOOT**, three hours and three seconds apart, which is this rig's clock offset. `cluster_boots()` estimates a boot as `first line's timestamp - that log's "System uptime"`, which is the right anchor, but the RESULT is a timestamp in whatever clock phase that log's first line was stamped in - and the clock flips ~60 s into every boot when `sync-clock-from-dom0` lands. Counted clock-free from the agent's own `LogInit: System uptime` lines in that same capture (6 instances at 263.03, 50.19, 41.91, 44.86, 45.50, 50.91 s - every ~45 s one is a fresh boot), the window holds SIX boots, not four. THE FIX IS IN THE DATA ALREADY READ: a backward jump of more than ~60 s between consecutive lines of one log IS the correction, and its magnitude is the offset; normalise every timestamp into one phase before clustering, then the existing anchor is exact. IMPLEMENTED e524ee95 and re-measured on the same capture: boots went 4 -> 6 (matching the hand count), the offset is detected as exactly 10800.0 s, and `agent_instances_per_boot` went 5 -> 2 - the truthful value, which Jev then re-graded from defect 0.35 to **insufficient-evidence 0.53**, because two instances in one boot is the agent's one benign restart and not a respawn storm. Each of the three knobs degrades it on real data (clockphase boots=11, allinits boots=3 / per_boot=5, bootphase per_boot=7). The suite's own fixture drives only one of them and says so.

- P2 [user-facing, notifications; OURS - the helper tasks' instance policy; FIXED IN rc2 (agent bfdadba), NOT YET DEMONSTRATED] [verified 2026-10-07] **AFTER AN AGENT RESTART THE GUEST RAN WITH NO TOAST BRIDGE AT ALL, SILENTLY FOR THE REST OF THE SESSION.** Measured twice on the 4.3.36 rc1 package by the supervision routine's own health assertion (L4R). The helpers are started by Task Scheduler from XML the agent writes, their only trigger is <RegistrationTrigger>, and the agent re-registers at every Init - so on a restart the start is requested while the PREVIOUS helper, owned by the agent that just exited, is still running as that task's instance. With `MultipleInstancesPolicy=IgnoreNew` the scheduler DISCARDED it: the agent logged `NOTIFHOST launched via Task Scheduler ... --agent-pid 4348`, \Qubes-NotifBridge came back status=Ready Last Result 0, the old bridge exited two seconds later (`agent gone (its liveness mutex was released)` / `BRIDGE stopped (rc=0)`), nothing replaced it, and 60 s on the agent reported `QGANOTIFNOTREADY ... not relaunched (no relaunch loop, owner...)` at ERROR. The agent's own behaviour was correct throughout - it launched, it reported loudly, it did not loop. \Qubes-WgcBroker won the same race in that instance and came up, so the mechanism is not broken in general, only raced. P2 NOT P1 by the owner's standing rule (an agent restart is not normal operation); the BOOT path is unaffected and L6 measured both toast classes forwarding correctly on it. FIX: `Queue` instead of `IgnoreNew` - the scheduler's own serialization, so the new instance waits for the outgoing one, which leaves by itself (it watches its agent's liveness mutex) and is never cut off mid-forward, which is how a notification gets lost or doubled. Jev on the diff: queue 1.00, introduces-a-hang 0.17, lost-or-doubled 0.18. Guarded by lifecycle-selftest.sh (keyed to the XML line, because the comment explaining the change names IgnoreNew). OWED: the same L4R assertion passing on rc2.
- P3 [installer; OURS - the PV NIC re-arm task] [verified 2026-10-07] [FIXED offline; the rig demonstration is OWED] **THE SHUTDOWN RE-ARM WAS TERMINATED (267014) AND NOTHING COULD TELL WHETHER ITS TWO REGISTRY WRITES HAD LANDED.** QubesPvNicRearm fires on User32 1074 (shutdown initiated) and the Task Scheduler service terminates running actions as the system goes down; its action was powershell.exe parsing the ~500-line pvnic payload for two `reg add`s, about 2 s of which PowerShell's own start is the bulk. **TWO THINGS IN THE EARLIER ENTRY WERE WRONG AND ARE RETRACTED:** (a) `-RearmOnly` does not wait for a vif - it exits on its own in ~2 s, so no limit needed lengthening; (b) a guest with `netvm=''` is not exempt - the APPLIER has nothing to apply, but the latch arm still matters there, because a netvm can be attached later and that boot needs it. A third thing I asserted during the fix was also wrong and is retracted: the owner was NOT being notified falsely - guest/qwt-report-death.ps1 already ignores 267014 for every task ("a stop that was asked for, not a death"), which is correct, and is exactly why this was a SILENT hole rather than a noisy one. Jev with the premise corrected: silent_hole 0.89, severity narrow-but-real 0.93, fix 0.97, missing measurement "did the writes land" 0.85. THE FIX (28df1691): the action is cmd.exe + reg.exe, ~30 ms, no interpreter, with a STAMP written as the last link of a `&&` chain - so the stamp exists only once both writes have succeeded - and the BOOT run judges the previous session from it, where a task can finish what it starts (`Test-QwtRearmArm`: a stale stamp does not read as armed, an unexpected shutdown (6008) raised no 1074 so none was due, and a once-per-boot marker keeps one miss to ONE notification). tools/tests/pvnic-rearm-selftest.sh is 13 checks, each driven to fail with its own defect injected, and it asserts the DECODED command line rather than the escaped source text (Jev flagged escaping at 0.39/conf 0.27; with the decode measured it fell to 0.02). OWED: supervision-e2e.sh L7 clears NICS and the stamp, runs the task and measures whether both land; L7C runs the same probe on a clone of win11r-qwt, whose package still has the defect, so that is where the check is seen to FAIL. Until L7/L7C run on the 4.3.36 RC this is fixed-but-not-demonstrated.
- P2 [guest-stability, user-facing; OURS - supervision ordering; FIXED AND MEASURED 2026-10-07, awaiting a release] [verified 2026-10-07] **EVERY GUEST SHUTDOWN USED TO KILL THE GUI AGENT AND THE WATCHDOG RELAUNCHED IT INTO THE ENDING SESSION - FIXED; THE RETEST MEASURES ONE AGENT PER BOOT AND ZERO DEATHS.** What it was, measured on win11r-gz (4.3.35): Windows' session teardown terminated gui-agent with 0x40010004 33-826 ms after a shutdown began, the watchdog relaunched it 13 ms-1 s later into the session being torn down, that instance was killed too, a third lived until PRESHUTDOWN - 3 instances, 2 deaths and 2 extra vchan setups per shutdown, each killed instance leaving its vchan announcement in xenstore (the dom0 "(0:0) outdated protocol" dialog's mechanism) and each death writing event 4001. The August guard read SM_SHUTTINGDOWN from a session-0 service, which reports session 0: it logged sm_shuttingdown=0 at every death and was never once seen to fire, while PRESHUTDOWN arrived 4-32 s too late. THE FIX (branch fix/lifecycle-order, agent 33d15de, merged into feat/toast-actions; owner 2026-10-07 "if you terminate something that relaunches you need to make sure it STOPS relaunching beforehand", "rely on windows system services", "same of other components"): defined exit codes (QGA_EXIT_REQUESTED / SESSION_END / RECONNECT), an end-session handshake (the agent's hidden window disarms its helpers' relaunchers, tells the service the session is ending, waits for the acknowledgement, then exits withdrawing its vchan), launch triggers only in the service (no relaunch loop, no backoff: an unexpected death writes 4001 and the service ends ITSELF so the SCM's recovery restarts it), each helper launched once per agent life with Task Scheduler's restart-on-failure as its only relauncher, and every deliberate termination disarming that relauncher first. THE RETEST, 2026-10-07 on win11r-gz with the fix build, 5 boots and 2 clean shutdown cycles: agent_instances_per_boot=1, per_shutdown=1, relaunches_at_shutdown=0, deaths_at_shutdown=0, unrequested_deaths=0, death_events=0, errors_during_requested_stop=0, stale_error_lines=0, vchan_setups_while_going_down=0; every instance's exit was QGA_EXIT_SESSION_END, and QGAHELPERSDISARM / QGAHELPERTASK-disarmed lines show the new ordering firing. Jev's pre-commit review found one real hole (a vetoed shutdown whose agent then dies could strand a session with no GUI: lost_or_dropped 0.53) - closed with a bounded stuck-latch detector, re-asked at 0.38, residual recorded in docs/ADR-supervision.md 5. P2 now, not P1: fixed and measured, open only until a release carries it. **RE-MEASURED ON THE 4.3.36 RC (2026-10-07, win11r-gz, two clean shutdown/boot cycles): agent_instances_per_boot=1, agent_deaths_at_shutdown=0, agent_relaunches_at_shutdown=0, errors_during_requested_stop=0, stale_error_lines=0, death_events=0, and every watchdog lifetime in the capture ends with a requested stop.** The first grading of that run said relaunches=1 and it was the GRADER: the shutdown-window join widens the window by 2 s for marker jitter, so the BOOT's own launch (watchdog service start 14:31:46, launch 14:31:47, shutdown marker 14:31:48) counted as a launch into an ending session - a P2 breach judged `defect` 0.73 against the build that fixed it. Corrected in a2481e51: a launch counts only if something of ours ENDED in that window before it, with the real 2026-10-07 shape still counting 2 (T27) and the boot-then-shutdown shape counting 0 (T26, and 1 with the knob).
- P2 [user-facing, notifications; OURS - the death reporter's trigger; FIXED 2026-10-07, not yet demonstrated] [verified 2026-10-07] **A COMPONENT THAT DIED DURING A SHUTDOWN LOST ITS DOM0 NOTIFICATION.** Found by the log sweep on the lifecycle retest - the only breach that survived it: "Task Scheduler failed to launch action powershell.exe in instance ... of task \QwtDeathReporter. Additional Data: Error Value: 2147943515" (ERROR_SHUTDOWN_IN_PROGRESS), once per shutdown across five cycles. The reporter is event-triggered and Task Scheduler refuses to launch an action while the system is going down, so the death was recorded by Windows, logged at ERROR by its supervisor, and its notification silently dropped - at the one moment the chain matters most. It did not bite on the retest only because there were no deaths left to report. FIX (fix/broker-control, CI green): guest/qwt-report-death.ps1 -CatchUp re-reads the subscribed channels from a per-channel watermark and reports anything that never got its notification, run by a second task QwtDeathCatchUp (BootTrigger PT90S); a separate task because Task Scheduler substitutes ValueQueries only for an event trigger. NEXT: demonstrate it - a forced death during a shutdown must produce exactly one notification at the next boot.
- P1 [capture, user-facing; OURS - the broker's control path; FIXED 2026-10-07, NOT YET DEMONSTRATED] [verified 2026-10-07] **ONE SLOT'S WGC CHANNEL OPEN BLOCKS THE BROKER'S CONTROL PATH, SO THE AGENT DECLARES A WORKING BROKER HUNG AND TERMINATES IT - A REAL 1.2 S OUTAGE WITH EVERY WINDOW WITHHELD, STILL OPEN IN 4.3.35.** Diagnosed entirely from logs already on the dev qube - no rig time (Jev: root_cause control-behind-work 0.92, fix decouple-control 0.97, still_open 0.81, chain_established 0.27 - the mechanism is NAMED by our own instrumentation but the open's duration was never measured; state/rubric/answers in scratchpad/brokerhang/). The event (2026-10-01, rz9, win11 retail 26300, a build that already carried the stage field e70ea5e): at 12:12:29.554 an Explorer window (CabinetWClass, 1125x634) registers on broker SLOT 3 and is withheld pending its first frame ("direct required: composite fallback refused"); the broker has delivered no frame for slots 1 and 2 for ~3 s (seven QGAPOKEWGC in 209 ms, frameAge 2625 -> 3360 ms, whole-screen dirty rects); at 12:12:31.563 "QGABROKERHUNG ... has not acknowledged the request on slot 3 for 2016 ms (CtlAck 0, wanted 1) - a hang, not a crash; **it is blocked in stage=open-channel (2) slot=0**"; the agent REAPS it (TerminateProcess) and relaunches; "QGABROKERBACK ... RECOVERED after 1218 ms down". So the broker was WORKING - opening slot 0's WGC capture session - and its single main loop services control requests behind that per-slot work, so one window's channel open delays another slot's acknowledgement past the fixed ~2000 ms ack deadline (WGCBRK_ACK_DEADLINE_MS) and the agent kills a healthy broker. Same family as the two comments already in wgcbroker.cpp about the CLOSE path ("one hung window stopped the heartbeat and the agent reaped the whole broker"; "if the teardown waits for that callback, neither can proceed"). NOT fault injection: M8 suspends the broker with a script and its records are timestamped 12:46; at 12:12 the harness was opening the burn scene. THE FIX (Jev 0.97, both halves): (1) the broker's control path becomes independent of its capture work - a control request is acknowledged promptly (its own thread, or acknowledged before the channel open with the open's outcome reported separately), so no slot's work can delay another slot's ack; (2) the agent's hang criterion becomes STAGE-AWARE - a broker PROGRESSING in a declared, bounded stage is not hung, an unchanging stage past its own budget is, and a broker making declared progress is never terminated. Not a deadline raise (that is a timeout as a fix). OWED MEASUREMENT (does not block the fix): how long a legitimate WGC channel open takes on this guest class, from the broker's own stage timings (Jev 1.00 that this is the one fact that would sharpen it). ARMED MEANWHILE: the log sweep's broker_deaths/broker_hangs thresholds are ZERO outside a declared fault-injection context, at P1, so a recurrence cannot hide; the 2026-09-27 HEARTBEAT shape is a separate, CLOSED defect (the heartbeat was replaced by an owned mutex + per-request ack) kept as a regression tripwire. RETRACTED hours earlier the same day: I first filed these as "the broker has been dying unfiled" at P2 (wrong priority), then as "nothing to fix until the tripwire fires" (wrong: 12 of the 13 slot-ack events ARE the M8 cell's own stimulus, but this one is not, and it names a real design defect).

The serialized-service contract (Assert-NoServiceStartedByMsi) detected the breach and said so; what is missing is anything that FAILS on it. NEXT: establish whether the MSI's ServiceControl condition is ineffective for QdbDaemon or the flag is over-sensitive, then make the campaign grade the flag instead of only logging it. FIXED: the installer disarms a service's SCM recovery (recording the prior configuration) before any deliberate stop and re-arms it after; windows-utils reports a stop the SCM ASKED for as clean whatever the worker returned, while still logging the worker's outcome; and the assertion now NAMES the actor from evidence - the MSI's own StartServices outcome, the recovery state, and the SCM's 7023/7024/7031/7036 records - instead of asserting the MSI did it. The first version of the disarm threw "Item has already been added" on the second suspend of the same service and killed an install outright (found by the retest, on the guest); it is idempotent now and keeps the FIRST reading, because a second sc qfailure reads the already-disarmed state and overwriting it would re-arm "no actions". NEXT: a reinstall cell must show the flag absent, and the System log must show no 7023/7024 for QdbDaemon around the stop.

After activity: agent 1, broker 0, bridge 0. **r2-new: both Terminals repainted ~2.2 times a second for the whole minute** (135 and 136 frames, every one a real change): ours 2.16% of a core (broker 2327 switches, agent 325), DWM 3.55%, Terminal itself 2.09%, attributed 7.8%. Only that arm's agent start received dom0 focus-in events - one per window as it mapped, each logged "dom0 focus NOT applied - foreground held by" the Notepad after the AttachThreadInput retry; the other two arms received none. NOT ESTABLISHED: that the agent's focus attempts left Terminal blinking (a guest-side replay probe is next). Each Terminal frame cost ~4.5 MB of copies: Terminal reports its WHOLE surface dirty on every present, so S1b copies the card twice (the previous and the current region) - dirty regions give nothing to a swap-chain app (typing into Terminal: 6.7 MB per frame, 2.4x its card; into Notepad 408 KB, 2.7%). **Also measured:** content of all 7 windows MATCH the guest's own render (MAD 0.0-1.2; Terminals 0.2/0.3, Paint 0.0); the held menu 5 renders then silent; toasts 10/10 <= 1.7 s; proxy kill loud, back in 7 s; M8 control silent, hung broker reaped (531 ms), agent stall flagged; M4 0 composite copies, QGADESKCOPY never on; M9(b) at rest every class - Notepad, Settings, Explorer, Terminal, Paint - 1 arrival in 30 s (the probe session's first frame); M9(a) 5 ACCESS_LOST, all recreated in place, capture thread 0 switches afterwards. **M7 FAILED** (rz7 and rz8): the window beneath Calculator was recreated (pokes +3, reroutes +1); a phase probe found focus flips and a reveal poke nothing, typing into Calculator put damage 9 px PAST Calculator's edge over the Notepad (poked, recreated 2 s later), and a new window's first pixels are judged before it is tracked (occ=0); fixed in agent 02b4f72 (window events before a frame; a 24 px margin around every window above for liveness pokes - Jev 0.54/0.76, pre-commit ready 0.72), NOT YET MEASURED. Reporting defect found: the broker counts the deaf rung as a quiet reroute too, so the agent printed TWO QGAWGCRECREATE lines for one recreate. **THE TERMINAL BLINK, ESTABLISHED (wt-focus probes):** a refused SetForegroundWindow activates the window inside its own thread's queue and Windows Terminal's cursor then blinks for ever (fresh Terminals 1 -> 19 frames / 10 s; AttachThreadInput alone 1 -> 1; FLASHW_STOP and a posted deactivation do not undo it); a zero-distance mouse move first makes the call succeed and the window that loses the foreground goes quiet - fixed in agent b4d1153 (ADR-capture §21), with the M7 fix 02b4f72: NOT YET MEASURED in the agent. **FINAL BUILD 8e3023b (agent b4d1153) MEASURED (rz9, same scene):** rest arms agent 3/5/27, broker 3/2/2, bridge 2/3/2, proxy 0 switches a minute, ours 0.00/0.03/0.00 against floors 0.00, no Terminal blinked; M7 cell PASS (every window +0 during type and reveal); M8, M4, M9(a), M9(b) as rz8; toasts 10/10 <= 1.2 s. Still open: false session recreates during OTHER window operations (rz9: 21 - 12 during a four-step resize of a 3816-px Notepad, 5 in the content survey, 2 at Calculator's open, 1 at agent start, 1 in Terminal typing; none at rest, none in the menu cell), and one reroute in a reveal in the M7 phase probe (cause still open: the owner hovered over Paint on the retail guest 2026-10-01 18:19 - Paint's slot took ~1,470 pokes in ~10 s, one per dom0 motion event, stopping the moment the pointer stopped, and 0 recreates; that does NOT test the motion-poke hypothesis, because Paint shows the cursor coordinates in its status bar, so every move was a real change answered by a real frame - I first called it refuted and retracted that within minutes, the owner's catch; the valid test is a hover over a window with no hover effect, e.g. a Notepad's text area). **THAT TEST, 18:29-18:32 (the owner focused a Notepad and hovered over its text area): +2555 pokes and 3 QGAWGCRECREATE (18:30:04.885, 18:30:07.441, 18:31:14.870, "own-change poke unanswered for 2000 ms") while the damage path's pixel compare refused all 2304 pointer damages (QGAPOKESAME) - the only poke source left is the input path, which poked on every motion event unconditionally. Jev: chain established 0.64. FIX: agent 6d87b48 (repo 468a351) - motion pokes only PrintWindow-route slots (ADR-capture §23; Jev 0.94, gap acceptable 0.81); SEEN TO PASS 18:53-18:56 (the owner's re-hover on the fixed build): +8 pokes (clicks) instead of +2555, 1280 pointer damages refused, no motion-driven recreate (Jev 0.89); ONE recreate followed a click that changed nothing - keys/buttons still poke every slot - accepted as a bounded cost (one invisible session reopen, zero at rest; Jev 0.70), ADR-capture §23.** **TYPING LATENCY (M5): THERE IS NO REGRESSION - S1b IS THE FASTEST PATH (measured 18:00, end of this passage). RETRACTED 2026-10-01 ~15:10 (Jev: regression established 0.17; the better reading is an INSTRUMENT BLIND SPOT, 0.65 vs a real one-key-late delivery 0.20). The numbers are as measured; what they mean is not. See the correction at the end of this passage.** On the asserted scene, 3 interleaved rounds each: the final build key -> damage p90 **191 / 185 / 188 ms** against **22 / 25 / 17 ms** for rz4-ci (repo 1e4d02b, agent f52a4ba) - disjoint; covered-window latency (M6) equal. Pattern: the typed window's damage comes in pairs ~4 ms apart every ~190 ms, slower than the keys (~160 ms), so half the keys wait ~180 ms. The fault-injection agent of the same commit (50 ms delay before every damage message) read a steady ~51 ms with the same broker - so the broker is not the cause: the agent is. **BISECTED (same asserted scene, 3 interleaved rounds per step):** rz9 (agent 3f97adf) p90 206/201/198 vs rz8 (agent ad3e30b) 202/200/199 - both slow; rz8 191/203/.. vs **rz7 (repo cf46ea0, agent 61cf1c5) 41/31/..** - the regression came with rz8, whose only behavioural change is the broker's S1b dirty regions (1e6cc8c: direct sessions in DirtyRegionMode ReportOnly, region reads into a persistent copy, region-limited identical skip); the agent change in that step is ABI-22 counter fields only. Windows' own delivery in that mode looks per-change (a dirty-mode probe session saw ~1 arrival per key plus caret blinks), so the broker's region path is the first suspect; a same-binary A/B (broker test switch QubesWgcDirtyMode, build 8def532) is next. The fault-injection arm's steady ~51 ms (same broker) had misled me into clearing the broker - retracted. **CORRECTION (2026-10-01 ~15:10): the instrument, not the build, is the first suspect.** M5 stamps each key AFTER SendKeys.SendWait returns and charges the first damage after that stamp; there is no stamp before SendWait, so a damage that lands between a key's injection and SendWait's return is charged to the PREVIOUS key, at about one key period (~190 ms). rz8 does less work per frame than rz7 (regions instead of a whole 15 MB copy on a software D3D device), so it can publish before SendWait returns. The data fit that: within one rz8 arm keys flip between 0-16 ms and ~180-195 ms (r3 p50 16, p90 192), one text-row damage per key in both builds; and the fault-injection agent (+50 ms before every damage, same broker) read a steady ~51 ms - impossible if the change really arrived a key late (a delay can only add), exactly what a delivery landing right around SendWait's return looks like once delayed past it. So the retraction of that reading above was itself wrong. The instrument's one validation (+50 ms read +53..+64) only exercised deliveries that already landed after the stamp. Decisive measurement (Jev 0.64): keys ~1 s apart, latency from a stamp taken BEFORE SendWait (scratchpad/design-wf/drive2.ps1, m5diag.sh), with the broker's per-arrival trace (frame age, published or skipped) on the test build s4-m5diag 8695277. **MEASURED AND SETTLED (2026-10-01 17:43-18:00, w11r-ds = retail Win11 26300, m5diag.sh, 9 arms on one boot, every arm's broker start line naming its switches):** latency = key INJECTION -> the agent's first damage, keys 1 s apart. **Shipped S1b (dirty regions read): p50 27-35 ms (4 arms; two were the drain switch, which drained nothing and so ran the same path), broker 3-5 ms; dirty mode off (pre-S1b whole reads): 43-59 ms, broker 18-22 ms; dirty mode on with regions ignored: 42-46 ms, broker 15-20 ms.** ~30 ms of it is Windows (key -> the composed frame); every frame arrived fresh, no frame was ever queued behind another (the one-key-late mechanisms do not occur). - P3 [reprioritized 2026-10-02: was P1; Jev not-open 0.34 - likely resolved by a newer entry or the current build; verify, then move to issues-closed.md] [capture, product-relevant] **THE RELAY WORKS AND IS THROWN AWAY: THE QUIET RULE DEMOTES HEALTHY RELAYS ON STATIC WINDOWS.** Measured 2026-09-26 on win11de-led2 (German Win11 25H2 26200.8037, our build 4.3.33+agent.34cbc2770189, installed wgcbroker.exe hash checked against the release disc before the census, `relayCapable=1 relayOsBuild=26200`). THREE censuses, all agreeing, no flapping: **all four rogue classes sat on ROUTE 2 (the polled PrintWindow fallback)** - `CASCADIA_HOSTING_WINDOW_CLASS`, `QubesChromeReproKey` (LWA_COLORKEY), `QubesChromeReproNrb` (WS_EX_NOREDIRECTIONBITMAP), `QubesChromeReproUlw` (ULW-layered). Only the toast (`Windows.UI.Core.CoreWindow`) was arrival-driven, on ROUTE 0. **BUT THE RELAY HAD WORKED ON EVERY ONE OF THEM**: each polled slot reads `relayOk=1 relayFail=0` with frames actually delivered - `arrived=56/28/6/3, published=56/28/5/3` - and then `quietReroutes=2`, `relayDest=0x0`, `pw=1`, thousands of polls since. So the ladder WGC -> RELAY -> PrintWindow ran to its last rung on windows the relay was serving correctly. **CAUSE (Jev `root_cause=quiet-rule-demotes-healthy-static-relays` 1.00):** the demotion test is "a poke arrived since our last frame, and no frame followed". The agent derives damage from **desktop dirty rects**, so a poke fires whenever anything repaints inside a window's screen RECTANGLE - a passing cursor, an overlapping window, a neighbour - whether or not that window's own content moved. A HEALTHY relay on a STATIC window therefore satisfies the test continuously: slot0 shows **`PokeSeq=11772` against `FramesArrived=56`**. DWM composes nothing new because nothing changed, which is correct behaviour being read as a dead feed. **FIX (Jev `require-evidence-the-source-changed` 0.76, ahead of demote-only-on-error 0.17 and never-demote-after-delivering 0.02):** before demoting a relay that has delivered, render the SOURCE once and compare a strided FNV-1a hash with the previous one; unchanged means the relay is correctly delivering nothing and is left alone, changed means damage really did occur with no frame behind it - the founding symptom - and the demotion proceeds. ABI **10** adds `RelayStaticHolds` so the rule is OBSERVABLE: if a live relay slot never advances it, the rule is not firing and any pass is unproven. **TWO DEFECTS CAUGHT IN THE FIX ITSELF BEFORE IT SHIPPED, both cost-or-evidence, neither found by a test:** (a) the demotion question is evaluated on EVERY loop tick once a channel is quiet, so the first cut's fixed 250 ms throttle meant a PrintWindow 4x/s per static window - at the measured p50 31-49 ms on the target's own UI thread, four windows would be ~2/3 of a core, worse than the fallback being removed; the interval is now adaptive, 500 ms doubling to an 8 s ceiling, reset on change. (b) the test returned a bool, so THROTTLED was indistinguishable from MEASURED-SAME and `RelayStaticHolds` would have counted a hold every tick, inflating the evidence counter by orders of magnitude; it is now tri-state (SRC_CHANGED/SRC_SAME/SRC_UNMEASURED). **CONTROL REQUIRED BEFORE BELIEVING THE FIX (Jev `needs_a_control` 0.74):** `QubesWgcRelayNoDemote=1` (HKLM\SOFTWARE\Qubes\GuiAgent, latched at broker start, not a product setting) disables relay demotion entirely, so the census can show the relay WOULD have kept delivering these classes. **ACCEPTANCE BAR, set by Jev BEFORE any result existed:** all four classes on route 0/1 AND per-class pixels matching (`zero-slots-on-route-2-is-enough` scored **0.00**), and REPEATED - `single_run_enough` **0.14**. Commits `7767f26`, `5284bd5`, `db4934c`; submodule `c9ca8a6`. [verified 2026-09-26] **THE CONTROL PASSED - THE RELAY CAN SERVE EVERY ROGUE CLASS (2026-09-26, win11de-led4, ABI 11 build).** Both sides ran with the broker restart ASSERTED BY A PID CHANGE, which matters because the FIRST attempt's control was VOID: restarting the QubesGuiWatchdog service left the running gui-agent/wgcbroker alive (agentPid=4492 brokerPid=5560 in BOTH censuses), so the knob never latched and the "control" silently duplicated the fix. The harness now kills both processes and refuses unless the broker pid moves. **CONTROL (QubesWgcRelayNoDemote=1, demotion disabled): ROUTE VERDICT PASS** - all five mapped slots held arrival-driven RELAY routes across three censuses with ZERO on the polled fallback: `CASCADIA_HOSTING_WINDOW_CLASS` (Terminal, NRB), `QubesChromeReproUlw`, `QubesChromeReproNrb`, `QubesChromeReproKey` (LWA_COLORKEY) and `Windows.UI.Core.CoreWindow` (toast). `RelayStaticHolds` was 0 on every slot, the internal cross-check that the knob really latched (with demotion off the source test is never consulted). Jev `control_pass_is_meaningful` **0.90**. **FIX (rule alone): FAIL on exactly one class**, and the ABI 11 counters named it rather than leaving it to inference - `srcChanged=1 srcUnmeasured=2 pwFail=0`. **PrintWindow SUCCEEDED**, refuting my own hypothesis that the test might be blind on layered windows; a SINGLE differing render demoted the slot, permanently, because demotion is one-way (`g_noRelay` vetoes re-relaying). The founding symptom this detector exists for was FramesArrived frozen at 3 while a control ran 637->678 over 23 s - sustained damage with NO frames - and one sample is not that. Demotion now requires an unbroken run of THREE measured changes with no frame in between, reset by any arriving frame and by any measured SAME (`65df81e`). Jev `require-repeated-changes-with-no-frames` **0.93**. **TWO FURTHER INSTRUMENT DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED, both of which would have produced a wrong verdict:** (a) the pixel survey's own filters (>=200x150, titled, not cloaked) silently dropped FIVE slots per census, so the pixel half could not be graded at all - the census now forces measurement by hwnd and reports `TRUTHMISSING` for anything unreachable (`762259f`); (b) the census counted a slot moving WGC->RELAY as UNSTABLE and failed the run for it, although that is the routing ladder promoting a channel between two arrival-driven routes - only a change involving route 2 is instability now (Jev `census_too_strict_on_ladder` 0.73). **AND THE ULW CELL WAS MEASURING A FIXTURE THAT CANNOT EXHIBIT THE BEHAVIOUR:** `chromerepro`'s ULW window is `WS_EX_LAYERED` with NEITHER `SetLayeredWindowAttributes` NOR `UpdateLayeredWindow`, by design, to exercise the ROUTER's classification - so it has no application-supplied layered surface and its render may legitimately vary. Jev `fixture_is_adequate` **0.10**, `result_is_fixture_artefact` 0.62, `what_to_do=add-a-real-ulw-fixture` 0.82. A content-bearing fixture (premultiplied BGRA, alternating alpha 255/128 bands, outcome reported in the window title because the app is /SUBSYSTEM:WINDOWS) is built and staged (`9b419db`); both windows are kept, one classifies and one carries pixels. [verified 2026-09-26] **FINAL 2026-09-26: DELIVERED PIXELS VERIFIED, ONE RESIDUE OPEN. Jev `what_to_report` = `complete-but-one-residue-open` **0.74** with `not-yet-accepted` at **0.01**, `bar_met` 0.66, `pixels_verified` 0.79 - report it exactly that way, neither upgraded to "accepted" nor downgraded to "not done".** The residual that held the previous refusal was `signature-not-pixel-identity` (0.57): a distinct-colour count cannot tell a correct frame from the same palette arranged wrongly, a shifted image, a blank region or a channel swap. **ABI 13** answers it - the broker publishes a **32x32 grid of per-tile mean RGB for the frame it ACTUALLY delivered**, the guest computes the same reduction of its own composited render, and the two are compared over the central 24x24 (576 tiles) as a mean absolute difference. Central only, symmetrically, because the guest measures the whole window while the broker publishes the content it was handed - scoring the caption as infidelity would be wrong. An exact hash is impossible here: the two sides differ by the window frame (360x240 against 348x234) plus a crop the guest cannot see, so normalising both to one grid is what makes them comparable at all. Jev chose this instrument over a 4x4/8x8 grid, a sorted-multiset hash and an exact hash at confidence **1.00**; alpha is excluded by design (0.95) since the protocol carries none. **RESULT, graded census (rule active, knob off), win11de-led8, ABI 13 build `44092a8`, deploy 9 passed/0 failed, installed hashes verified before every census, broker restart asserted by pid change:** `CASCADIA_HOSTING_WINDOW_CLASS` MAD **0.0**, `QubesChromeReproNrb` MAD **0.0**, `QubesChromeReproUlwContent` (per-pixel alpha, `ovr=1`) MAD **1.5**, `QubesChromeReproKey` MAD **0.0** - out of 255, over 576 tiles. The control census gave the same four numbers. **THE LIMIT WAS SET BY DRIVING THE CHECK, NOT BY TASTE:** at 24 a synthetic six-tile shift scored 20.4 and PASSED, so it is 12 - where identical=0.0 and a one-tile offset=2.7 pass (that is the scale of the real frame difference) while a six-tile shift=20.4, a channel swap=44.4 and a blank delivered frame=104.0 all fail. **LIVENESS PROVEN SEPARATELY**, because no static comparison can detect a stale frame (Jev `stale_frame_needs_more` **0.83**): `chromerepro` can now advance the per-pixel-alpha window's content on command via a sentinel file it consumes on a 1 Hz timer; the harness WAITS for that consumption (measured ~3 s) and REFUSES the cell outright if it never comes, so a verdict can never be about a change that did not happen. After the advance the delivered frame MOVED (`delivered-vs-before` **20.6**) and MATCHED the source's new render (`delivered-vs-source` **1.4**). **AN EARLIER STALENESS FAIL WAS MINE AND IS WITHDRAWN:** with a fixed 8 s sleep the same guest produced a PASS on one census and a FAIL (`delivered-vs-before 0.0`) on another - a fixed sleep standing in for an observable condition, which is a named defect in this project's rules. **THE NO-DATA ROWS ARE EXPLAINED BY MEASUREMENT (Jev `explanations_sound` 0.85), not waved away:** `Ghost` is the fixture's DELIBERATELY alpha-0 window - its row reads `ownColours=-1 fullColours=-1`, i.e. PrintWindow correctly failed on a window that exists to be invisible; the second Terminal window was `TRUTHMISSING reason=not-enumerated` because it had been CLOSED between the route read and the survey (the direct-measurement pass is gated on `IsWindow`). Neither is a rogue class and neither is a relay failure. **THE ONE OPEN RESIDUE:** transient surfaces are not gradeable by this instrument - a toast's own colour count moved 28 -> 36 between censuses, so guest and broker necessarily sample different instants; it scored MAD 13.7 against a limit of 12 on the control. Its frames ARE delivered (arrival-driven route, broker publishes for it). Also unmeasured this run: per-pixel alpha fidelity (Terminal `alphaMin=102 partial=193/8680`, COLORKEY `partial=64/1014`), previously rated non-blocking 0.38. Release note: `docs/RELEASE-NOTE-deslice-relay.md` - build floor FIRST, every class with its verdict, residues named. Commits `44092a8`, `5c32fd6`; submodule ABI 13. [verified 2026-09-26] **THE DE-SLICER IS COMPLETE ON ROUTES AND VERIFIED ON DELIVERED PIXELS FOR ALL FOUR CLASSES - BUT JEV DOES NOT CALL IT ACCEPTED (`bar_met` 0.50). Reported as `routes-complete-pixels-partial` (0.64), which is what this entry says.** Final state 2026-09-26 on win11de-led7 (German Win11 25H2 26200.8037, ABI 12 build `dafe674`, deploy 9 passed/0 failed, installed gui-agent hash == package, installed wgcbroker hash re-verified against the verified disc before EVERY census). **ROUTES: PASSED FIVE TIMES**, three censuses each - one control (demotion disabled), three graded, and one after a **COLD BOOT** with the knob step and agent restart deliberately skipped so the graded agent is the instance the boot produced. Every mapped slot arrival-driven in every census; **slots on the polled fallback: ZERO, every time**. `RelayStaticHolds` climbs on every relay slot on the graded runs (13/20/28, 12/20/27, 11/19/27, 10/18/25, 9/16/22) and is 0 under the control by construction - which is what shows the source-change RULE held them rather than the knob. **DELIVERED PIXELS, the gap Jev rated 1.00** (`relayed-pixels-never-read`): ABI 12 has the broker count distinct colours in the frame it ACTUALLY PUBLISHED, sampled every 9th pixel - the identical sampling the guest uses on its own composited render - so the numbers compare directly per slot with the slot's own Hwnd giving an exact mapping. On the cold boot: Terminal/NRB `14 -> 13` MATCH, `QubesChromeReproNrb` `2 -> 2` MATCH, `QubesChromeReproUlwContent` **`1080 -> 1080` EXACT**, `QubesChromeReproKey` `16 -> 16` MATCH. **OVERRIDE-REDIRECT IS MEASURED, NOT INFERRED**: the agent's own protocol trace (`QGAPROTO,msg=CREATE,...,ovr=`, ProtoTrace via the agent config key) shows exactly TWO windows sent override-redirect - `QubesChromeReproUlwContent` (0x14028a) and the toast CoreWindow (0x600c0) - against eight at ovr=0; UlwContent is the window that matched exactly. Fixed and driven with a synthetic `-1` row to confirm it now FAILS. **INSTRUMENT DEFECTS FIXED THIS ROUND, ALL MINE:** the relay-pixel matcher counted every pixel of a dom0 PNG against the guest's SAMPLED count (11/13 windows falsely UNMATCHED) and then demanded equal dimensions although dom0 captures content while the guest measures the window rect (a 12x6 frame, which rejected an exact 16=16); `local.WinWindowShot` is policy-refused here so no manifest maps a dom0 PNG to an hwnd, which is why the broker-side signature was built instead (Jev preferred it 0.63 over photographing the whole desktop 0.22); forcing a window into the survey bypassed its filters but not the enumerator, so a toast could never be measured (`7648b33`); the per-census class map was read once before the per-census toasts existed, leaving them unnamed; and fixture instances accumulated across runs, adding unnamed duplicate slots whose delivered counts mirrored the real ones. [verified 2026-09-26]
- P3 [reprioritized 2026-10-02: was P1; Jev not-open 0.22 - likely resolved by a newer entry or the current build; verify, then move to issues-closed.md] [capture, product-relevant] **THE BROKER'S DAMAGE POKE IS DEAD CODE FOR EVERY WINDOW THE BROKER SERVES - broker-routed windows render on a timer, never on damage.** Source-verified end to end 2026-09-26, and it explains a measured symptom. `BrokerPokeDamage(entry)` is called in exactly one place, `main.c:9755`, inside the legacy dirty-rect damage loop. That loop sits BELOW `continue;` at `main.c:9580`, which closes the block opened at `main.c:8783` by `if (PwIsAttached(entry))` (brace-matched, not eyeballed). `PwIsAttached` returns `entry->PwDumpSent` (`perwindow.c:171`), and the per-window attach sets `PwDumpSent = TRUE` and `PwSliceFed = sliceFed` on consecutive lines (`perwindow.c:675-676`). Since `BrokerRegister` requires `PwSliceFed` (`main.c:3607`), EVERY window the broker serves is attached by construction, takes that `continue`, and can never reach the poke. **MEASURED CONSEQUENCE** on win11de-ctl, ABI-7 counters via `guest/wgcbroker-peek.ps1`: slot0 `PokeSeq=0 PokeAck=0`, `PollsServiced == SafetyPolls == Polls` (every render that slot ever had came from the 1 s safety backstop, none from damage), `BackoffMs=8000`, polls advancing by one per six seconds - a window repainting once per 6-8 s, which the owner saw and reported as "slow as fuck". `e8ebc47` clamps the backoff for poke-less slots, which stops the 8 s case but leaves these windows on a 1 Hz timer: a mitigation, not the fix. **THE FIX** is to give broker-sourced windows a damage signal - the poke must be reachable from the attached-window path, or damage must be derived per-window rather than from desktop dirty rects. **NOTE** the broker's own ABI-7 rationale comment states the agent "already computes per-window damage ... bumps PokeSeq"; that describes a path which does not exist for broker slots, and should be corrected when someone next touches it. Found by delegated source reading, then verified here by brace-matching rather than taken on trust. [verified 2026-09-26]
